# tt-metal — bug-audit knowledge pack

Mined 2026-09-26 from tt-metal's closed issues, PRs and first-parent history: 9,972 fix cases triaged (4,333 code bugs, 884 test bugs), 1,311 deep-read with fix-completeness judged against later history and the current tree. The recall-benchmark holdout (tt-metal-holdout.jsonl) was excluded from every step. Review-thread mining for this repo is pending; see SKILL.md. Class weights are approximate: a post-mortem of benchmark picks found about 12% of triage "code bugs" were not defects (cleanups, lint fixes, feature enablement), so read the table as an ordering, not exact shares.

Full per-class material for `tt-metal.md`. **Candidate sites** are places where earlier analysis suspected an unfixed copy of a fixed bug. They are leads to verify, never findings to report as-is. Line numbers drift, so re-find each site by its code before trusting it.


## `contract-mismatch`: Producer and consumer re-derive one fact differently

Two sides must agree on one fact: a credit or page count, a size, an address or stride, a layout, a register or valid choice, a protocol flag, an encoding, a shape rank. Instead of reading it from one shared source, each side works it out on its own, and the two drift apart. Four shapes keep coming back. (1) Parallel recomputation. Host and device, calculator and emitter, reader/compute/writer, prepare and consume, or the two ends of a link each compute the value from a formula, a literal or local context: a hard-coded 32/8/4, sizeof(A)+sizeof(B), a subset versus a superset of devices, a padded versus a logical extent, a local direction label. It agrees under one config and breaks when an arch, topology, CQ count, dtype, page size or shard layout changes. (2) Lossy rebuild of a value object. A spec, MemoryConfig or topology is rebuilt from selected fields or a default ({}). Sharding, per-core allocation, alignment or dim metadata is silently dropped, or goes stale after a rank change. (3) An implicit precondition held only by convention: one command per fetch entry, a scatter chunk count of at least 2, a single-packet size within the max burst, subblock height of at most 2, a row count of at least 1024, bias logical height of 1, a buffer that is 'empty means all'. The callee enforces it with a debug-only assert, or with nothing, so Release builds and new callers violate it silently. (4) A hand-maintained mirror of an authoritative table goes stale: bitfield structs over generated registers, ISA encoding enums, counter-name tables, header/value lists, per-mesh config tables, or a default-branch fallback in a format switch. Renames and extensions then miss one copy.

**Checks**

- For every quantity used on both sides of a handshake (initial credits vs. returned or max credits, expected completions vs. faked acks, semaphore or CB page counts, slot strides), trace each side back to its host source. Flag it unless both read the same config object or function over the same set of devices or cores, or an assert ties them together. Then evaluate each formula under every topology, CQ count, arch and subset/superset device set.
- Wherever a size is computed in one pass and consumed or emitted in another (calculator vs. populator, hand-summed sizeof headers, size field vs. laid-out bytes, copy length vs. advance length), line the two up per branch, including pinned/inline/large-page branches and alignment calls. Check that copy lengths come from the SOURCE extent, never the padded destination. Check that the equality check is TT_FATAL, not a Release-stripped TT_ASSERT.
- For every CB/DFB entry, stride or alignment a factory computes for data moved by the NoC, check that it comes from the actual buffer's alignment for its memory type (buffer->alignment(), aligned_page_size, HAL), not a literal 32 or max(L1,DRAM). Check that the alignment is taken from the buffer that is the NoC source for reads or the destination for writes. Re-evaluate for small row-major pages on the arch with the largest DRAM alignment (64B).
- For every function that returns a transformed spec, layout, MemoryConfig or topology, diff the output fields against the input. Flag MemoryConfig{}, narrower constructors (layout+buffer_type+shard_spec only) and verbatim-copied placements across rank-changing ops (view/reshape/permute). Check that equality, hash and reflection cover every flag, so that a dropped field is visible.
- For every per-link or cross-thread protocol option (first-level ACK or deadlock avoidance, unpack-to-dest participation, credit mode), check that both ends compute it from an input both agree on (the link, a generated global constant), not an endpoint-local label, local format or local trim profile. List every enum value (including Z/NONE/pass-through) and confirm none of them reaches a default branch by accident.
- When a device-side kernel has a fixed capacity or precondition (max sub-cmds, min scatter chunks, max burst size, subblock height, minimum row length, one command per unit), find every host or kernel producer. Confirm that each one reads the bound from a shared definition and clamps to it, and that any setup or priming call is guarded by the same predicate as the use (for example use_scatter_write). Evaluate derived counts at the smallest and largest page size and dtype.
- For host routing predicates that select a specialised kernel (row-broadcast bias, fused path, sharded vs. interleaved route), check that the predicate tests the LOGICAL extent or layout the kernel's semantics depend on. Check that every route computes the same function and output spec (rank, broadcasting, fused-column order).
- For every hand-written mirror of an authoritative definition (register bitfield structs, ISA mod/format enums, counter-name tables, header vs. value lists, per-arch pad sizes, LOG2 vs. linear alignment constants, format-to-size switches with a default), diff it against the source per arch. Flag default branches that return one specific format's value instead of asserting, and consumers that look up retired names with .get(name, 0).
- For lowered ops (conv_transpose -> conv2d micro-op, grouped -> dense weights), check that every call into the micro-op and its weight/bias/config/slice/perf helpers gets the TRANSFORMED parameters (groups, stride, padding, output_padding). Check that layout knobs (blocking, C_in_block) have the same default and sentinel meaning on every surface: C++, binding, config struct, fallback.
- For per-location allocations (per-core shard bases, per-device ids), grep every consumer that does address()+offset or device()->id() on a single scalar accessor: host read/write, CB binding, cache-hit runtime-arg patching, sanitizer registration.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- MemoryConfig{} or MemoryConfig(x.memory_layout(), x.buffer_type(), x.shard_spec()) inside compute_output_specs / host tensor ops (tt_metal/impl/tensor/*, ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/{tilize,untilize*,fold}/device)
- round_up_to_mul32\( or align\(.*, *32\) sizing CB/DFB page_size for row-major DRAM data in ttnn/**/program_factory.cpp (embedding, moreh_getitem, argmax)
- fabric_*scatter_write_set_state( whose chunk count is a *_per_packet value not inside `if constexpr (use_scatter_write / >= 2)` in ttnn/**/ccl/**/kernels and sdpa ring writers
- to_noc_unicast_write(NocUnicastCommandHeader{get_noc_addr(page, accessor)}) in kernels created with WriterDataMovementConfig (NOC1). On WH DRAM the fabric header flips an already-NOC1 coordinate a second time (ttnn/**/ccl/**/kernels)
- x.get("value_<NAME>", 0) / safe_ratio("value_...") keys in tools/tracy/perf_counter_analysis.py that match no name in */hw_counters.h
- llk_math_eltwise_unary_datacopy(_init)?<DataCopyType::B2D paired with llk_unpack_A<bcast_type> where bcast_type may be NONE (tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute/bcast.h, ARCH_QUASAR branches)
- static_cast<int>(Converter::as_float(threshold)) or (float)uint_param in SFPU int paths; std::to_string((uint32_t)param) in unary_op_utils host codegen (tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/sfpu, ttnn unary)
- DataflowBuffer(x).reserve_back(N) immediately followed by push_back, or wait_front followed by pop_front, with no pack/unpack between, in Quasar/tt-2xx compute kernels
- sizeof(CQPrefetchCmd) + sizeof(CQDispatchCmd) hand-summed outside device_command*.cpp; TT_ASSERT on 'Command sequence size mismatch' / num_sub_cmds <= max in tt_metal/impl/{buffers,trace,dispatch}
- (shard->dim % rank) or get_normalized_index(shard->dim) on an unvalidated placement dim; tensor_topology() passed unchanged onto a spec of different rank (ttnn/core/tensor, ttnn/**/ccl)

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernel_config + kernels/cq_prefetch/cq_dispatch (credit counts, command framing, sizes); tt_metal/impl/buffers/dispatch.cpp and tt_metal/impl/tensor (calculator vs. emitter, copy sizes, per-core addresses, spec rebuilds); tt_metal/fabric builders and ttnn/**/ccl kernels (per-endpoint link protocol, NoC coordinate systems, scatter-write preconditions); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv (conv_transpose2d lowering, weight/bias prep, sliding_window config); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul and transformer/sdpa (bias routing, subblock constraints, fused vs. unfused output spec); ttnn data_movement / reduction ops (tilize/untilize/copy/sort/argmax output specs, alignment literals, MemoryConfig rebuilds); tt_metal/hw/inc (compute API bcast, dev_msgs shared structs, addrgen format switch) and tt-llk arch-specific copies (Quasar branches lag WH/BH fixes); tools/tracy and tt-llk python perf helpers (name tables, parallel header/value lists)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- A fix often corrects one side or one path and leaves the others. Examples: host-side semaphore stride fixed months before the device get_semaphore; multicast FabricWriter guarded but the unicast writer and matmul/strided siblings not; WH/BH bcast fixed while the Quasar branch still hard-codes B2D; the older graph-trace consumer never learned the alias flag. After any fix, grep every twin (arch fork, Quasar copy, unicast/multicast, old/new consumer).
- Fixes that avoid the trigger instead of unifying the source regress later. Examples: steering inter-mesh cables away from DA directions while DA is still derived per endpoint; bypassing the ops that drop the per-core flag instead of making them preserve it; model-side round-trip workarounds for slice/CCL corruption. The mismatch is still there for the next caller.
- Guard removals and migrations bring back latent mismatches. A default-true bypass flag hid bad slice tables until a refactor removed it. The create_descriptor migration dropped the bias broadcast TT_FATAL. A wide-schema refactor dropped the len(names)==len(values) assert. When you touch a validator, list what callers relied on its old leniency.
- Preserving fields fully can break callers that depended on the lossy behaviour. The MemoryConfig preservation in pad/unpad/to_row_major was partly reverted when models broke, which left an asymmetric pair (to_tile preserves, to_row_major drops). Land the preservation together with the caller updates, not one without the other.
- Two concurrent fixes of the same drift, made from opposite ends in different files, merged cleanly into a new inconsistency. Re-check paired structures on post-merge main, not on the PR base.
- Fixes that change a dtype or default without following it end to end stayed partial. Sort indices were widened to UINT32 on the host while the device index generator still truncated to 16 bits. conv3d defaulting to a full block overflowed L1 until the sentinel meaning was unified on both sides.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tools/tracy/perf_counter_analysis.py:1431 — Packer Efficiency still reads retired value_PACKER_DEST_READ_AVAILABLE (renamed PACKER0_DEST_READ_REQ in hw_counters.h); .get(...,0) makes it silently 0. Same retired names at :1436, :1462, :1713-1729, :1746
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute/bcast.h:76 — Quasar unary_bcast_init hard-codes DataCopyType::B2D for every bcast_type, including NONE, where unpack fills SrcA. This is the math/unpack valid mismatch fixed for WH/BH; the exec path at :127 has the same problem
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/all_gather/device/kernels/unicast_common.hpp:121 — the constructor primes scatter-write state with pages_per_packet unconditionally, although use_scatter_write (:233) requires >= min chunks. This is the defect fixed only in the multicast writer
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/all_gather_minimal_matmul_async/device/kernels/matmul_dataflow_common.hpp:1010 — scatter set_state primed with a host min(max_addrs, pages_per_packet) that is 1 for fp32 tiles, with no >= 2 guard
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/neighbor_pad_halo/device/kernels/np_writer.cpp:307 — get_noc_addr(page, dst_accessor) from a NOC1 writer is passed raw into to_noc_unicast_write, whose contract is NOC0 coordinates. The coordinate is double-flipped for WH DRAM destinations
- ttnn/core/graph/graph_trace_utils.cpp:85 — extract_peak_L1_memory_usage adds every kNodeCBAllocate size without checking kGloballyAllocated, so tensor-aliased CBs are double counted (the DFB branch right below does check borrows_memory)
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_relu.h:95 — _relu_max_ vInt/uint32 decodes the threshold as static_cast<int>(as_float(bits)), so small integer thresholds become 0. The sibling _relu_min_ uses the integer directly. The BH copy at tt_llk_blackhole/.../ckernel_sfpu_relu.h:92 has the same issue
- tt_metal/impl/tensor/tensor_apis.cpp:277 — to_row_major host conversion builds its output spec with MemoryConfig{} (sharding and placement dropped) while to_tile preserves them; pad/unpad in host_pad_apis.cpp:305/:391 drop them the same way
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transformer/sdpa/device/kernels/compute/compute_streaming.hpp:2438 — dfb_signal reserve_back(1) immediately followed by push_back with no packer op between. That breaks the Tensix-side DFB rule, and the guard is compiled out in Release
- models/tt_dit/layers/conv2d.py:89 — the (1,8) mesh slice table asks for 8 width slices of a 128-wide TILE output, but the op allows at most ceil(128/32)=4, so it hits the op TT_FATAL when that shape is reached
- tt_metal/impl/buffers/circular_buffer_config.cpp:223 — set_globally_allocated_address_and_total_size stores buffer.address() (the first core's shard base) for all cores. The same applies to the cache-hit runtime-arg patch at tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptor_patching.cpp:262, which is wrong for per-core-allocated buffers
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/sort/sort.cpp:258 — preallocated-output validation accepts UINT16 indices for BF16 input whatever the sort-dim length, so indices wrap silently past 65535 while the default-spec path switches to UINT32


## `missing-case`: Unhandled enum, dtype, shape and topology cases

A dispatch (an if/else-if chain, an if-constexpr ladder, a ternary, a boolean routing predicate) lists the cases its author had in mind and ends in a silent default: the Float16_b/bf16 kernel flavour, the signed Int32 branch, an empty fall-through that leaves Dest unchanged, a metadata-only view, the composite fallback. A new or rare value then takes a path with the wrong semantics and produces no error. Typical cases are UINT32/UINT16/UINT8, a second ERISC, a new storage variant, HYBRID allocator mode, a service core, or an idle ethernet core. The degenerate geometries get the same treatment: an axis extent of 1 or 2 (ring or torus where forward == backward), an empty list, the only node in a list, a sentinel -1 link, zero volume, rank 0/1, index 0 in `x-1`, a shift count of 32 or more, and domain edges such as negative input, NaN, Inf or -0. The same decision is usually duplicated across per-arch copies, single- and multi-device loaders, unary/binary/ternary entry points, sibling validators, Quasar forks and host/kernel pairs. Fixes land in one copy and the others drift. The worst variants degrade silently: a zero ownership mask no-ops synchronisation, a hardcoded define ignores an optional input, and a golden written to mirror the kernel hides the bug from tests.

**Checks**

- For every dispatch that maps an enum or DataType to a kernel template argument, a file name or a code path, list every value the upstream API or the kernel's static_assert admits, and confirm each one has an explicit arm. The chain should end in static_assert, TT_THROW or an exhaustive switch with no default, never a silent default to a bf16/Float16_b/Int32 flavour or an empty else. Pay special attention to UINT8/UINT16/UINT32 reaching a signed or 16-bit branch.
- When the same decision exists in more than one place, put the copies side by side and diff the handled set. Places to compare: WH/BH/Quasar copies, legacy tt-llk vs metal llk_api, unary/binary_ng/ternary entry points of one op, single- vs multi-device loaders, dump vs load, routing predicate vs op-config switch vs init selector, a validator whose comment says 'same as X', and experimental/quasar forks. Any value one copy handles and another does not is a finding.
- For wraparound or neighbour arithmetic ((x+1)%n, (x-1+n)%n, Ring/Torus topology), work through n==1 (self-neighbour) and n==2 (forward == backward peer). Check whether results go into a destination-keyed map where one direction overwrites the other. If an entry point demotes Ring to Linear, confirm the validators and program factory downstream accept the demoted value and do not TT_FATAL on the original.
- For linked or index-based containers, check every unlink, shrink or reset path with a removed node that is the only node, the head or the tail, and with a link equal to the sentinel (-1/SIZE_MAX/nullptr) that is then used as an index. Both neighbours' links must be repaired. The guard must also hold in release builds, not only as a TT_ASSERT.
- For shape code, try rank 0/1/2, zero volume, unequal ranks and non-tile-aligned dims. Look for hardcoded shape[2]/shape[3], negative indexing that starts at -0, alignment built from an already-aligned shape, a zero page size or divisor, and zero-copy view shortcuts taken by an early return on storage type before the layout checks (TILE with a changed padded [-2:]).
- For SFPU math, enumerate the domain edges (negative, +-0, subnormal, +-Inf, NaN) for every transcendental function, in every arch copy and approximation mode, and in legacy helper bodies that other ops reuse. For integer-from-float conversions that shift by (exp-23), check that the exponent is clamped on BOTH sides, because the shift instruction uses only the low 5 bits of the amount.
- When a resource can be carved by several allocators, owners or core kinds (global plus sub-device allocators, per-core HYBRID banks, service/dispatch/storage/idle-ethernet cores, multiple allocator IDs per bank), confirm every occupancy query, exclusion list, validation and mutation covers all of them on every branch: empty id list, explicit list, each mode flag, and the default id 0.
- For a fused or specialised fast path chosen by boolean predicates (depthwise 1D/2D, fused bias, composite vs native CCL, falcon backend), enumerate the input combinations the predicates cover: groups, kernel h/w, bias, dtype, mesh shape, optional tensors. Confirm every combination lands in a path that supports it, and that the general fallback checks on the host every limit its kernels only static_assert.
- For each optional input or host-only state, follow it into every program-factory or kernel variant and check it is consumed under the same presence test (no pinned define with a TODO beside it). Check that every attribute that changes codegen or layout is in the program hash or cache key. Check that tests use payloads that differ from the default, and goldens that are independent of the kernel.
- For autograd ops and Python bindings, check every overload: the backward of a broadcasting op must reduce the gradient over the expanded dims, including implicit leading dims when ranks differ. A bound value type with a C++ operator== must expose __eq__ (and __hash__ where needed).

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `else { defines[...] = "...<DataFormat::Float16_b>"; }` or `: "Float16_b"` / `: "Int32"` as the tail of a dtype chain: ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/{unary,binary_ng,ternary}/**/*_utils.cpp and the experimental/quasar copies
- `else if constexpr (operation == SfpuType::` chains with no trailing `else { static_assert(`: tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/sfpu and tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu
- `if constexpr (FORMAT == DataFormat::UInt16) {...} else {` with signed sign-fill or compare semantics in the else branch: SFPU shift/compare/typecast kernels
- TTI_SFPEXEXP ... TTI_SFPIADD(-23 ...) ... TTI_SFPSHFT with no exponent clamp on the large side: ckernel_sfpu_typecast.h, ckernel_sfpu_exp.h (WH/BH)
- _calculate_log_body_ / transcendental bodies whose only special case is `v_if(in == 0`: Quasar llk_sfpu and legacy tt-llk common/inc/sfpu
- `% mesh_shape[` / `(x - 1 + n) % n` / `TorusTopology::RING` / `num_devices == 2 && topology == Ring` with no extent>2 check, and `TT_FATAL(.*topology == .*Ring` in factories: tt_metal/fabric, ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/{ccl,experimental/ccl}
- `if (!is_device_tensor(t)) return ...view(` and host `view()` helpers with no TILE/padded[-2:] check: ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/reshape_view, ttnn/core/tensor/tensor_impl.cpp
- `lowest_occupied_compute_l1_address` / `AllocatorID{0}` / `num_allocators() == 1` / `get_bank_ids_from_logical_core(...)[0]`: tt_metal/impl/{program,dataflow_buffer,allocator,host_api}
- `nb::class_<T>` blocks with no `nb::self == nb::self` where T declares operator==; `->add_grad(res[` or `add_grad(out->get_grad())` with no unbroadcast: ttnn/cpp/ttnn-nanobind, ttnn/core/distributed, tt-train/sources/ttml/ops
- `block_row_size / 64`, `? 128 : 64`, `>> 6`, and `shape[2]`/`shape[3]` next to TILE_* without a rank check: ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/kernels/dataflow and pad/tilize/untilize factories

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise (unary/binary_ng/ternary dtype-to-kernel dispatch, composites); tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu and tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/sfpu (per-arch op/format ladders, domain edges); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl and experimental/ccl plus tt_metal/fabric (Ring/Torus on extent-2 axes, fallback selection); tt_metal/impl/{allocator,program,dataflow_buffer,sub_device,buffers} (multi-allocator, HYBRID, service-core cases); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (reshape host view, tilize/untilize element-size assumptions, pad rank); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv/conv2d (depthwise routing predicates, coalesced read limits); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar (forked copies that miss later fixes); ttnn/ttnn/core.py, ttnn/ttnn/operations/core.py and nanobind bindings (strategy branches, eq/ownership)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land on one copy of a duplicated decision. The sibling entry point (ternary vs binary_ng where), the sibling validator (DFB vs CB region), the Quasar or legacy tt-llk fork, or the other overloads (min/max backward, operator+ with a raw tensor) keep the missing case. Always grep for every copy before calling a fix complete.
- A demotion or fallback added at the entry point was never checked against downstream preconditions. For example, a Ring-to-Linear demotion feeds a factory that TT_FATALs unless the topology is Ring, and a restored composite route forwarded the raw topology and dropped the compute config. The fix turns a wrong result into a guaranteed abort.
- Guarding only one side of a range: the typecast zero fix clamped the small exponent but not the large one, and the fix blamed an 'instruction quirk' rather than the modulo-32 shift semantics, so the sibling side stayed open.
- Symptom patches such as routing a shape to a fallback, lowering PCC thresholds, or adding an arch-specific pytest.skip hid the real missing dtype or reconfig case and were later reverted or re-fixed when the root cause was found.
- A later refactor re-introduces the missing case. Examples: a descriptor migration hardcoded a flag that had been computed (row-broadcast bias = 1), a new mode (HYBRID) was added to one validator only, or an ownership mask was populated on one construction path only, so the gate silently no-op'd on the other.
- A predicate narrowed or widened for one case (fabric 2D vs 2D-dynamic, 1D depthwise kw==1 to kw>1, has_bias exclusion) left gaps between predicates or stale counts inside the path. Several follow-up fixes were needed to re-derive per-block indices and to cover combinations neither predicate claimed.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/ternary/device/ternary_op_utils.cpp:535 — The WHERE dispatch maps only FLOAT32/INT32. A UINT32 predicate falls to where_tile<Float16_b> (16-bit load/store on 32-bit data), while the TSS/TTS paths map UINT32 correctly.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/all_to_all_async/device/all_to_all_async_device_operation.cpp:225 — The entry point demotes Ring to Linear for 2 devices, but all_to_all_async_program_factory.cpp:151 TT_FATALs unless the topology is Ring, so a 2-device call always aborts.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/quasar/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_log.h:51 — The only special case is in==0, so negative, NaN and +Inf inputs return a finite polynomial value. The legacy tt-llk WH/BH common ckernel_sfpu_log.h:55 has the same gap and is reachable from xlogy.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_typecast.h:998 — In fp32->uint8, SFPSHFT by (exp-23) has only the small-side clamp. Exponents that make the shift 32 or more wrap modulo 32 and leave mantissa bits in the low byte. The BH copy near :972 is the same.
- tt-train/sources/ttml/ops/binary_ops.cpp:242 — The min/max backward passes the full-shape gradients from min_bw/max_bw to add_grad without unbroadcast. The operator+(TensorPtr, ttnn::Tensor/AutocastTensor) overloads at :89-100 also skip the reduction.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/reshape_view/reshape.cpp:623 — A host tensor always gets a pure view whatever its layout. A TILE host tensor whose padded inner-2D shape changes is reinterpreted instead of re-tiled, and the host view() at ttnn/core/tensor/tensor_impl.cpp:365 has no TILE check.
- tt_metal/impl/dataflow_buffer/dataflow_buffer.cpp:2666 — validate_dataflow_buffer_region uses only the tracker query. It lacks the HYBRID per-bank loop and the service-core check that validate_circular_buffer_region (program.cpp:2414) gained.
- ttnn/core/distributed/distributed_nanobind.cpp:245 — The MeshCoordinateRange and MeshCoordinateRangeSet (:267) bindings have no __eq__, although mesh_coord.hpp:216/253 declare operator==, so Python compares by identity.
- ttnn/ttnn/core.py:355 — The tile-alignment check uses `shard_shape[0] % 32 != 0 and shard_shape[1] % 32 != 0`, so it raises only when both dims are misaligned. The HEIGHT tile branch at :348 also pads to roundup(h, cores) rather than cores*32.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transformer/sdpa/sdpa.cpp:33 — A host-safe arch is computed and marked [[maybe_unused]], and then input_tensor_q.device()->arch() is dereferenced unguarded, so a host Q null-derefs before validation.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv/conv2d/conv2d_utils.cpp:545 — is_1d_depthwise_conv excludes has_bias, so a biased depthwise conv1d with kw>1 takes the general path. That path's reader coalesces kw*C bytes, bounded only by a device static_assert against the NoC burst size, with no host guard.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/untilize_with_unpadding/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_unary_unpad_dims_split_rows.cpp:38 — Tiles per block come from a 4-or-2-bytes boolean (/128 or /64) instead of the element size, so a 1-byte TILE dtype gets the wrong count.


## `validation-gap`: Guards checking the wrong thing, or only some paths

A precondition the consumer depends on is either missing, checked against the wrong reference, or checked on only some of the paths into the consumer. The common wrong-reference cases: an aggregate bound where the consumer needs a per-unit one (total buffer size vs per-bank/per-shard size, L1 core count used for DRAM shards), a padded shape where the logical one matters (a tile-padded seq=1 tensor looks like 32 rows), the product of axes instead of each axis, a strict-vs-inclusive off-by-one, or a comparison that reads back the value it is meant to check (compute_output_specs returning the preallocated output's own spec, or a "configured correctly" assert that reads the table holding the bad value). The common coverage cases: a check that lives in one wrapper, one factory, one arch copy, or only the first element (front() of a tensor list, the unicast but not the multicast command path, the constructor but not the offset/update/cache-hit mutator), so other entry points skip it. Many guards are also weak: TT_ASSERT or LLK_ASSERT (compiled out in release), log_warning, or debug-only allow-lists that hard-halt on shapes that are legal but were never listed. When a missing check lets bad input through, the usual result is a silent out-of-bounds read, aliasing, or a hang. The opposite failure also happens: validation gets tightened (equal tile sizes, physical-shape equality, a DRAM-only alignment rule applied to writes) and rejects legitimate flows until someone reverts it.

**Checks**

- Per-unit vs aggregate: for every capacity check on a per-core, per-bank or per-shard resource (CB size, shard count, scratch region), confirm the bound is the per-unit size of the resource set that actually backs the value (L1 compute cores for L1, DRAM banks for DRAM). Test the 'use every unit' edge case to settle < vs <=.
- Every mutator re-applies the bound: list every constructor, setter, offset setter, update/rebind API and cache-hit override path that changes a base address or size. Each must re-derive the same bound as (unit size - offset). Any unsigned `x -= offset` must be preceded by `offset <= x`, or it wraps and disables the check that follows.
- Self-referential validation: in validate(), if the expected value comes from compute_output_specs, another derived table, or the same config entry, check whether that source short-circuits to the value under test (for example `if (preallocated) return preallocated.spec()`). If it does, the check is a tautology. Recompute the expected value from inputs and attributes only.
- Companion-tensor contract: for every optional tensor read in lockstep with a primary input (indices, cos/sin, masks, scales, batch_offset), validation must tie its logical shape, padded tile grid, dtype width and layout to the primary's, at both the composite wrapper and the device primitive. Kernels that page the companion with the primary's stride (`i*Wt+j`) read out of bounds when the companion is narrower. Also check each caller after a split or slice, and on every mesh-shape branch including 1x1.
- Coverage comparisons use logical_shape: any 'A covers B' check (seq length, token index, broadcast dim) done on padded_shape() is suspect. 0-based indices need strict <. A factory that silently takes min() of the two lengths is the same gap.
- Choke-point placement: an invariant enforced in one convenience wrapper, one sharded branch, one factory or one arch copy (WH/BH vs Quasar, main vs experimental) must also be enforced at the lowest point every path goes through. Diff each arch's copy of the same API for missing asserts, e.g. dest-index bounds.
- Release-mode strength: user-input or cross-component size checks written as TT_ASSERT, LLK_ASSERT, log_warning or `#ifndef DEBUG` do nothing in production. Flag the ones that are the ONLY guard in front of a hang or a buffer overrun, and any 'TODO: should be TT_FATAL'.
- Fast-path selectors: for each can_use_*_optimized / select_program_factory, list what the chosen factory assumes: input AND output buffers in L1 (when CBs are borrowed from tensors), shard grid inside the compute grid and in the factory's core enumeration order, tile-aligned shards, default sub-device. Each assumption needs a check or a fallback.
- Tightened guards and exemptions: when a change adds a TT_FATAL or propagates a source attribute into derived outputs, find a real caller that produces the rejected state, and apply the same rule to the paired transforms (to/from layout, pad/unpad, input/output). For each 'path X handles Y, skip the check' exemption, trace path X end to end. Look for guards that sit after an unconditional return.
- Directional or consumer-dependent rules: when a restriction applies only to one direction (read vs write vs atomic) or one consumer (compute vs data-movement), check that the validator receives that property and that convenience macros do not hardcode it. Check that granularity checks use the same constant as the shift or divide that consumes the value.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `compute_output_specs(.*tensor_args)` used inside validate* and compared against `optional_output_tensor`/`preallocated_output`, where compute_output_specs begins with `if (tensor_args.<out>.has_value()) return ...tensor_spec()` (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/device/*_device_operation.cpp)
- `max_size_ -= ` / `set_address_offset(` followed by `set_globally_allocated_address(`; `buffer.size()` or `aligned_size()` used as a CB bound instead of `aligned_size_per_bank()` (tt_metal/impl/buffers, tt_metal/impl/host_api/tt_metal.cpp)
- `TT_FATAL\(.*padded_shape\(\)\[-2\] >=` and `std::min(.*padded_shape` in rope/rotary/embedding/cache ops (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer)
- Optional `indices_tensor` / `*_indices_tensor` validated only by dtype; reader kernels computing `page_id = i * Wt + j` for a second accessor (ttnn reduction/topk, sampling, models/common/sampling)
- `can_use_sharded_optimized_factor*` or select_program_factory without `buffer_type() != BufferType::L1` for the INPUT, or without a shard-grid vs compute_with_storage_grid_size() containment check (ttnn data_movement tilize/untilize families)
- `TT_ASSERT(command_size_B <= max_` or any TT_ASSERT/LLK_ASSERT that is the sole size or bounds guard (tt_metal/impl/dispatch, hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api)
- `LLK_ASSERT.*get_dest_max_tiles` present in blackhole/*_api.h but absent from the quasar/*_api.h twin; direct `_llk_math_eltwise_unary_sfpu_params_(` calls that bypass the checked macro (tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api)
- `dim == -1 ||` special-casing instead of `dim < 0 ? dim + rank`; `padded_shape().rank()` used for normalization (ttnn host op wrappers: gather/scatter/reduction)
- `TT_FATAL(input_tile_size == output_tile_size` and output CB page size taken from the input data format (ttnn eltwise/bcast/typecast sharded factories)
- Sanitizer macros that hardcode a direction (`DEBUG_SANITIZE_NOC_READ`) and are reached from write/atomic APIs such as noc_semaphore_inc and noc_inline_dw_write (tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/debug/sanitize.h, hw/inc/api/dataflow)

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/impl/buffers (Buffer/ShardSpec validation, circular_buffer_config) and tt_metal/impl/allocator; tt_metal/impl/host_api/tt_metal.cpp (legacy APIs: CreateDevice, UpdateDynamicCircularBufferAddress, runtime args); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (tilize/untilize/reshard/gather/scatter/bcast sharded fast paths); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer (rotary_embedding*, nlp_create_qkv_heads*); ttnn device-op validate + program-hash layer (preallocated outputs, optional companion tensors, topk); tt_metal/hw/ckernels/{quasar,blackhole,wormhole_b0}/metal/llk_api and tt-llk debug coverage tables; tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/debug/sanitize.h and dataflow API call sites; tt_metal/impl/tensor/tensor_apis.cpp host layout conversions; distributed/fabric bring-up (mesh_device, fabric_firmware_initializer, metal_env)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land at the call site that reported the bug and skip the siblings. Examples: the unicast path gets chunked but the multicast path does not; one arch or the experimental Quasar copy is missed; the sharded factory is guarded but the interleaved one is not; the wrapper validates but the device-op validate still has the old rule. Diff all copies after the fix.
- A later feature reopens a closed invariant. The per-bank CB bound was fixed, then an address_offset feature re-derived max_size_ without the offset and added an unchecked unsigned subtraction. Re-audit every mutator whenever new state (offsets, service cores, sub-devices) is added.
- Tightening goes too far and gets reverted. Examples: strict shard-core bank checks broke legitimate service-core users; physical-shape equality in host to_layout broke sharded conversions, and it was reverted on one direction only, so the paired transform still carries it. Before adding a TT_FATAL, list the legitimate out-of-map users.
- Guards get downgraded. A size check was turned back into TT_ASSERT for performance, and earlier sharding checks were TT_ASSERT and did nothing in release. A fix is not complete until its backstop survives release builds.
- The workaround hides the root cause. Pinning CI to batch 1, building tensors on the host first, or loosening a server arg made the symptom go away while the out-of-bounds read or silent truncation stayed. A fix that works by changing allocation or construction points to an OOB or uninitialized read.
- Normalizing with the wrong quantity. Using padded rank (max(rank,2) for TILE) instead of logical rank broke 1D tensors and needed a same-week follow-up. Use the quantity the downstream validator uses.

- tt_metal/impl/dispatch/system_memory_manager.cpp:863 — the only final guard on prefetcher command size is TT_ASSERT (compiled out in release), so an oversized command hangs the prefetcher instead of erroring
- tt_metal/tt-llk/common/tensor_shape_coverage_pack.h:22 — is_pack_tensor_shape_covered returns false for every shape. LLK_VALIDATE_TENSOR_SHAPE_PACK has no callers yet, so the first call site would halt debug builds on every shape, including 32x32
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/quasar/metal/llk_api/llk_math_transpose_dest_api.h:43 — forwards dst_index unchecked, while the BH twin asserts dst_index < get_dest_max_tiles_rt. Quasar llk_math_reduce_api.h:101, llk_math_binary_api.h:83 and datacopy (llk_math_unary_datacopy_api.h:85/127) also lack dest-capacity asserts

## `index-math`: Offset, stride and index-space arithmetic mismatches

An index, offset or size gets computed in the wrong space or unit, and the code still runs. The recurring mistakes: using a count where a start index is needed; using nominal capacity (per_shard - remaining, idx * per_core) instead of a running sum of what earlier partitions took; tiles vs heads vs bytes vs pages; bank index added to a byte offset; request order vs physical slot; logical vs padded vs folded rank; a range or offset relative to (0,0) or the grid width when it should be relative to the range's own start; a bank-relative operand given an absolute base. Chunk and ring arithmetic breaks at the edges: per-chunk size derived from the iteration index when the first chunk can be partial, so the unsigned subtraction wraps; a transfer that is not clamped to what is left in the current unit; a cursor advance missing the count factor; ptr == limit wrap tests with stride > 1; capacity that is not a multiple of the push size; producer and consumer charging different credits on the wrap or resize paths. Shape arithmetic hard-codes rank 4 (literal dims 2/3, [0]*[1]*[2]), aligns broadcast dims from the front, scales the logical dim before tile padding, or fails to invert a canonicalizing transform. Almost every one of these stays hidden under the default test shape: identity slots, one tile, a range starting at the origin, even splits, count == 1, full width equal to block width.

**Checks**

- Give every offset, index and size term a space and a unit (bytes/pages/tiles/heads, logical/padded, request order/slot, bank-relative/absolute, range-relative/grid-relative). Flag any +, - or * that mixes two of them, e.g. `limit - ptr / size`, `base + bank_id + offset`, `per_risc0_heads * single_tile_size`, or `count % shard_pages` used as a start id.
- For every work split across cores, links or RISCs, walk the LAST, uneven partition by hand. Its start offset must be a running sum of what earlier partitions actually took, or be derived from its global start index via div/mod, including secondary indices like batch or mask row. It must not be derived from nominal capacity, from count, or from zero. Test with a split that does not divide evenly (3 links, odd tile counts).
- When a core list for runtime args is built separately from the CoreRangeSet the kernels were created on, both must come from the same set in the same order. Flag `bbox.end_coord + 1` passed to an origin-based enumerator (grid_to_cores), coordinate wraps using the grid width or 0 instead of the range's start/end, inclusive CoreRange ends built from a count without `- 1`, and x/y counts bounded by one grid axis but used on the other.
- In chunked or streaming loops, the per-chunk size must come from a running remainder clamped to the bytes left in the CURRENT unit (page/tile/row). It must not be derived from `total - i*chunk`, and the chunk size alone is not enough. Unit bookkeeping (page_id, offset) may advance only when the unit is exactly complete. Test offset % page != 0 together with size not a multiple of page.
- For every ring or FIFO cursor: the increment must be count * stride; the wrap test must be >= or modulo, never ==, whenever the step may not land exactly on the limit; the capacity must be a multiple of every push or reserve size, or the wrap must keep the overshoot; and the cursor must be kept per channel or per slot. Producer and consumer must charge identical credits on the normal, wrap, resize and header-skip paths, with the same base (offset or absolute) and the same comparison.
- Wherever a normalized dim is compared to a literal (2, 3), or shape[0..3]/[-2]*[-3] is multiplied, look for a local rank==N check. If there is none, a fold over the dims or rank-relative logic is needed. A dim normalized against the logical shape must not index the padded shape, which has a different rank for rank<2 tiled tensors. Shapes of different rank must be aligned from the trailing end.
- When a buffer is sized as k copies by scaling a shape dim, scale AFTER tile or alignment padding (or scale the page count). Also check that the kernel's k-th slot offset (k * input_pages) fits. Grep every variant of the op (experimental, stable, fused) for the same arithmetic.
- For code that uses two index spaces (caller order vs slot/row/device), every buffer must be sized and indexed in one space. Every per-request side table (page tables, params, masks, per-layer tables) must go through the same permutation. Require a test with a sparse, non-identity mapping.
- For packed or rebuilt wide values (LO/MID/HI/COORD, bank<<32|offset), write out each field's shift and width against the decoder. Check that no two terms share a shift, that the low field is range-checked, and that macros use their own parameters and not a global with a similar name.
- For blocked kernels where block < full width: the row stride must come from the FULL width, and the block index must reach the address math at every API tier. Also check that the width baked in at init (MOP loop counts) equals the width passed at each call, including tail blocks. A block/offset parameter defaulting to 0 on a multi-block path is a red flag.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `bounding_box()` ... `end_coord.[xy] + 1` ... `grid_to_cores(` -- ttnn program factories (kv_cache, rotary_embedding*, nlp_create_qkv_heads, any sharded path)
- `inter_shape = .*logical_shape();` followed by `inter_shape[0] *= 2` or other scaling before padding -- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/** intermediate sizing
- `padded_shape()[0] * .*padded_shape()[1] * .*padded_shape()[2]`, `shape()[-2] * .*shape()[-3]`, `\bdim == [0-3]\b` with no rank()==4 assert -- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/{ccl,data_movement,eltwise}
- `std::min(\w+ - \w+ \* \w*(page|chunk)` and `xfer = page > cb_page ? cb_page : page` -- dataflow helpers (data_movement common.hpp) and dispatch kernels (cq_dispatch/cq_prefetch)
- `(rd|wr)_(ptr|entry_idx) +=` without the num_tiles/num_entries factor; `(ptr) == .*limit`; wrap that resets to base_addr -- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-2xx dataflow buffer, ckernels/quasar llk_io, remote_circular_buffer.h, CB helpers
- `fifo_size - fifo_rd_ptr` / `size - abs_ptr`; `data_ptr += sizeof(Header)` followed by `length - sizeof(Header)` feeding a release-pages count -- credit rings in hw/inc/api and dispatch kernels
- `pack_untilize_dest_init<A>` ... `pack_untilize_dest<B>` with A != B, and pack_untilize_block calls missing block_c_index -- ttnn pool/upsample/untilize compute kernels
- `for (i = start_id; i < start_id + n; ++i)` writing `.page_id = i` where start_id used a destination stride (dst_HtWt) -- writer kernels crossing head/batch boundaries
- `page_tables_per_layer[i]`, `out[slot] = ...` into a tensor sized by len(slots), `empty_slots=list(range(n))`, `batch // data_parallel` next to torch.chunk -- models/tt_transformers and models/demos generators
- `const auto& a = X.padded_shape(); const auto& b = X.padded_shape();` then `a[d] == b[d]` (tautological validation); `[[maybe_unused]]` on a coordinate parameter -- device_operation validate functions, tensor_accessor-style flatteners

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl (all_gather/reduce_scatter/broadcast/mesh_partition, ccl_common slicers); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations program factories with sharded/sub-grid core enumeration (kv_cache, experimental/transformer, reshard, sort, concat); tt_metal/impl/dispatch kernels and tt_metal/impl/buffers (paged writes, prefetch relay credits, distribution spec); circular/dataflow/remote buffer cursor and credit code (tt_metal/hw/inc/api, tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-2xx, ckernels/quasar llk_io); tt_metal/tt-llk and ckernels llk_api: Dest-bank-relative operands and pack_untilize block/full-width addressing; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement composite wrappers (gather, expand, reshape, concat) and rank handling; models/tt_transformers and models/demos/* generators (slot vs request order, data-parallel splits, per-layer page tables)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- The fix corrected one copy of copy-pasted arithmetic and left its twins alone. The stable reduce_scatter still doubles the logical dim after the experimental variant was fixed. There are about ten `bbox + grid_to_cores` factories besides the one fixed. The test generator was fixed in one microbenchmark and not its sibling. Always grep for the expression's twins across experimental, stable and fused variants.
- Refactors that move a cursor into a new per-slot struct dropped a factor (num_tiles). The accompanying test only pushed one entry, so the regression went unseen. Diff the arithmetic term by term and require count > 1 in tests.
- A fix that is correct for the tested positions was accepted as complete. The gather rank>4 inverse is correct only when dim is among the last three dims. The softmax causal-mask wrap is correct only when mask_Ht == Wt. Enumerate every dim position and non-square case before declaring it fixed.
- Fixes introduced edge-case arithmetic of their own: round-to-even made a divisor 0, and a wrap branch mixed a size with an absolute address (`fifo_size - rd_ptr`) and needed a re-fix. Fixing an ordering bug also exposed a latent `back()` on an empty vector. Re-check divisors, bases and the invariants the old behaviour satisfied by accident.
- A workaround was shipped in place of a root-cause fix (a blocking flush for an ordering race). Later the helper's default reverted to the non-blocking path, so the protection is now implicit. Treat a workaround as an open lead whose guard can silently disappear.
- Kernels were left with no bounds check on the derived index (for example, sort computes h with no check against Ht), and release builds drop the debug-only asserts, so correctness rests entirely on host arithmetic staying exact. Where it is cheap, add a device-side or always-on bound check next to the host fix.

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/kv_cache/device/update_cache_multi_core_program_factory.cpp:97 — the sharded path uses bbox.end+1 with origin-based grid_to_cores, so runtime args go to the wrong cores for an offset or non-rectangular shard grid. The same pattern is at :426.

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/rotary_embedding_hf/device/rotary_embedding_hf_multi_core_program_factory.cpp:287 — sharded core list is enumerated from the origin using bbox extents; also at :598.

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/nlp_create_qkv_heads/device/nlp_create_qkv_heads_program_factory.cpp:557 — the q shard grid's bbox is used with origin-based grid_to_cores. This is wrong for an offset q grid unless validation pins it to the origin.

- tt_metal/impl/buffers/buffer_distribution_spec.cpp:143 — the explicit-cores constructor checks member tensor_shape_in_pages_ (still default) before the squeeze assigns it, where it should check the argument. The zero-volume/empty-cores guard therefore tests the wrong variable.


## `tt-shard-layout`: Shard geometry diverges between host, kernel, and buffer

Host code rebuilds shard-to-core placement or shard geometry from a shortcut, and that shortcut disagrees with how the sharded buffer actually lays data out. Common shortcuts: bounding-box or origin-rooted arithmetic (i % grid_x, bbox.end_coord+1, grid_to_cores), the full allocated grid instead of the data-holding cores, the device grid instead of the tensor's shard grid, the other tensor's orientation, a uniform shard size, or a hand-sized CB. The bug surfaces only on non-default layouts: multi-range or holed grids, grids not anchored at (0,0), COL_MAJOR orientation, uneven or partial last shards (in width and block sharding as well as height), over-provisioned grids, padded vs logical shapes, row-major rows that are not L1-aligned, and DRAM instead of L1. Config classification is a second family. Ops decide "sharded?" from which spec representation happens to be present (a 2D shard_spec vs an nd_shard_spec) or from a raw config that is not the one validation checked. They inherit the input's shard spec onto a tensor with a different shape, or dereference an absent shard_spec on a layout-only config. Results are silent wrong data (swapped row blocks, users past 8 misplaced), out-of-bounds L1/DRAM writes from padding cores or oversized aliased CBs, hangs on CBs that were never created, and fatal errors on valid inputs.

**Checks**

- Find every place a shard/work index becomes a CoreCoord (runtime args, remote source/dest core, mcast range, override callback). It must index corerange_to_cores(tensor's own grid, that tensor's own orientation). Flag bbox.start + i%W, bbox.end_coord+1 used as a size, grid_to_cores(n, W, H), or {i % num_cores_x, i / num_cores_x}. Test with a holed multi-range grid, an offset grid, and input/output orientations that differ.
- Check that per-core loops, the 'last/partial core' choice, and cache-hit override loops cover exactly the data-holding cores (ceil(extent/shard) per dim), not the allocated grid. Check this for HEIGHT, WIDTH, BLOCK and N-D. A used-core recompute that has only HEIGHT/WIDTH branches leaves BLOCK open. Replace 'core == end_core' remainder logic with per-shard clipping min(chunk, total-offset).
- For partial last shards, the transfer count must use the partial extent, while the L1 row stride uses the FULL (aligned) shard width. Core walks must use >= and not == against the total width. The inverse op (to-sharded vs from-sharded vs reshard) and the host page mapping must apply identical rules.
- For a CB aliased onto a tensor buffer (.buffer=, borrowed_from, globally allocated), its total_size and the kernel iteration count must equal the buffer's real aligned per-core size (aligned_page_size x pages). Round bytes with the allocator's alignment, never element counts. Treat any 'CB exceeds buffer' warning as an OOB write. Divisibility guards must be TT_FATAL, not TT_ASSERT, because TT_ASSERT is compiled out in release.
- Output-config classification: 'is this sharded?' must key on memory_layout()/is_sharded() or on the computed output spec. It must not key on nd_shard_spec().has_value() or shard_spec().has_value(), and this applies in validators too. Every layout (HEIGHT/WIDTH/BLOCK/N-D) needs its own branch, and an unknown layout is a fatal error, not a fallthrough to interleaved. Validation and factory selection must evaluate the same predicate on the same resolved config.
- Fast-path factory selectors must check buffer type (is_l1) AND layout AND shard_spec().has_value() for EVERY tensor the factory aliases, input and output alike. Check the mixed cases (L1 in with DRAM out, sharded in with interleaved out). Any CB a selected kernel touches unconditionally must be created under the same condition that selected that kernel.
- For shape-changing ops that inherit the input memory config (value_or(input.memory_config()), views, internal squeeze-to-3D), the shard shape and rank must be recomputed from the output dims. When shard specs are synthesized, the declared grid must come from the populated shard count, not the device grid. For BLOCK, the divisors must follow orientation, and overflowing the grid is an error, not a clamp.
- Operands consumed core-locally (a CB bound to operand B's buffer on operand A's cores) must have identical grid and orientation. Trace where each operand's grid comes from (device grid vs hard-coded 8-wide, batch vs padded batch), evaluate on every arch grid width, and check that the op validates grid equality, not just is_sharded().
- Sharded kernels that compute a global index as core*shard_size+local must clamp the padded last shard and surplus cores before forming a page id or NoC address. A bound passed as a compile-time arg but never read is a red flag.
- In auto-config and heuristic code (matmul program configs), the grid size AND origin must come from the tensor's shard spec, including 1x1/1xN/Nx1 grids. Guards like 'grid.x > 1 and grid.y > 1' that send small grids to a device-grid fallback are suspect. Packed runtime-arg fields derived from shard dims need a release-mode range check against the kernel's decode width.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- bounding_box\(\)\.end_coord\.[xy] \+ 1 and core = \{i % num_cores_x, i / num_cores_x\} in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/*sharded*program_factory*.cpp, kv_cache, transformer factories
- grid_to_cores\(num_cores, num_cores_x, num_cores_y in a factory whose all_cores = shard_spec.grid (kv_cache, groupnorm-style factories)
- std::map<std::pair<uint32_t, uint32_t>, std::vector<uint32_t>> core_stick_map: a gather plan ordered by coordinates but consumed in data order (pad/transpose sharded factories, Quasar copies)
- corerange_to_cores(output_grid, ..., row_major) where row_major is read from the INPUT shard spec (pad/slice/transpose)
- num_cores_unpadded computed under if (HEIGHT_SHARDED) ... else if (WIDTH_SHARDED) with no BLOCK branch; core == end_core remainder selection (sharded_to_interleaved and its partial/Quasar variants)
- round_up_to_mul16/32 applied to shard_spec.shape[1], or shape[0]*shape[1]*datum_size sizing a CBDescriptor with .buffer = tensor buffer; TT_ASSERT(shard_bytes % tile_size) (eltwise unary/typecast, move)
- output_mem_config.nd_shard_spec().has_value() or .shard_spec()->/.value() on a caller-supplied config, used as the sharded predicate or dereferenced without a has_value() guard (reduction, pad selectors, reshape)
- if (out_sharded) { ... out_cb_index CB } while the kernel is chosen by if (in_sharded) (experimental/transformer nlp_* factories)
- packed runtime args like (x << 16) | (n << 8) | skip with a kernel decode of & 0xff / & 0xffff and no TT_FATAL bound (reshard generic)
- models: num_cores_to_corerangeset(batch, device.compute_with_storage_grid_size()) for one operand vs ttnn.CoreGrid(y=4,x=8) for a sibling; literal mesh divisors like dim // 8 // 32; get_device_tensors(x)[0] compared with full-batch host tensors

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (pad, slice, transpose, sharded i2s/s2i/reshard, move, indexed_fill); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/* (stale copies of pre-fix factories); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/kv_cache/device; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary (row-major sharded CB sizing); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/config (auto program config vs shard grid); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer (rotary, concat heads, create_qkv_heads); tt_metal/impl/buffers + ttnn/core/tensor (page mapping, N-D squeeze, host encode paths); models/tt_transformers/tt (rope grids, Galaxy memcfgs, generator shard-0 read-back)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land in the mainline factory and skip the experimental/quasar clone. Every fix here has left a verbatim pre-fix copy, so always grep the twin directory.
- Fixes were staged by strategy: HEIGHT/WIDTH got fixed and BLOCK (and N-D) stayed open through several rounds. A debug-only TT_ASSERT stood in as the 'guard' until someone noticed release builds walk padding cores. Check every sharding strategy and both the create and override paths.
- A fix repairs the first symptom (the runtime args on a non-kernel core) and leaves the second invariant from the same shortcut in place (the coordinate-sorted gather order, or the other tensor's orientation). That only surfaced later as silent row swaps.
- Model-side workarounds (reshard hops, DRAM round trips, unpad-before-reshard, skipped batch sizes) got reverted or left in place while the op bug stayed. Treat a model 'normalising copy' or a TODO as a pointer to an unfixed library defect.
- Rounding fixes used the wrong unit (elements instead of bytes) and made an aliased CB larger than its buffer. The fix was reverted, then re-landed with pytest.skip on the failing shapes, and only removed a year later after model corruption. A skip of the failing shapes signals that the root cause was missed.
- Guards were added in the wrong place: after an earlier use of the shard-derived tensor, gated on one spec representation, or only in the public wrapper and not in the device op's validate. Later consolidations also downgraded TT_FATAL to TT_ASSERT or dropped the alignment check entirely.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/pad/device/pad_rm_sharded_height_only_program_factory.cpp:141 — shard_id to core uses bounding-box inner/outer arithmetic, and core_stick_map (:128) is a std::map ordered by coordinates. Both defects fixed in mainline pad remain; runtime args at :402 also use bbox start + i%W.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/device/transpose_hc_sharded_program_factory.cpp:353 — grid_size = bbox.end_coord+1 (origin-anchored) and core = {i % num_cores_x, i / num_cores_x} at :54/:111/:428/:450. An offset or multi-range shard grid misplaces runtime args and source cores.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transpose/device/transpose_hc_sharded_program_factory.cpp:352 — Quasar twin with the same bbox end+1 arithmetic plus a core_stick_map std::map ordered by coordinates (:166).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/kv_cache/device/update_cache_multi_core_program_factory.cpp:82 — the sharded path sizes num_cores_x/y as bbox.end_coord+1, then enumerates origin-rooted cores via grid_to_cores (:97, and the override at :200/:426).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/kv_cache/device/fill_cache_multi_core_program_factory.cpp:65 — same end_coord+1 plus origin-rooted grid_to_cores for sharded input (:80, :151, :224).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/sharded_to_interleaved/device/sharded_to_interleaved_program_factory.cpp:102 — num_cores_unpadded is recomputed only for HEIGHT/WIDTH. BLOCK keeps the full grid, so the loop at :256 and the end_core remainder logic walk padding-only cores past the tensor end on over-provisioned block grids.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/pad/device/pad_device_operation.cpp:37 — the selector checks the output is_sharded/HEIGHT_SHARDED but not is_l1(), and dereferences output_mem_config.shard_spec()-> at :46 with no has_value() guard. A layout-only config is undefined behaviour, and a DRAM output fails instead of falling back.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/pad/device/pad_rm_sharded_height_only_program_factory.cpp:442 — the output grid is enumerated with the INPUT shard orientation (row_major from the input spec, :263), and nothing validates that the orientations match.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction/generic/device/reduce_op_device_operation.cpp:193 — output shard-grid validation is gated on output_mem_config.nd_shard_spec().has_value(), so legacy 2D sharded output configs skip the grid-containment and alignment checks.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/nlp_concat_heads_boltz/device/nlp_concat_heads_boltz_program_factory.cpp:150 — the output CB is created only if out_sharded, while a sharded input always selects the sharded kernel, which reserves the output CB. Validation allows interleaved output, which risks a hang.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/sharded/reshard/device/reshard_program_factory_generic.cpp:651 — num_strides is packed into 8 bits and start_data/stride fields into 16 bits, with no release-mode range check. A tall uneven reshard could overflow them and silently corrupt data.

## `build-config`: Build flags, globs, linker scripts silently misconfigure artifacts

The build or link configuration says one thing and the artifact gets another, usually with no error. (1) A preprocessor or feature macro guards correctness code, but the macro is never emitted for that target or caller. It may be missing from the HAL/JIT define list, defined only in a generated textual prolog the firmware TU never sees, set by only one of several program factories, or stale after a rename inside an arch-only block that the default build never compiles. The result is a hardware enable, drain or cache flush compiled out, or a silent #else default. A related case: a runtime throw under `#if !FEATURE ... #endif` still compiles the optional API after it. (2) Packaging and install rules capture the wrong file set. Bare trailing `/*` configure-time globs pull in transient files. Hand-typed extension lists drop headers that JIT kernels #include. INSTALL_RPATH bakes in absolute build-tree or machine paths. Library-type choices (OBJECT vs SHARED) duplicate global state across .so files. (3) Linker scripts and memory maps leave compiler-emitted sections (.srodata*, .sdata*) as orphans, or align segments to word size when the NoC load path needs 16 bytes. They also derive one constraint from another's constant, size firmware regions with almost no headroom for worst-case flag builds, or define firmware-exported symbols (the global pointer) through PROVIDE() so the symbol is missing in some processor/mode variants. (4) Argument and format plumbing breaks: several options packed into one quoted argv element, space-joined strings where CMake expects lists, or runtime format strings passed to consteval-checked fmt APIs, which then fail under a newer language standard.

**Checks**

- For every #ifdef / #if defined() / if constexpr guard around correctness code (hardware enables, barriers/drains, cache flushes, pointer-wrap masks), find where the macro is defined and confirm it is visible in every TU and every caller that needs the code. Check the HAL/JIT define lists per core type (-D flags vs a generated textual prolog), and check each host program factory that builds that kernel. A guard whose macro is never set for a reachable path is dead code.
- Flag every per-core or per-thread resource selector that ends in a silent `#else <default>`. Require an #error or static_assert there, and check that its macros come from -D flags, not from a per-kernel prolog that firmware TUs never include.
- Preprocess or compile every arch/core-only block (Quasar DM, ERISC, 2-ERISC mode) for its real target. After any rename of a template parameter or identifier, grep inside the inactive #if branches for the old name.
- For each configure-time file(GLOB/GLOB_RECURSE) that feeds install() or a FILE_SET, evaluate it against the git-tracked files. Flag patterns ending in a bare `*` (they capture transient files). Also flag any tracked file under the globbed dir that no pattern matches, especially .hpp/.h headers #included by JIT kernels that sit under a *.cpp-only glob. When many globs change at once, diff the installed file list before and after.
- Grep INSTALL_RPATH, CMAKE_INSTALL_RPATH, install destinations and generated config files for PROJECT_BINARY_DIR, CMAKE_BINARY_DIR, CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR, /opt, /home. When removing one, list every location the artifact is installed or copied to (the prefix, wheels, in-source editable copies) and confirm each resolves through $ORIGIN-relative entries alone (run ldd on the relocated artifact).
- Diff each linker script against its siblings for the same core family. Every compiler-emitted input section (.srodata.cst*, .sdata*, .sbss*, .tdata, init/fini arrays) must be placed explicitly, or orphans must be rejected. Every NoC/DMA-loaded output section must start on the transfer alignment (16 B), not the word alignment. Check that the ELF loader's alignment check matches the write path's requirement.
- For each symbol a kernel imports from firmware (--just-symbols or weakened ELF), confirm every processor/mode firmware variant defines it unconditionally, including variants that skip the common crt0. Before turning PROVIDE() into a hard definition, check what keys off the symbol (gp-relative relaxation needs the startup code to actually load gp).
- For fixed-size firmware/code regions in device memory maps, build under the worst-case flag combination (profiler + watcher + debug print + perf counters) and flag regions with little headroom. Flag FORCE_INLINE helpers from shared headers that inline into firmware. Also check that each separation/alignment constraint has its own named constant and is applied to every core type that shares the hazard.
- For OBJECT/STATIC libraries that hold globals or static variables, list every shared object that links them under each build-option combination. More than one loaded consumer means duplicated singletons and a double free at exit. Also confirm option-dependent LIB_TYPE variables are set before the first add_library that uses them.
- In shell scripts that build argv arrays, flag any single quoted element containing more than one option (a space followed by '-'). In CMake, flag launcher/flag variables built as space-joined strings instead of lists. For fmt/std::format wrappers, require format_string<Args...> parameters, and build public headers under every consumer language standard.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rn 'GLOB_RECURSE[^)]*/\*\s*)' --include=CMakeLists.txt ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations tt_metal  (bare trailing-* install globs)
- GLOB_RECURSE ... device/kernels/*.cpp in ttnn op CMakeLists, cross-checked with `grep -rn '#include "[^/]*\.hpp"' device/kernels` for local headers not captured
- grep -rn -A2 'INSTALL_RPATH' --include=CMakeLists.txt --include=*.cmake | grep -E 'PROJECT_BINARY_DIR|CMAKE_BINARY_DIR|CMAKE_SOURCE_DIR|/opt/|/home/'
- `#if defined(ARCH_QUASAR) && defined(COMPILE_FOR_DM)` and `#if defined(COMPILE_FOR_*ERISC)` blocks in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib and tt_metal/hw: identifiers that no longer exist in the enclosing scope
- kernel-side `#ifdef ENABLE_*`/`#if FEATURE` wrapping async_atomic_barrier / async_write_barrier / flush / pop_front, cross-checked against `defines["..."]` insertions in each program factory for that kernel path
- `#elif defined(TRISC_UNPACK|TRISC_MATH|TRISC_PACK)` ... `#else` in headers reachable from tt_metal/hw/firmware (prolog-only macros), versus UCK_CHLKC_* / COMPILE_FOR_* -D flags from tt_metal/llrt/hal/*/hal_*common.cpp
- `static .* __attribute__((section(` inside a #define or lambda body (CTSTR, SAN_STRING) expanded in inline/template functions (COMDAT linkage can move the object out of the named section)
- tt_metal/hw/toolchain/*.ld and tt-llk test *.ld: `.data : ALIGN(4)` or output sections lacking `*(.srodata*)`/`*(.sdata*)`; `PROVIDE(__global_pointer$`; `ALIGN(. + *PAD` arithmetic
- `#define MEM_\w+_FIRMWARE_SIZE` in dev_mem_map.h with `git log -L` showing repeated small bumps
- `#if !\w*HAS_\w+` followed by throw/return and `#endif`, then optional-API calls (MPIX_*, feature-only symbols) outside the guard; unconditional includes of vendor-specific headers (mpi-ext.h)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/CMakeLists.txt (FILE_SET kernels globs, especially experimental/ and deepseek_prefill/); tt_metal/hw/toolchain (main.ld, erisc-*.ld linker scripts); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/*/dev_mem_map.h (fixed firmware regions, pads); tt_metal/hw/firmware and tt_metal/llrt/hal (per-core define lists vs firmware #ifdefs); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib and op dataflow kernels (arch-only #if blocks, factory-set defines); tt_metal/CMakeLists.txt, ttnn/CMakeLists.txt (RPATH, library types, install rules); tt_metal/hw/inc/api/debug and tt-llk/tools/include/sanitizer (section-placed strings); tt_metal/distributed/multihost (optional MPI/ULFM feature guards)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixing a mass pattern by hand (narrowing many bare globs to typed extension lists) causes the opposite failure. Wrong lists dropped real kernel sources and headers from packages. New ops later copy-pasted the bare glob back. Verify with an installed-file-list diff, and add a lint or CI packaging check rather than relying on convention.
- Removing an absolute RPATH broke an artifact installed at a different relative depth (an in-source copy of the Python bindings) that had depended on it silently. It was reverted and relanded with an extra $ORIGIN entry. Enumerate every place the artifact lands before removing a search path.
- Making a PROVIDE()'d linker symbol unconditional fixed a link error but changed codegen (gp-relative relaxation) in a firmware variant whose gp was never loaded. The fix was reverted and the original link failure still has no landed fix. Check every side effect a linker symbol triggers, per firmware variant.
- Firmware-region overflows are repeatedly 'fixed' by bumping the region size to absorb the latest growth, with no headroom policy or worst-case-flag build gate. Treat a size bump as a workaround, not a fix.
- Fixes applied at one site of a shared shape leave siblings live. The section-placement fix covered the DEVICE_PRINT record path but not the CTSTR string macro, which a nearby comment works around at a single call site. The exit-barrier fix covered the main kernel but not its Quasar copy or the receiver/other signaler kernels. When fixing, grep for every copy of the shape, including arch forks.
- A fix whose symptom disappears may only have fixed one of several causes (CC-enable fixed, but a multi-tile PCC loss was deferred). Confirm the whole failing test matrix before closing, not just the first repro.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary_backward/CMakeLists.txt:15 — kernels FILE_SET globs only device/kernels/*.cpp, but dataflow/row_major_pages.hpp is #included by both row-major reader and writer kernels, so installed packages would ship kernels without their header (JIT compile failure)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/matmul_decode/CMakeLists.txt:16 — bare `device/kernels/*` install glob

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek_prefill/attn_res_gather_softmax/CMakeLists.txt:21 — bare `device/kernels/*` install glob; the same line at :19 in seven sibling deepseek_prefill ops (inbound/outbound_socket_service_sync, mhc_split_sinkhorn, moe_hash_gate, pack_scaled_fp8_kv_cache, per_token_cast_back, per_token_cast_to_fp8)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/CMakeLists.txt:20 — `*/device/kernels*/*` remains a bare catch-all at the first level; only the deeper level is narrowed
- tt_metal/CMakeLists.txt:161 — INSTALL_RPATH still hard-codes absolute /opt/openmpi-v5.0.7-ulfm/lib, a machine-specific path baked into the installed library
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/debug/device_print.h:57 — CTSTR still places a lambda-local static in a named section, so COMDAT linkage in inline/template callers can move it out of the string section and the host silently drops it; SAN_STRING users like tt_metal/tt-llk/tools/include/sanitizer/output.h:27 (inline trigger_name) have exactly this shape
- tt_metal/hw/toolchain/main.ld:233 — __global_pointer$ is still PROVIDE(), so a firmware variant that neither links crt0 nor references gp exports no symbol for kernels that need it (the cold-cache link failure has no landed fix)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/index_tile_dataflow.hpp:121 — the uint32_t index-tile path fills the DFB entry with plain RISC stores and calls push_back with no Quasar-DM cache flush, unlike the reduce-scaler helper, which documents that flush as required
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_sender_writer_padding.cpp:677 — Quasar fork ends with only async_write_barrier after the fused-op signaler's atomic increments; the main-tree copy gained an unconditional async_atomic_barrier
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_receiver_writer_padding.cpp:238 — calls op_signaler and sender_sem.up(), then returns with no atomic drain (the same pending-atomics-at-exit shape exists in experimental/ccl/strided_all_gather_async/device/kernels/minimal_default_reader.cpp:223)


## `tt-tile-shape`: Tile geometry and padding assumed, not derived

Code assumes a full 32x32, 4-face tile, or a tile-aligned logical shape, when it should take the geometry from the operand or the tensor. In the LLK and compute-API layers this shows up in several ways. Init or getter overloads fill num_faces, face_r_dim or partial_face with constants. Wrappers accept a geometry parameter and drop it ("not used"). MOP loop counts are hard-coded to 4. Per-arch copies of the metadata accessors are stubs. Reconfig fast paths compare only the data format, so a change in face or tile shape is skipped. Uninit paths restore 16-row-face defaults. On the host side, the logical and padded shape spaces get mixed. Examples: a padded volume divided by a logical dim, a logical dim floor-divided by 32, a logical dim scaled to size a staging buffer when that dim sits inside a tile, padded rank used to normalise a dim, and a padded shape passed where TensorSpec expects a logical one. Reductions, contractions and selections (sum, mean, variance, max, argmax, prod, matmul K) run over whole tiles and trust the implicit padding. The padding is often not the op's identity value, because reshape, slice and other device ops leave garbage there. The result is silently wrong output that PCC often cannot see. Tiny and shared-exponent tiles also break DRAM alignment on archs with 64 B alignment when the L1 stride uses the unpadded tile size.

**Checks**

- For every LLK/compute-API init, short-init, reinit, uninit and execute wrapper, check that num_faces, face_r_dim, tile_r/c_dim, partial_face and narrow_tile come from the operand (CB id or descriptor). Flag literals such as 4 faces, 16-row faces or DEFAULT_TENSOR_SHAPE. Flag parameters that are accepted but not forwarded. Flag sibling wrappers in the same file that disagree on whether they pass operands.
- Build a table: geometry-derived config state -> {hw_configure, reconfig, init, uninit} writers. Flag any register or MOP field that only hw_configure writes. Flag any old-vs-new operand reconfig shortcut whose skip condition compares only the data format and not face_r_dim, num_faces, tile dims or partial/narrow flags. This applies to the unpack AND pack sides.
- Diff each per-arch copy (WH/BH/Quasar) of the metadata accessors, reduce, tilize and matmul LLK wrappers. Any accessor that returns a constant where a sibling arch indexes the generated per-CB array is a stub. Where an operand is routed to the other source register (in0->SrcB), check that init and execute resolve every per-operand attribute through the same swap.
- Find MOP loop counts and address-mod increments (outerloop, innerloop, dest incr) and add up the counter movement per pass for 1-, 2- and 4-face tiles and face_r_dim < 16, per broadcast type. Pair each math-side count with its unpack-side publish count. Also check that face_r_dim never exceeds 16 for any reachable window or stick count.
- For every reduction, contraction or selection over a tiled dimension (sum/mean/var/RMS, max/min/argmax/topk/sort, prod, matmul K, softmax), check that padding lanes of the final tile are masked or overwritten with the op's identity (0, 1, -inf/+inf, INT sentinels). The consuming op must do this on EVERY entry path, including already-TILE input, fused residual/bias operands, both matmul operands, and every last tile along the reduced dim (not only the final tile). The divisor or scaler must use the logical extent. Reject analytic 'assume the padding is zero' corrections.
- In host program factories and compute_output_specs, check that each count's numerator and denominator come from the same shape space. Flag physical_volume()/logical dim, logical dim / 32 without div_up, scaling of a logical dim that lies inside a tile to size an intermediate, padded rank in dim normalisation, and padded_shape passed to TensorSpec. Fast paths chosen from padded tile counts must also require logical tile alignment.
- Check that device validate() rejects the non-tile-aligned shapes its kernels cannot mask. Do not rely on a composite or host wrapper to reroute, because direct prim callers bypass it.
- For DRAM->L1 readers and writers, compare the CB page size and per-tile write-pointer step with the allocator's DRAM page stride (tile size rounded to the arch DRAM alignment). Evaluate this for every accepted dtype x tile shape, especially bfp8/bfp4 tiny tiles on BH, and on the output path too.
- For kernels that read row-major side tensors (indices, targets, plans, masks) per tile-row, check that the read length does not exceed the page's remaining logical bytes when H % 32 != 0. Values read that way must be bounds-checked before they are used as addresses. Kernels that walk faces 0..3 must skip faces that hold only padding when the logical width or height is 16 or less.
- For view and shape ops (squeeze, reshape, slice, getitem, split) and host conversions (bfp pack/unpack, readback), check that logical and padded shapes are carried together. Identity or early-return branches must use the same convention as the main path. The layout tag must match the element order (tilize before a tile-order pack). A requested output dtype must be converted or rejected on every layout branch.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/*/llk_lib and hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api: `outerloop = 4`, `num_faces = 4`, `/*not used*/`, `DEFAULT_TENSOR_SHAPE`, `partial_face = 0` / `const bool partial_face = 0`
- hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_io: `get_(operand|output)_(num_faces|face_r_dim|tile_r_dim|tile_c_dim|partial_face|narrow_tile)\(.*\)\s*\{\s*return [0-9]+;`
