- hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api: `format\[old_.*!= .*format\[new_` reconfig shortcuts (unpack_src_format / pack_dst_format), which gate on format only
- llk_lib uninit/untilize/tilize: writes of a fixed 16-row face value or `Tile_x_dim` / TileDescriptor rmw with a constant instead of the operand's face_r_dim
- LLK_ASSERT used as the only guard against unsupported tile shapes (it compiles out in production). Example: throttled matmul MOP with tiny tiles
- ttnn factories: `padded_shape()[-1]` or `physical_volume() / W` feeding Wt/W for row reductions, with no `logical_shape`, `fill_implicit_tile_padding`, col/row mask or `logical == padded` TT_FATAL
- ttnn compute_output_specs: `auto s = ...logical_shape(); s[0] *= 2` for TILE tensors; `TensorSpec(` built from `padded_shape()`; `padded_shape().rank()` near `dim < 0` / `dim + rank`
- reduction host wrappers: `tilize_with_val_padding(..., 0` or transposes without pad_value feeding MAX/MIN/PROD; TILE branch lacking fill_implicit_tile_padding
- matmul/eltwise dataflow readers: `l1_write_addr += *_single_tile_size` / `set_page_size(..., single_tile_size)` without `tt::align(..., dram_alignment)`; in0 readers without pad_last_ktile
- dataflow kernels: `noc_async_read(..., TILE_HEIGHT * sizeof(uint32_t))` at offset `tile_row * TILE_HEIGHT * 4` from row-major side buffers; face loops `for face in 0..3` over row-major data

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/llk_lib (datacopy/B2D, matmul, reduce, unpack untilize/tilize) and the BH metal llk_api/llk_io layer: the BH copy lags WH in shape handling; tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api reconfig paths (llk_unpack_common_api.h, llk_pack_common_api.h); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization (layernorm, layernorm_distributed, groupnorm) and experimental/transformer pre-all-gather norm variants; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction (generic, prod, argmax) and pool2d; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul device factories and dataflow readers (K-padding, DRAM alignment for tiny tiles); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl reduce_scatter intermediate sizing and data_movement (split, squeeze, gather/scatter, embedding); tt-train/sources/ttml/metal/ops (cross_entropy, softmax, moe_group/ungroup) where row-major side buffers are read per tile-row

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes often patched the one wrapper or variant that failed, while siblings in the same file or family kept the bug: the full init next to the fixed short inits, sharded layernorm variants other than Welford, the residual operand fused ahead of the stats, the non-experimental reduce_scatter that drives the same kernel, and experimental DIT/fused-RMS copies. Sweep every sibling variant and every arch in the same change.
- A fix for the unpack reconfig 'format-only' skip was not mirrored on the pack side, which still gates on dst format only. When fixing one direction of a reconfig or uninit pair, check the other.
- Analytic compensation for padding (rescale the divisor, subtract K*mean^2) assumed zero padding and was superseded by masking. Padding contents are not guaranteed after reshape, slice or exp. The consuming op must mask or identity-fill.
- Padding fixes that covered only the last tile of a block (the matmul K pad) were incomplete. Every tile in the last K row or column needs it. The perf-cost-driven revert and reland of the same fix shows these guards are also at risk of being removed for performance.
- Fixes that removed an error symptom (watcher alignment error) without fixing wrong results, or that kept a test skipped or DISABLED, left the class without CI guard. Keep a non-aligned-shape regression test that feeds device-produced (dirty-padding) input and checks a non-affine-invariant metric.
- Guards placed only in the composite or host wrapper (Welford non-aligned H*W reroute, embedding dtype check in one branch) leave direct prim calls and other branches exposed. Put the rejection in device validate() and cover every layout branch.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/llk_lib/llk_math_eltwise_unary_datacopy.h:456 — B2D MOP outerloop fixed at 4. SCALAR and COL override it but ROW broadcast does not, so a 1- or 2-face tile gets 4 clears against num_faces unpack publishes (this is a BH hang; do not blindly mirror the change to WH:386)
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/blackhole/metal/llk_api/llk_pack_common_api.h:203 — old/new-output pack reconfig reprograms only when dst format differs, so a switch between outputs with the same format but a different num_faces or tile dims is skipped (same at wormhole_b0 :228)
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/llk_lib/llk_math_matmul.h:578 — the throttled matmul MOP supports only 32x32 tiles, guarded solely by an LLK_ASSERT that compiles out, and it is still entered for any tile shape when THROTTLE_LEVEL>0 (:678-682)
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/llk_lib/llk_unpack_untilize.h:151 — uninit restores a hard-coded 16x16 face x-dim regardless of the operand's face_r_dim
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/reduce_scatter/device/reduce_scatter_device_operation.cpp:47 — takes logical_shape, then `inter_shape[0] *= 2` for Linear (:57). With a sub-tile height the tile/page count does not double, so the intermediate is under-allocated relative to the kernel's input_num_pages offset
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/dit_layernorm_pre_all_gather/device/dit_layernorm_pre_all_gather_welford_program_factory.cpp:26 — W/Wt come from padded_shape and the padded W is passed to Welford. validate() has no tile-aligned-width check and there is no padding fill
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/fused_distributed_rmsnorm/device/fused_rmsnorm_pre_all_gather_program_factory.cpp:28 — sums x^2 over padded W. The pre-all-gather validate() lacks the logical==padded width check that the post-all-gather op has
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/groupnorm_device_operation.cpp:74 — device validate() does not reject use_welford with non-tile-aligned H*W. Only the composite reroutes, so direct prim calls reduce over padding rows
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/embedding/embedding.cpp:76 — typecast is applied only when the output is TILE, and the dtype TT_FATAL (:57) covers one branch only. An explicit layout=ROW_MAJOR or a non-aligned shape silently drops the requested dtype
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in0_ring_all_gather.cpp — no pad_last_ktile, unlike the sibling in0 readers. Check whether non-tile-aligned K can reach it (same question for reader_bmm_tile_layout_in0_sender_dram_sharded_height.cpp)
- tt-train/sources/ttml/metal/ops/moe_group/device/kernels/dataflow/moe_group_combined_kernel.cpp:570 — fixed TILE_HEIGHT*4-byte read per tile-row from a row-major plan buffer. Overruns unless the plan buffer is always padded to whole tile-rows
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/gather/tosa/gather_tosa.cpp:60 — the index rank constraint uses padded_shape().rank(), which TILE layout promotes to at least 2, so a logically 1D TILE index passes the rank-2 check


## `tt-format-resolution`: Data format resolved from the wrong source

A buffer's data format, Dest width, unpack mode or signedness is taken from the wrong place: another operand, the output tensor, the last configured CB, a shared hardware load/store mode, a JIT global default, or a catch-all bfloat16 branch. The format that is actually in the buffer is not used. The hardware then quietly reinterprets the bits. Two's-complement ints get read as sign-magnitude or as float. Fp32 gets truncated to TF32/bf16/fp16 through the Src registers. Block-float tiles get treated as 16-bit elements. UInt32 values at or above 2^31 get ranked as negative. On the device side the recurring shapes are these: reconfig calls whose 'old' or 'new' argument is not the CB actually unpacked or packed on every ifdef path; mode bits (zero-flag, Read_int8, Dest width in an instruction operand) set once at configure and never recomputed on reconfig, or dropped by a read-modify-write mask; and datapaths chosen from a predicate that special-cases one member of a format family. On the host side: fp32_dest_acc_en or UnpackToDestFp32 decided from one tensor (the output) or left at a default ComputeConfig; per-dtype tables that silently fall through to a float or bf16 kernel; scalars encoded in the tensor's dtype with a static_cast; and a fix applied to one program factory but not to the sibling factories that run the same kernel.

**Checks**

- Program factories: for every compute kernel, find where fp32_dest_acc_en comes from. It must be true whenever any CB it touches can be Float32/Int32/UInt32 under the op's validate(). This includes the case where the caller passes no compute config (a default ComputeConfig{} or ComputeConfigDescriptor{}). It also includes the case where only an INPUT is 32-bit, not just the output. The flag must agree with every CB marked UnpackToDestFp32.
- Kernels: for every reconfig_data_format*/pack_reconfig_data_format(old,new) and every *_init(cbA,cbB), trace the actually programmed srcA/srcB/pack format along every path: first iteration, loop back-edge, each #if/FUSE_*/untilize_out variant, and scalar-first operand swaps. Confirm that 'old' is the CB configured last and 'new' is the CB the next unpack/pack really touches. Resolve ternary aliases such as 'untilize_out ? partials : out' before comparing.
- Find every kernel that spills fp32 partials and reloads them into Dest (copy_tile, copy_block_matmul_partials). The factory must mark that CB unpack-to-Dest-fp32 when fp32 Dest is on. Then list EVERY factory that launches the same kernel, including Metal-2 ports, Quasar copies and fused-CCL copies, and confirm each one does. A fix in one factory does not fix its siblings.
- For each format-dependent hardware mode bit (src zero/denormal flag, packer Read_int8/Read_32b/Read_unsigned, Dest-width operands of ZEROACC/MOV*), check that hw_configure, every reconfig overload and every short init recompute it from the new format. For each read-modify-write, diff the fields assigned in the value against the mask passed. A field that is computed but not in the mask is silently dropped. Where a software cache shadows a register, grep for raw writes to that register that bypass it.
- Wherever code branches on a load/store mode, instruction modifier or register width to decide signedness or int-vs-float, list every data format that maps to that mode. UInt32 shares the INT32 mode with Int32. The branch must use the data format itself. Test at the bit-31 boundary, and confirm test stimuli use the production encoding (two's-complement, not sign-magnitude).
- Integer SFPU code: every Dest load/store of integer data must name an explicit integer layout (INT32/SM32/LO16/DataLayout::I32). DEFAULT follows the configured float format, and it becomes FP32 when 32-bit accumulation is on. Flag two's-complement emulation built from setsgn/0x7FFFFFFF/+-1 and signed-int kernels reused for UINT32 ordered comparisons, min/max or shifts.
- Host dtype tables (define maps, get_op_init_and_func, kernel selectors): list every dtype the op's validate() accepts and make sure each has an explicit branch. A catch-all else that maps to bf16 or float helpers is a finding when block-float or integer dtypes can reach it. Diff against the same table in a sibling op (for example binary_ng has bfp8/bfp4 fill variants but ternary does not).
- Multi-tensor factories: pair each CB's data_format, page size and tile size with the tensor whose buffer is bound to that CB. Flag sizes computed from tensor A that are used for tensor B, and shared tile_size_bytes advances across two CBs. When a buffer's format is made wider, recheck L1 capacity for the widest shape in every sibling factory.
- Kernels that synthesize tiles (mask, scaler, fill), or that leave alignment gaps in a CB: check that the pointer's element width matches the CB format for every accepted dtype, including block-float, whose tiles are smaller and share an exponent per 16 datums. Gap bytes in a block-float CB must be zeroed.
- Scalar encoding: every host scalar packed into the tensor's dtype (static_cast float to int, negative to unsigned) needs either a documented promotion or a hard error. Every public entry point that reaches the same packer, such as where/ternary/unary scalar overloads that call the prim directly, must share that policy.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rnE 'fp32_dest_acc_en\s*=\s*[^;]*(output|src0)[a-z_]*data_format\s*==\s*tt::DataFormat::Float32\s*;' ttnn/cpp/**/device/*factory*.cpp. Keep hits whose kernel unpacks to Dest or transposes, and hits where the same function sets UnpackToDestFp32 from input dtypes.
- ComputeConfig\{\}|ComputeConfigDescriptor\{\}|ComputeConfig\{\s*\.compile_args in ttnn/cpp/**/device/*program*.cpp (CCL reductions, tilize/untilize, transpose kernels), where validate() admits FLOAT32.
- '{INTERMED0_DFB, UnpackMode::UnpackToSrc}', or 'fp32_dest_acc_en ? tt::DataFormat::Float32' with no UnpackToDestFp32 in the same factory. Applies to matmul factories and the fused CCL matmul copies.
- pack_reconfig_data_format(X) followed by pack_tile(..., Y) where Y is an alias like untilize_mode_out_*; '#ifndef FUSE_BIAS' guarding a reconfig. Applies to matmul/conv compute kernels and their *_metal2 clones.
- cfg_reg_rmw_tensix<..., A_MASK | B_MASK ...>(union.val) where the union assigns a field whose MASK is absent (e.g. PCK_DEST_RD_CTRL_Read_int8). Applies to tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/cpack_common.h, cunpack_common.h and cmath_common.h.
- cfg_reg_rmw_tensix<ALU_ACC_CTRL_Zero_Flag_disabled_src_RMW> outside cmath_common.h with no _invalidate_src_zero_flag_state_() nearby. Applies to the tt-llk llk_lib/experimental and sfpu/experimental directories.
- if constexpr (INSTRUCTION_MODE == InstrModLoadStore::INT32) deciding signedness; 'input_dtype == DataType::INT32 || input_dtype == DataType::UINT32' before a '*_int32(' kernel in unary_op_utils.cpp; plain 'vInt x = sfpi::dst_reg[...]' without .mode<> in ckernel_sfpu_*.h.
- else { defines["FILL_TILE_WITH_FIRST_*"] = "..._bfloat16" } with no BFLOAT8_B/BFLOAT4_B case, in ttnn eltwise ternary/binary utils. Also reinterpret_cast<uint16_t*>(get_write_ptr(...)) mask/scaler generators whose CB format is the input dtype (moreh_* factories).
- static_cast<int32_t>(scalar)/static_cast<uint32_t> inside pack_scalar_runtime_arg*, and prim::binary_ng/prim::ternary scalar calls that do not go through invoke_binary_ng_impl's promotion check.
- tt::DataFormat::Float16_b hard-coded for intermediate CBs in normalization, pool and moreh factories that accept FLOAT32; reduce_tile<PoolType::MAX>/GMPOOL on a CB that may be Float32 (selection semantics lost to TF32).

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory and matmul compute kernels (plus *_metal2 and CCL-fused copies); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/{binary_ng,ternary,unary} host dispatch and program factories; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/{transpose,permute,untilize,concat} factory variants; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl reduction program factories; tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_{wormhole_b0,blackhole}/common/inc (pack/unpack/math configure vs reconfig); tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu integer kernels (reduce, comp, shift, typecast); tt_metal/jit_build genfiles/data_format unpack-dst resolution; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh and normalization factories (hard-coded or input-derived mask/intermediate formats)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes landed in one program factory or kernel while identical siblings stayed broken: sharded transpose variants, three more matmul factories that pin partials to UnpackToSrc, the boltz qkv-heads copy, and the matmul kernel the fixed conv epilogue was cloned from. Always enumerate every factory or kernel copy (interleaved/sharded/RM, Metal-2, Quasar, fused CCL) before calling a fix complete.
- A fix computed the new field value correctly but left the pre-existing read-modify-write mask alone, so the reconfig path still drops the bit. Configure and reconfig must program the same field set.
- Host-side guards worked around an LLK defect for one dtype (a bf16-only guard) and left the sibling formats on the same LLK branch (bfp8/bfp4) unguarded. Nearby comments then contradicted the guard.
- A fix pattern was propagated too far: 'treat UINT32 like INT32' was right for equality but wrong for ordered compare and min/max. Algebraic lowerings (min = -max(-x)) and pad sentinels (INT32_MIN+1) added their own integer-range bugs and needed later refixes.
- Widening an intermediate format (bf16 to fp32) doubled L1 use. One factory was re-fixed with adaptive sizing and the sibling 2D factory still overflows. Also, per-op opt-in to fp32_dest_acc_en leaves precision loss as the default for every op that has not opted in yet.
- Mode-bit caches added to fix a stale-flag bug set a contract (invalidate on raw write) that experimental kernels violate. A revert-then-reland cycle and blanket test skips hid regressions. Tests with same-dtype or bf16-only stimuli, or with values below 2^31, could not observe the bug.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/cpack_common.h:692 — the reconfig computes PCK_DEST_RD_CTRL_Read_int8, but the RMW mask omits Read_int8_MASK, so switching to or from an Int8/UInt8 pack with 16-bit Dest leaves the bit stale
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/common/inc/cpack_common.h:546 — same Read_int8 bit dropped from the BH reconfig RMW mask
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/compute/bmm_large_block_zm_fused_bias_activation.cpp:497 — the FUSE_BIAS epilogue reconfigures the packer to out_dfb_id but packs into untilize_mode_out_dfb_id (partials when untilize_out). The reconfig at :584 is skipped under FUSE_BIAS. This is the conv bug that was fixed, left in the matmul original.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/ternary/device/ternary_program_factory.cpp:1343 — fp32_dest_acc_en comes from the output format only, while :1350+ marks FLOAT32 predicate/value CBs UnpackToDestFp32. With an fp32 input and a bf16 output, 32-bit data is unpacked into a 16-bit Dest.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/device/transpose_wh_sharded_program_factory.cpp:48 — fp32_dest_acc_en is set for Float32 only, but transpose_tile takes the unpack-to-dest and Dest-transpose path for Int32/UInt32 (the interleaved factory at :55 was widened)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/device/transpose_wh_sharded_rm_program_factory.cpp:81 — same Float32-only Dest-width condition in front of tilize plus transpose_tile
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_optimized_program_factory.cpp:550 — Float32 interm0 under fp32_dest_acc_en is pinned to UnpackToSrc, so the cross-K-block partials reload truncates to TF32 (same at matmul_multicore_reuse_mcast_dram_sharded_program_factory.cpp:1016 and matmul_multicore_reuse_batched_hs_dram_sharded_program_factory.cpp:508)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/llama_all_gather_matmul_async/device/llama_1d_mm_fusion.cpp:479 — the gathered matmul kernel with Float32 interm0 (:128) sets no unpack_to_dest_mode, while the equivalent 1D gathered factory marks interm0 UnpackToDestFp32
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary/common/unary_op_utils.cpp:494 — UNARY_GT/LT/GE/LE route UINT32 to the signed *_int32 compare kernel, so uint32 values of 2^31 or more compare as negative
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/ternary/device/ternary_op_utils.cpp:475 — the dtype define table sends BFLOAT8_B/BFLOAT4_B to the fill_tile_with_first_*_bfloat16 helpers (also :498, :521). This is reachable from where() TTT with a row/col/scalar broadcast on block-float inputs.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh/moreh_softmax/device/softmax_w_small/softmax_w_small.cpp:95 — the MASK CB uses the input format and tile size, so bfp8 input makes the uint16 mask writer overflow a 1088-byte entry (same at softmax_w_large.cpp:99, softmax_h_small.cpp:91, softmax_h_large.cpp:91)
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/llk_lib/experimental/llk_math_reduce_custom.h:323 — a raw set/clear of the src zero-flag without invalidating the math-side tracker, so a later datacopy init can skip its write and flush UInt16 datums (BH twin at tt_llk_blackhole/.../llk_math_reduce_custom.h:393)


## `tt-arch-divergence`: Code ported or shared across WH/BH/Quasar keeps the old arch's assumptions

Code written for one arch, or copied from it (Grayskull to WH, WH to BH, WH/BH to Quasar), keeps that arch's hardware facts as constants, literals or unstated preconditions. The usual ones are DRAM/NoC alignment (32 B on WH, 64 B on BH/Quasar, L1 16 B), PCIe/DRAM endpoint coordinates and which NoC they belong to, register bit-field layouts, resource counts, thread/slot enums, core-type macro sets, and instruction semantics such as a Dest clear's region and bank select, a mode register's reset default, sign-magnitude versus two's-complement compares, errata-affected config writes, and posted versus CPU-blocking waits. The code keeps compiling and passing on the original arch, then silently corrupts data, hangs, faults or fails to build on the new one. The gap usually hides behind arch-gated test skips, allow-lists that name one arch (`arch != WH_B0`, `is_blackhole` without Quasar), arch-dispatching helpers whose result is unwrapped as one variant alternative, or per-arch wrappers that silently drop a parameter or argument the sibling arch honours. A related shape: a host-side gate and the kernel that relies on it compute the same arch property differently (dst vs src buffer alignment, element size vs a hard-coded bf16 line). They then disagree on exactly the arch/dtype/memory mix nobody tests.

**Checks**

- Sub-page NoC transfers (face rows, sticks, index elements, 16*elem_size, SUBTILE_LINE_BYTES): the source and destination must agree modulo the alignment of the buffer being READ FROM, taken from the HAL for that memory type (DRAM 64 B on BH/Quasar), or the transfer must be staged through an aligned scratch buffer. Flag literal 32/16 'minimum read size' constants and gates computed from the other endpoint's buffer.alignment().
- When a host factory allocates a scratch/staging buffer and the kernel decides to use it, both must evaluate the same expression (same alignment source, same element size or line size). Also check that the op's dtype validation does not admit dtypes the kernel's hard-coded byte geometry (bf16 face or line sizes) cannot handle.
- CB page stride must equal the DRAM page stride (tile size rounded up to the arch's DRAM alignment) on the read AND write sides, for every dtype and tile shape. Build a tile-size x dtype x arch table: bfp8/bfp4 tiny tiles (for example 544 B) are 32 B-aligned but not 64 B-aligned.
- Grep for arch predicates that stand in for a hardware property (is_blackhole, arch == WORMHOLE_B0, arch != X). Every arch that shares the property, Quasar especially, must take the same branch, and mirrored or forked copies of an op (for example an experimental/quasar tree) must carry the same predicate as mainline. A ternary arch ? X : Y feeding a stride runtime arg must give X the same padding and alignment invariant as Y.
- Diff each per-arch llk_api wrapper and LLK init against its sibling arch. An argument one arch forwards (dst format, face count, num_faces) that the other omits is a finding. So is a template parameter that is [[maybe_unused]] on one arch but not static_asserted to its default, and so is a hand-copied instruction sequence in the metal tier that duplicates the arch's own LLK.
- For every instruction whose operand meaning depends on a mode or arch (Dest clear/ZEROACC, auto-bank select, fp32 flags, compare format fields, LUT source registers): find where the mode is programmed on each arch and check that every kernel entry path reaches that write first. Check that callers encode indices in that mode's units, and bound (Dest row offset + index) so it cannot cross a bank or half-Dest boundary at the last tile, in 16-bit and 32-bit Dest.
- Hardware register reads that pack a counter into a field: the field must be masked or shifted before any compare or arithmetic. Flag raw volatile dereferences or NOC_STREAM_READ_REG that bypass the arch-aware masking accessor, since upper bits that read as zero on WH need not on BH.
- #if chains over core-type macros (COMPILE_FOR_ERISC, COMPILE_FOR_IDLE_ERISC, DRISC, Quasar DM) in bounds or sanitizer code must name every flavour the build emits. A missing idle-eth leg falls through to the worker MEM_L1_SIZE bound. Diff sibling validators in the same file for inconsistent macro sets.
- std::get<Gen1Alternative> (or any unchecked variant cast) applied to a value from an arch-dispatching factory (to_compute_hardware_config(arch, ...)) will throw on the other generation. It needs a visitor/accessor or a holds_alternative guard.
- Treat arch-gated test skips ('mismatch on BH'), host-side arch-conditional rewrites (transpose-op-transpose) and 'workaround no longer needed' cleanups as unresolved device bugs. Find the hidden invariant and confirm the real fix landed and was validated on EVERY arch the kernel runs on.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- ttnn/cpp/**/kernels, ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel/dataflow: `#define NOC_MINIMUM_READ_SIZE|MINIMUM_READ_SIZE\s*=?\s*\(?32`, and noc_async_read/noc.async_read of `SUBTILE_LINE_BYTES|FACE_WIDTH \* .*element_size|16 \* ELEMENT_SIZE` into a CB offset that advances by the same amount
- ttnn program factories: `dst_buffer->alignment()` or output-buffer alignment used to gate a READER-side misalignment/staging path; `alignment > FACE_WIDTH \* .*element_size()` on the host paired with a kernel `constexpr .*MISALIGNED = ALIGNMENT > 32`
- ttnn factories: `\bis_blackhole\b` near get_alignment/align( with no is_quasar term, and `is_blackhole\) \?` or `\? shard_width :` feeding a padded/stride runtime arg; compare experimental/quasar/* forks against their mainline twins
- ttnn/cpp/**/device/*factory*.cpp: `std::get<ComputeGen1Config>(` or `std::get<DataMovementGen1Config>(` on a value from `to_compute_hardware_config(`
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole|tt_llk_quasar: `TT_ZEROACC\(p_zeroacc::CLR_16` with an index from dst_index/16, tile<<2|<<3 or tile_start+face_num while a Dest target offset also advances; `zeroacc_absolute_tile_mode` to find the active mode
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/debug/sanitize.h and other bounds code: `#if defined\(COMPILE_FOR_ERISC\)` with no `COMPILE_FOR_IDLE_ERISC` leg, followed by an `#else` that uses MEM_L1_SIZE
- device/fabric/dispatch code: `NOC_STREAM_READ_REG\(.*SPACE_AVAILABLE` or `\*reinterpret_cast<volatile.*get_stream_reg_read_addr` compared with ==/!= and no `& ((1 << .*_WIDTH) - 1)` mask
- per-arch llk_api dirs (tt_metal/hw/ckernels/{wormhole_b0,blackhole,quasar}/metal/llk_api): diff the argument lists passed to `_llk_.*_init_`, and diff the directory listings for headers present on one arch that a compute-API header includes with no ARCH_* guard
- tt_metal/impl/host_api, tt_metal/llrt: write_core/read_core/write_dram_vec/read_dram_vec called with `host_buffer.size()` or `page_size()` and no tt::align/get_alignment_requirements; `supports_dma_operations` deciding by size alone
- Blackhole LLK: `REG2FLOP` in live code (use WRCFG with a stall), and CFGSHIFTMASK/replay bodies that read a non-banked scratch config register seeded by a RISC store at init

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (transpose, reshape_on_device, sharded I2S/S2I) and experimental/transformer nlp_* decode readers: sub-tile NoC reads under BH 64 B DRAM alignment; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh (getitem, nll_loss, common read helpers): hard-coded 32 B read granule; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/*: forked factories that miss mainline arch fixes, and Gen1-variant access; tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole and tt_llk_quasar llk_lib (eltwise_binary dest-reuse, experimental custom_mm/sdpa): Dest clear bank semantics and mode registers ported from WH; tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api (+ llk_sfpu): per-arch wrapper signature/argument drift, missing per-arch ports, int32 compare semantics; tt_metal/hw/inc/internal (debug/sanitize.h, tt-2xx NoC v3, dataflow_buffer, stream_interface) and api/debug/dprint_tensix.h: core-type macro sets, resource counts, register field widths; tt_metal/impl/host_api + tt_metal/llrt (tt_cluster, firmware initializer): size/alignment transport preconditions and per-NoC endpoint tables; matmul dataflow readers/writers and factories: tiny-tile CB stride vs DRAM page stride

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- An arch-aware fix is often applied to only the one call site in the report, while sibling kernels and helpers in the same family keep the hard-coded constant: alignment fixed in read_line but not getitem or get_noc_offset, and a DMA-size guard added to four callers instead of the size-routing chokepoint. Fix at the chokepoint, or sweep every user of the constant.
- The host gate and the kernel gate for a workaround were written from different inputs (destination vs source buffer alignment, element size vs a hard-coded bf16 line size), so the fix is inactive or mismatched for mixed memory configs and non-bf16 dtypes. Verify both gates evaluate the same expression.
- Fixes that go through a revert and re-land often re-land with a smaller or different bug (raw width passed as the padded stride, with the reader and writer halves fixed months apart), and the matching op (I2S vs S2I, read vs write side) gets corrected much later. After a re-land, check the counterpart op.
- Workarounds (host transpose, arch-gated tile paths, withdrawn optimisations, test skips, silently ignored parameters) often stand in for the real device fix. When they are later removed on the belief the root cause is fixed, the fix may exist on only one arch. Validate on every arch and at the edge shape (last Dest tile, 16-bit Dest) before removing a workaround.
- A fix to one hardware-semantics quirk hides the next one in the same instruction: Dest-clear indexing, then the mode register, then bank-crossing on the last face. A fix may also land in one LLK file and list the other users of the instruction as follow-ups that never happen. Grep every user of the instruction, not just the failing op.
- Guards added as debug-only asserts (LLK_ASSERT, emule-only sanitizers) or as compile-time blocks leave production builds unprotected, and leave the feature (for example the DM-to-TRISC handoff on Quasar) disabled rather than fixed. Record what is still unsupported and make sure it fails loudly.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/device/transpose_hc_tiled_program_factory.cpp:59 — the misaligned-read gate uses dst_buffer->alignment(), but the unaligned ops are reads from the INPUT. With a DRAM input and an L1 output on BH, the staging path is skipped.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/reshape_on_device/device/reshape_op.cpp:32 — admits FLOAT32 for the TILE path. The host scratch gate (reshape_tile_program_factory.cpp:70) uses element_size, while the kernel hard-codes bf16 32 B lines, so the two gates disagree for fp32.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh/moreh_getitem/device/moreh_getitem_tilized_kernels/reader_moreh_getitem_tilize.cpp:113 — local NOC_MINIMUM_READ_SIZE 32. DRAM offsets are rounded to 32 B where BH needs 64 B, and the tests are skipped on BH.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh/moreh_getitem/device/moreh_getitem_tilized_kernels/reader_moreh_getitem_tilize_w.cpp:119 — the same hard-coded 32 B DRAM read granule and rounding (reads at lines 248-305).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel/dataflow/moreh_common.hpp:659 — get_noc_offset rounds DRAM offsets to the hard-coded 32 B NOC_MINIMUM_READ_SIZE (line 20) instead of the HAL DRAM read alignment.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/interleaved_to_sharded/device/interleaved_to_sharded_program_factory.cpp:443 — the L1-alignment branch tests is_blackhole only. Quasar shares BH's 64 B DRAM / 16 B L1 alignment but takes the WH 'aligned = true' branch.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/debug/sanitize.h:659 — debug_sanitize_l1_access checks COMPILE_FOR_ERISC only. Idle-eth builds define only COMPILE_FOR_IDLE_ERISC, so they fall back to the worker MEM_L1_SIZE bound.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/debug/dprint_tensix.h:378 — the Fp32_enabled override forces Float32 before the switch. Int32/UInt32 Dest (which runs with 32-bit Dest) is decoded as Float32, and the Int32 case is unreachable in that mode.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax/device/softmax_program_factory_attention_optimized_sharded.cpp:345 — std::get<ComputeGen1Config> on the arch-dispatched compute_hw would throw on Quasar. It is one of about 22 such sites in ttnn/cpp (also softmax_program_factory_attention_optimized.cpp:463 and the general_* softmax factories).
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_quasar/llk_lib/llk_math_eltwise_binary.h:338 — CLR_16 ZEROACC with tile_start+face_num in the ELWMUL dest-reuse loop, the same shape as the BH bank-crossing defect. Nothing handles the last face of a bank on Quasar.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_sender_writer_padding.cpp:699 — the output writer advances by the unpadded get_tile_size stride (line 214). A bfp8/bfp4 tiny-tile output to interleaved DRAM on BH is not 64 B-aligned.
- tt_metal/llrt/tt_cluster.cpp:869 — write_core/read_core pick DMA by size alone and never enforce DMA's multiple-of-4 size rule. The detail::WriteToDeviceL1/ReadFromDeviceL1 span overloads (tt_metal/impl/host_api/tt_metal.cpp ~279/320) pass host_buffer.size() straight through on WH.


## `stale-state`: Persistent state outlives its op, kernel, or init cycle

State that lasts longer than the unit of work that set it gets read by a later unit as if it were fresh. On the device side this state is hardware registers or command-buffer fields: NoC VC, transaction-ID tag, byte-enable, packer ReLU, counters, the zero-flag bit, debug-mode bits, SFPU constant LREGs, replay slots, and Dest contents. Firmware resets these only at boot or on a mode change, so whatever one kernel leaves behind reaches the next kernel. On the host side it is singletons, file-scope containers, lazy getters, cached snapshots and program-cache entries. Teardown does not clear them, or a cache hit skips the construction that would have refreshed them. Four mechanisms cause it. (1) A set/init with no paired restore or uninit. (2) An incremental update path (with_state, reconfig, a partial-batch write, a skip-if-equal early-out) that refreshes only some of the dependent fields, or compares only a subset of the inputs. (3) The reset is done by an agent that might not run this time (an idle RISC, a queue other than 0, a core that does not own the resource). (4) A software shadow or cache of hardware or device state that a raw write, a conditional device update or an async lag makes wrong. Fixes are often correct only for the one site that was reported, or they are protected only by a debug-only assert.

**Checks**

- For every API or kernel that writes a register, command-buffer field or mode bit that survives kernel exit, confirm that one of two things holds: firmware resets it on every launch, or every writer restores the default on every exit path. Check release builds, not only debug-assert builds. Grep all writers of that field across ttnn, model and test kernels, not only the one that was reported.
- For each stateful set_state/with_state or set_state-once-then-send-many pattern, list every field set_state seeded and what each one is derived from: address low bits give the byte-enable, the chunk count gives the payload size, and so on. Then check that each with_state update mask refreshes the derived fields, including on the final remainder iteration. Also check that nothing else that shares the command buffer runs between set_state and with_state: default inline writes, semaphore incs, trid writes.
- For every reconfig or reconfig(old,new) or skip-if-equal early-out, diff the fields the full hw_configure writes against the fields the reconfig rewrites. Flag early-outs that compare only a subset of the inputs (for example, dst format only, but not src format or geometry). Flag cached values that are not recomputed for the new geometry. Flag hard-coded 'old' operands that are not the CB configured last on every path.
- Wherever a software cache mirrors hardware or device state (a zero-flag tracker, a launch-message wptr, replay-recorded flags, host copies of the token or position), check two things. Every raw write must go through the setter or invalidate the cache. Every mirror update must be gated by the same condition that decides whether the device-side update is emitted. The cache key must also cover every value the cached body captures.
- For each init/teardown or open/close pair, list every member, static, file-scope container and cached snapshot that init fills in. Confirm teardown clears each one, or that init always rebuilds it. Confirm teardown decides what to undo from state recorded at init time, not from a mutable global config. Flag reset() helpers that have no callers and containers that only ever get inserted into.
- On teardown, destructor and disable paths, flag any call to a lazy get-or-create accessor (instance(), get_control_plane()). Also flag internal re-calls of a global set_config with defaulted trailing parameters: they widen or reset state that the caller chose.
- When per-agent state (per RISC, per CQ, per core, per slot) is reset by that agent itself, ask what happens if the agent is idle or skipped this time. Require the reset to come from an agent that always runs, and to cover every instance (all CQs, all RISCs, every core in the kernel-group range, even cores that do not own the resource).
- For caches that memoize construction (the program cache, the JIT cache, trace replay), list all side-channel state that the uncached path writes and that dispatch or tooling later reads: routing tables, zone maps, runtime args. That state must be part of the cached record. Also check that dry-run or capture modes do not mutate cached entries on the cache-hit path.
- In compute kernels, follow each global packer or unpacker mode (ReLU, L1 accumulation, the reduce mask, tileize_mode) through pack-thread program order, including across loop back-edges. Every pack issued while the mode is active must want that mode, including packs of intermediate tilize or transpose output.
- In kernels with nested loops, flag running index or counter variables that are declared outside the outer loop and advanced inside the inner loop without a per-iteration reset. For batching writers, flag a missing post-loop flush of a partial batch.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep 'set_async_read_state<NocOptions::CUSTOM_VC\|read_one_packet_set_state<true>' under ttnn/cpp/**/kernels and model kernels: each needs a restore to vc=1 before exit (the DRAM-sharded in1 reader has the idiom)
- grep 'noc_async_write_set_trid(\|_with_trid(' in kernels with no final set_trid(0); also firmware main() entry points that are missing the packet-tag clear (every RISC, eth variant and arch)
- grep 'cfg_reg_rmw_tensix<ALU_ACC_CTRL_Zero_Flag_disabled_src' outside the math-thread setter in cmath_common.h with no _invalidate_src_zero_flag_state_() nearby, in tt-llk llk_lib/experimental and sfpu/experimental
- grep -E 'SFPCONFIG\([^;]*(, *11 *,|LREG11)' in tt-llk sfpu: LREG11 is the -1.0 constant and is seeded only at boot, so it must be reloaded before return
- In llk_api llk_pack_common_api.h and llk_unpack reconfig overloads: 'if (pack_dst_format[old] != pack_dst_format[new] ...)' early-outs that ignore src format and geometry
- *_with_state<...UpdateMask::DstAddrs> or with_state<update_addr_lo=true> calls where chunk count, payload size or byte-enable was seeded once by set_state; packetizing loops that send on 'chunk_count == N' with no post-loop flush (ttnn ccl kernels)
- Teardown, close and destructor bodies that call get_*() or instance() accessors whose definition does 'if (!member_) initialize...()'; set_fabric_config(...) re-calls with fewer arguments than the full signature (tt_metal/impl/context, device_manager)
- pack_relu_config(ReluConfig::zero()) followed, before the next ::none(), by tilize_in / transpose_tile_block / pack_tile to a non-output CB (conv and matmul compute kernels)
- Python: module-level singletons (profiler = Profiler(), process-global maps) read by parametrized tests with no per-test clear; reset_*()/reset_params() called with no slots argument inside a prefill path (models/common/sampling, the model generators)
- PACK(TTI_SETADCXX(p_setadc::PAC, ...)) or raw packer-counter writes in model and test compute kernels that relied on an old default of the shared counter

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/tt-llk (pack/unpack reconfig, zero-flag and debug-mode bits, SFPU constants and replay flags, especially the experimental/ dirs); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal noc_nonblocking_api* and dataflow_api (stateful command-buffer APIs, per arch and API version); ttnn/cpp/**/kernels/dataflow and ccl kernels (custom VC, trid, reused fabric packet headers); tt_metal/impl/context + impl/device + impl/dispatch (MetalEnv/MetalContext init and teardown, fabric config, dispatch topology); tt_metal/fabric (router L1 counters, clear lists, stateful EDM sends); tt_metal/tools/profiler + jit_build (per-RISC buffers, zone maps built only on compile); models/common/sampling and models/**/generator.py (per-slot state under partial prefill and async decode); tests/**/fixtures and test kernels (state leaked between tests in one process)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- A fix usually lands at the one reported kernel or entry point. Siblings with the same shape stay: other kernels that set a custom VC, the single-operand reconfig when only the combined one was fixed, idle_erisc after brisc, and other arches or API versions such as Quasar V1. Always sweep every writer of the same field.
- The fix put ownership on a thread that does not consume the state, or the fix only ever set the flag and never cleared it. It was reverted for trading one leak for another. The lasting fix moved ownership to the consuming thread and kept a cache there that re-derives the value in both directions.
- The invariant was enforced only by LLK_ASSERT or a watcher assert, both compiled out of production builds. Treat that as no guard.
- A workaround scrubbed the symptom (a test-harness Dest scrub, manual clear() in some tests, a reverted stateful path because the clobberer could not be found, caller-side 'clear the cache before bulk free'). The producing LLK or API stayed unchanged. Treat a workaround verdict as a sign that live siblings exist.
- Narrowing a heuristic added a fallback that brought back the original bug for a subset of cases: row-sharded models fall back to a stale host token. Guards also ran after the operation they were meant to protect, with the gather before the shape check.
- Changing a shared default (for example, the packer counter default) broke hidden dependents in model and test kernels that relied on the old value. Grep raw register writes outside the owning layer when a default moves.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/common/inc/sfpu/experimental/ckernel_sfpu_sinkhorn.h:157 — SFPCONFIG overwrites the -1.0 constant in LREG11 with a parity mask. Nothing restores it (no 0xBF80 reload in the file), and firmware seeds it only at boot, so a later SFPU op that uses LCONST_neg1 on the core reads the mask.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/prefetcher/prefetcher/device/kernels/reader_dram.cpp:49 — read_one_packet_set_state<true> with a runtime VC writes it into the command buffer's control register. There is no restore to the default VC before exit, and firmware re-initializes that register only when the NoC mode changes.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_ring_all_gather.cpp:234 — set_async_read_state<CUSTOM_VC> with a per-bank VC and no restore, unlike the idiom in reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_sender_dram_sharded.cpp:255.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_sender_writer_padding.cpp:372 — CUSTOM_VC set_state on the DRAM-sharded paths (also :564) with no restore of the default VC.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_pack_common_api.h:228 — the two-operand pack reconfig skips entirely when the dst formats match. It ignores src format and geometry (num_faces, face_r_dim, partial face), so packer geometry goes stale. The BH twin is at blackhole/.../llk_pack_common_api.h:203.
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/common/inc/sfpu/experimental/ckernel_sfpu_topk_xl.h:1282 — raw writes of the zero-flag bit (1, then 0 at :1292) with no _invalidate_src_zero_flag_state_(), which cmath_common.h requires. The tracker can then disagree with the hardware, and a later skip-if-equal setter leaves UInt16 flushing wrong.
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/llk_lib/experimental/llk_unpack_AB_reduce_custom.h:76 — the unpack thread does a raw write of the math-owned zero-flag bit and bypasses the math-side cache. The BH copy has the same line.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/all_gather_async/device/kernels/broadcast_rm_writer.cpp:126 — the scatter writer sends only when chunk_count == num_out_pages_per_packet, and nothing flushes after the page loop. Tail pages of a range that is not packet-aligned are written locally but never reach remote devices.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/compute/bmm_large_block_zm_fused_bias_activation.cpp:310 — with PACK_RELU and no FUSE_BIAS, the last K block enables ReLU before the in0_transpose_tile path runs transpose_tile_block, which packs in0 tiles. Negative in0 values get zeroed before the matmul uses them.
- models/common/sampling/generator.py:213 — apply_prefill_state calls reset_prompt_tokens(prompt_tokens) and then reset_output_state() (:214) with no slot scoping. A partial prefill therefore wipes the penalty masks and counts of rows that are still decoding. scatter_sampling_params_to_slots (~:770) also broadcasts a filler value to unoccupied slots.
- tt_metal/impl/context/metal_env.cpp:364 — teardown_fabric_config calls the lazily-constructing get_control_plane() (definition around :414) just to clear it. If the control plane is null, teardown builds a full control plane. The same file at :536/:549 re-calls set_fabric_config with defaulted tensix/udm/router parameters.
- tt_metal/fabric/erisc_datamover_builder.cpp:301 — router_buffer_clear_size_words is set to a byte count (align(4*N, field_size)), but fabric_init.cpp:67 uses it as a uint32 element count. Each clear writes 4x the intended size into neighbouring router L1 fields.


## `numeric-formula`: Wrong math in SFPU approximations, host converters and composites

Hand-written numerics tend to be right only on part of the input domain, and nothing checks the rest. Four ways this shows up: (1) An approximation or iteration is valid only on a sub-range but gets applied everywhere. Examples are a Newton seed outside the convergence basin for small x, a rational or polynomial fit used outside its fitted interval, a half-range helper given the signed input, magic-reciprocal divisors exact only below about 2^31, and saturation thresholds derived for bf16 but reused on the fp32-Dest path. (2) Cancellation or overflow is built into the formula. Examples are k*ln2 + poly(m) just below 1, 1+erf(x) when erf saturates, E[x^2]-E[x]^2, v - q*s once one ULP exceeds s, and power-series matrix inverses whose intermediates are far larger than the result. (3) Constants and bit tricks are subtly wrong: ln2 rounded to fp16 (0.692871) in fp32 paths, coefficients that disagree with the derivation next to them, an unsigned '>>31' that gives a 0/1 mask instead of all-ones, add-half-then-truncate used as round-to-nearest-even, and alignment shifts that drop the sticky bits. (4) Init and composition contracts drift. A consumer's init copies a shared helper's programmable constants by hand, or overwrites them, or is keyed on a different approx/fp32 flag than the compute body. Some correction steps and guards never help: a repair step that moves the value away from the target (s - v), a guard comparing a truncated value with its source, or a gradient mask with the wrong sign or strictness. The tests usually miss all of this. They sample [0,1] or narrow ranges, use one seed for every argument, score with a PCC dominated by an identity diagonal, or use goldens edited to match the device.

**Checks**

- Domain coverage: for every approximation (polynomial/rational/LUT fit, Newton or Goldschmidt refinement, magic-number divide), find the interval it is valid on (from its comment, fit table or convergence basin) and compare it with the op's documented domain and every value the platform can produce (harvested core/bank counts, page ids, both Dest widths). Each region outside the fit needs a clamp, an asymptotic branch or a special-value path for +/-0, subnormals, +/-inf and NaN. Handle fixed points before the loop.
- Precision-mode thresholds: in kernels templated on is_fp32_dest_acc_en, check that saturation constants ('result = 1.0' above T, 0 below T) and bf16 region boundaries were re-derived for fp32 output. Flag kernels where the fp32 flag changes only the final rounding or the iteration count.
- Cancellation scan: look for 1 +/- f(x) where f saturates (erf, tanh, sigmoid), k*C + poly(m) near a zero, E[x^2]-E[x]^2 (a relu/clamp on a variance is the tell), v - trunc(v/s)*s at large |v|, and explicit high powers in series inverses. Bound each intermediate over the real input range against the result's magnitude and the format's mantissa.
- Constant audit: recompute every hand-coded coefficient from its derivation comment and the true math value. Flag fp16/bf16-rounded constants (ln2 = 0.692871, log2e) in fp32-producing paths. Then grep every copy of the same constant or body (no_init variants, fused/inlined copies, binary pow/xlogy/lgamma/digamma, WH/BH/Quasar) and confirm they were all updated together.
- Init/constant ownership: for every helper that reads programmable constant registers (reciprocal, exp, log, sigmoid), list its callers. Each caller's init must call the helper's own init under the same template flags the compute uses. It must not write literals into vConstFloatPrgm*/vConstIntPrgm*, and it must not write those registers after calling the helper init. Compare which registers each arch's helper init programs, because on one arch it may use all three.
- Rounding and bit tricks: add-half-then-shift is round-half-up, not RNE. Any bits dropped before the round decision (alignment to a shared or block exponent) must be ORed into a sticky bit. A 'mask = x >> 31' borrow trick needs a signed operand. Test ties and just-above-tie inputs against an exact model, and diff against a sibling converter that is known to be correct.
- Correction steps and guards: each repair step must move the value toward the target interval, and must cover both signs if it runs after copysign. Every overshoot guard must be able to fire; a truncated quotient compared with its untruncated source never can. When a guard is fixed in one near-copy (fmod/remainder, unary/binary, WH/BH), diff the others.
- Symmetry and branch conditions: half-range helpers extended by odd/even symmetry must receive |x|, and the reflection must match the function (-f, 2-f, 1+erf). Composite gradients must match the analytic derivative term by term (strict vs non-strict inequalities, integer vs fractional exponent sub-domains, mask sign).
- Distributed/chunked reuse: when a ring, flash or chunked wrapper reuses a single-block kernel, list the global statistics that kernel derives from its inputs (normalizer, rowsum(dO*O)) and confirm the caller passes global values, not per-chunk ones.
- PRNG consumers: grep for direct hardware-PRNG reads, not only the wrapped API. Check lane decorrelation or mixing, the lock-up seed guard, distinct non-adjacent per-core seeds or stream ids, and that each caller's scale matches the primitive's half-open vs inclusive range contract.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rn '0\.692871' tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/sfpu tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu — fp16-rounded ln2 in fp32 log/pow bodies
- grep -n 'vConstFloatPrgm[012] *=' in ckernel_sfpu_*.h, then check whether the file calls sfpu_reciprocal*/_sfpu_sigmoid_/log bodies with no matching helper init, or writes these registers after the helper init (WH vs BH copies diverge)
- v_if(x >= <c>f) { result = 1.0f; } / 'vFloat result = 0.0f;' followed only by range v_ifs, in SFPU functions templated on is_fp32_dest_acc_en; this shape also drops NaN
- v_if(v >= s) { v = s - v; } and v_if(quotient > v * recip_val) in ckernel_sfpu_*fmod*.h / *remainder*.h; repair loops placed after sfpi::copysgn
- Newton updates 'y * (1.5f - 0.5f * x * y * y)' or 'y * (2 - x*y)' seeded from a constant or 1/x; zero tests written as 'x_bits != 0', which miss -0.0
- Host converters: 'mantissa >> exp_diff' before a round-mask/tie test with no '|= (dropped != 0)' sticky OR, or 'mantissa += 1 << (SHIFT-1)' then '>> SHIFT' (tt_metal/impl/data_format)
- Magic reciprocals '(uint64_t)n * 0x[0-9A-F]{8} >> 32' and '(unsigned)(...) >> 31' masks in tt_metal/hw/inc; test them exhaustively against '/' for every divisor the platforms can produce
- Composite backward ops (ttnn/.../unary_backward, binary_backward): where(lez|ltz|gez|gtz(input), inf|nan, ...) masks, and goldens in ttnn/ttnn/operations/*_backward.py that masked_fill_/clamp the torch gradient
- Variance as E[x^2]-E[x]^2: subtract(mean_sq, pow(mean,2)) or combining gathered partial stats in the layernorm post-allgather kernels; FP32 operands passed through FPU/SrcA (copy_tile or mul_tiles_bcast_scalar without UnpackToDest) before a precision-critical subtraction
- TTI_SFPMOV(0, 9, ...) raw PRNG reads outside ckernel_sfpu_rand.h; CoreRuntimeArgs{seed} identical across cores; rand_tile_init(seed + core_index); scale = to - from passed to rand_tile

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/hw/ckernels/{wormhole_b0,blackhole,quasar}/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu (log, recip/rsqrt/sqrt, gelu, digamma/lgamma, fmod/remainder, binary pow/xlogy, erf/erfc, tanh LUT, rand/dropout); tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_*/common/inc/sfpu (shared log/recip bodies and their no_init variants); tt_metal/impl/data_format (host float to block-float/MX converters); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/mod_div_lib.h (constant-divisor division for bank addressing); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary_backward and binary_backward, plus their Python goldens; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction and normalization (std/var, Welford, layernorm post-allgather); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental (kda chunk recurrence, dropout) and tt-train compute kernels (layernorm_bw, ring attention); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/sliding_window and pool (dilation-aware padding, alignment helpers)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- The fix lands in one of several near-copies and the rest keep the defect. Examples: the ln2/polynomial fix reached the init-constant log body but not _no_init_ or binary pow; the fmod guard fix did not reach remainder; BH inits still hardcode the reciprocal constant where the WH copies call the helper init; the Quasar log kept the old scheme. Always grep every sibling body, fused copy and arch port.
- A composition rewrite shipped with an init that did not match the helpers' constant or flag contract. It took two more fixes: first the init was keyed on an approx flag the compute ignored, then the helper constants were overwritten after the helper init. The first fix stayed hidden because the kernel did not even compile.
- A fix repaired one of two independent defects. Examples: the loop direction was fixed but fp32 cancellation for large |x| stayed; RNE ties were fixed but the sticky bit across the alignment shift was not; the Welford rewrite fixed the formula but left TF32 truncation through SrcA/FPU pre-scaling. After a fix, enumerate every failure mode again from first principles.
- Opt-in back doors (legacy_compat defaulting to true) and a new seed that only moved the failure elsewhere (rsqrt seeded with 1.0 converges too slowly at the extremes) left users on the bad path. Check the default and the worst-case end of the domain, not just the reported input.
- Tests got weaker instead of the code getting fixed. A golden was edited to inject inf for negative input so the pow_bw gradient bug passed. Gate ranges are still pinned tiny after a series-inverse fix. PCC is dominated by an identity diagonal. One seed serves every argument. Check that the test would have caught the original bug.
- A shared-primitive fix did not reach direct consumers of the same hardware source. The rand_tile PRNG mixing/stream-id fix left dropout reading raw PRNG bits with identical per-core seeds.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_log.h:77 — _calculate_log_body_no_init_ still uses the fp16-rounded ln2 0.692871f. It is used by lgamma and digamma. The same line is in tt_llk_blackhole at :77 and in quasar metal ckernel_sfpu_log.h:71.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_binary.h:47 — binary POW builds log(base) with vConstLn2 = 0.692871f and the old cubic, so the error is exponentiated. The BH copy at :47 is identical.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_remainder.h:186 — the dead overshoot guard 'quotient > v * recip_val' (quotient is v*recip truncated) is still here. The equivalent fmod fix was never ported. The BH copy is at :110.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_fmod.h:59 — the repair loop runs after copysgn and tests only v >= s, so negative residues are never reduced. v - q*s cancels in fp32 for large |x|. The remainder copy at :201 and the BH copies have the same shape.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_gelu.h:425 — the GELU derivative applies bf16-derived saturation (1.0 for x >= 3.1719, 0 below -13.375) in fp32 Dest mode as well. The true GELU'(3.17) is about 1.0075. The 0-default has no NaN path. The BH copy at :425 is identical.
- tt_metal/impl/data_format/blockfloat_common.cpp:296 — 'mantissa >> exp_diff' drops bits before the RNE decision with no sticky OR, so values just above a tie round down for non-max elements in every bfp format.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary_backward/unary_backward.cpp:282 — pow_bw returns +inf for every input <= 0 whatever the exponent, although integer exponents have finite gradients. The golden at ttnn/ttnn/operations/unary_backward.py:886 was edited to match, so tests cannot catch it.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/blackhole/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_lgamma.h:168 — the init hardcodes vConstFloatPrgm0 = 2.0f for the reciprocal instead of calling the reciprocal init; the WH copy calls recip_init. This is latent drift. ckernel_sfpu_binary_remainder.h:314 on BH has the same pattern.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_dropout.h:37 — dropout reads raw hardware PRNG bits with no lane mixing, and init_prng_seed at :57 has no guard against the lock-up seed. BH is at :41/:61.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/dropout/device/dropout_program_factory.cpp:217 — every core gets the identical seed (also at :220), so each core replays the same mask stream.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/mod_div_lib.h:11 — the magic-reciprocal fast paths (d=7,28,56,63,70,...) are inexact above about 1.4e9-3.4e9 and nothing asserts the range. This is latent for realistic page ids.
- models/experimental/diffusion_gemma/tt/kernels/compute_trace_seeded_uniform.cpp:35 — it seeds rand_tile_init(base + offset + core_index) with no stream id, and scale = to - from at :31 ignores rand_tile's inclusive upper bound.


## `arg-binding`: Arguments silently bound to the wrong slot, default, or mode

An argument the caller meant never reaches the parameter, register, table index or source slot it was meant for, and the build gives no diagnostic. There are four recurring mechanisms. (1) A defaulted parameter swallows an omission: CB/operand ids defaulted to 0 or 16, fp32-dest or approx flags defaulted to false, activation params defaulted to 0, rounding modes defaulting to stochastic. The caller leaves the argument out and the hardware gets configured from CB 0 or run in the wrong mode. (2) Positional shift between same-typed integer parameters: a noc id lands in an offset slot, an exponent lands in vector_mode, a Set_Dvalid slot gets an Unpack_Pop constant, a class index and a type index get swapped, or a new parameter inserted before a run of defaulted bools shifts every later argument. (3) Mode- or arch-conditional consumption: a parameter such as a read VC is written to hardware only under one compile-time mode, so in the other mode it is dead. Or one arch applies a flag at init and another at execute, so a wrapper that hardcodes or drops the flag looks fine on the first arch and is wrong on the second. (4) Order or peer confusion across layers: non-commutative ops (atan2), matmul's reversed operand-to-source mapping versus eltwise reconfig order, src/dst or my/upstream fields swapped when wiring paired kernels, the wrong queue or a default queue used instead of the handle passed in, and eager versus traced paths forwarding different kwargs. Most of these are latent. They show up only when two CBs have different formats, when noc differs from noc_index, when the CQ is non-zero, or on one arch.

**Checks**

- List every API or LLK parameter that names a CB, operand, output, noc, vc, trid or queue and has a literal default (0, 16, noc_index, false). For each one, check whether the body indexes per-CB tables or programs hardware with it. Then grep the callers that omit it, uninit, reconfig and _short variants included. Any omitting caller whose real buffer is not the default is a finding. Removing the default so omissions fail to compile is the preferred fix.
- For functions with two or more adjacent same-typed or convertible defaulted parameters (offset then noc, vector_mode then param0, class_idx then type_idx), line up every call site and macro expansion position by position on every arch. A variable named noc, vc or pow landing in a slot named offset or vector_mode is a misbinding. Mode or selector parameters typed as plain int should be enum classes.
- Trace every hardware-selecting parameter (VC, noc index, posted, trid, rounding mode) down to the register write that realizes it, under every template mode (dedicated vs dynamic NoC, one-packet vs any-length) and every arch. If the write happens only under an unrelated mode, the parameter is dead in the other modes, and tests that sweep it are vacuous.
- When a wrapper calls a templated LLK, check that it forwards every template or mode parameter the callee exposes rather than hardcoding literals. Check that the paired init and execute (and init and uninit) calls get identical flags from the same source. Be most suspicious where one arch consumes the flag at init and another at execute.
- Before any matmul-family init or call, check that reconfig_data_format passes operands in matmul's source order (in1 feeds SrcA, in0 feeds SrcB) or uses the Reverse-order template. Check also that a reconfig follows any intervening op (mask add, tilize, reduce) that used different operands. 'Both CBs have the same format today' means latent, not safe, so check whether the factory can ever give the two CBs different formats.
- Diff the argument lists across sibling call sites of the same helper: bias vs no-bias epilogues, sharded vs interleaved, per-arch copies, eager vs traced paths, op vs prep or estimator. A site that passes fewer arguments than its siblings, into defaulted parameters, is a bug whenever the default (0 as a multiplier or reciprocal, output_padding {0,0}, a bypass flag) is not valid for every op.
- For non-commutative ops, trace one asymmetric input through the golden, the binding docstring, the host composite, any scalar or broadcast operand swaps, and the kernel parameter names. Distrust a caller that compensates with a neg or a swap, since it usually hides a wrong-order callee.
- In host code that wires paired kernels or endpoints (H/D, src/dst, upstream/downstream), write out what my, upstream and downstream mean for each side. Check that every field comes from the correct peer and the correct field of that peer, and that symmetric calls use the same argument order on both sides. In functions that receive a queue or context handle, flag any other way of fetching a queue (a defaulted index, a device default accessor, a literal 0).
- In code generators that emit kernel source as strings, check that every string containing {0}, {1} or {} really goes through the formatter. An unformatted '{1}' compiles as a braced initializer (true).
- Wherever a cache stores a parameter-to-slot mapping built by identity lookup (find_if on a pointer), check the case where one object is passed for two parameters. Also check whether the key encodes aliasing, and whether the repair depends on two independently defined iteration orders lining up.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rnE '(operand|output|icb|ocb|cb|cbid|input_operand|pack_output)[a-z_0-9]* *= *(0|16|tt::CBIndex::c_[0-9]+) *[,)]' tt_metal/hw/ckernels tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute, then look for empty-argument callers such as 'reduce_uninit()' and '*_init_short()' in ttnn kernels
- grep -rnE 'bool (is_)?fp32_dest_acc_en(_t)? *= *(false|true)' tt_metal/hw/ckernels tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute, especially the experimental/, experimental/2_0 and quasar llk_api copies
- In the per-arch noc_nonblocking_api.h: 'uint32_t read_req_vc' parameters whose NOC_CMD_STATIC_VC(read_req_vc) write sits inside 'if constexpr (noc_mode == DM_DYNAMIC_NOC)', or that are never written at all
- reconfig_data_format\((\w+),\s*(\w+)\) followed within about 10 lines by matmul_(tiles|block|init)\(\1,\s*\2 without SrcOrder::Reverse, in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/kernels/compute
- llk_\w+<true, *true>\( or LLK calls with literal template args inside ALWI compute-API wrappers; *_init<A,B> paired with *_block<C,D> in pool and tilize kernels
- template <..., uint32_t PARAMn = 0> helpers (ActivationApplyHelper, apply_activation_from_pack) whose call sites pass fewer template args than the host sets as compile-time args
- llk_(unpack|math|pack)_\w*(hw_configure|init|reconfig)\w*(<[^>]*>)?\((0|1|16)[,)] with a literal CB, and wrappers whose uint32_t icb/cb parameter never appears in the body
- Inside functions taking 'MeshCommandQueue& mesh_cq' or a cq_id: calls to mesh_command_queue(), command_queue(0) or a default-CQ accessor used with blocking=false
- TT_OP_/TTI_ multi-field macros (UNPACR_NOP, PACR_STRIDE) where a p_<struct>:: constant lands in a slot that belongs to a different field, a literal 0 sits in a descriptor or bd_table index slot, or the *_VALID width is wider than the ISA field
- Host sizing from a device-queried count (get_optimal_dram_bank_to_logical_worker_assignment().size()) while the kernels index 'constexpr ... TABLE[NUM_CORES]' tables, with no TT_FATAL pinning the count

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute (tilize.h, reduce.h, transpose, copy/unary init wrappers); tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api (especially experimental/, experimental/2_0 and quasar copies); tt_metal/hw/inc/api/dataflow and tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-*/*/noc_nonblocking_api*.h (VC, noc, trid parameters); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/kernels/compute (matmul reconfig order in sdpa, rotary, moreh, deepseek); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv (halo and inner-op forwarding, weight-prep vs op divergence); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise (non-commutative op order, unary codegen init strings); tt_metal/impl/dispatch and tt_metal/distributed (paired-kernel wiring, queue selection); models/tt_transformers generator (eager vs traced kwarg forwarding)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- A fix that adds a new enabling flag must widen the existing guard (existing mode OR new flag), not replace it. Replacing the dynamic-mode guard with the new flag broke the shared-command-buffer case and was reverted, and the original dead-VC defect is still unfixed.
- Sweeps that remove defaulted CB or fp32 parameters were scoped to one thread tier (unpack/pack) or to the main llk_api. They missed math-thread wrappers, uninit functions, experimental/ and 2_0 layers, and the Quasar copies, some of which were added at the same time. Re-run the grep over every tier and every newly added file.
- An operand-order fix in one kernel path left sibling paths inside the same kernels broken: masked-matmul, tilize_q and FP32 ring. The class recurred several times. Fix every reconfig-before-matmul site in the op family at once, and add an assert-enabled test with mixed formats.
- A fix to one misbinding in a paired host config was followed days later by another misbinding in the same struct (my_X taken where my_downstream_X was meant). Audit every field of the wiring, not just the reported one. The same applies to move constructors left with mismatched field names after the constructor bug was fixed.
- A fix that makes one surface (op or estimator) take a real parameter left the parallel surface on a hardcoded default. Weight prep kept output_padding {0,0}, the two sides picked different slice counts, and a PCC failure followed. Update every caller that auto-derives the same choice.
- Swapping arguments at the caller without fixing the callee's internal convention (quadrant constants) was reverted. Layers that compensate for each other must be fixed together, down to the reference convention.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx/blackhole/noc_nonblocking_api.h:491 — NOC_CTRL with NOC_CMD_STATIC_VC(read_req_vc) is written only under DM_DYNAMIC_NOC, so the caller's read VC is dropped in dedicated mode.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx/wormhole/noc_nonblocking_api.h:428 — same dedicated-mode read-VC drop as BH.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/dataflow/noc.h:203 — Noc::async_read with CUSTOM_VC computes req_vc and forwards it to noc_async_read, where it is ignored on dedicated-NoC kernels (the dataflow_api.h:517/562 wrappers expose read_req_vc with no effect).
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-2xx/quasar/noc_nonblocking_api_v1.h:323 — the Quasar fast_read takes read_req_vc and never writes it to any register.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute/reduce.h:100 — reduce_uninit(icb = 0). On Quasar the MxFp4 format restore is keyed on icb, and callers that omit it (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/kernel_lib/reduce_helpers_compute.inl:719, the quasar sdpa compute_common.hpp:298) restore CB 0 instead of the reduced operand.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/rotary_embedding/device/kernels/compute/rotary_embedding_single_tile.cpp:97 — natural-order reconfig_data_format(in_cb, trans_mat_cb) before matmul_tiles(in_cb, trans_mat_cb), so SrcA/SrcB formats are reversed whenever the input and trans_mat formats differ.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh/moreh_mean/device/kernels/moreh_mean_w.cpp:58 — reconfig_data_format(cb_input, scaler) before matmul_tiles(cb_input, scaler) is reversed for matmul (also at :107, and similar in moreh_sum_w and moreh_matmul).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek_prefill/mhc_split_sinkhorn/device/kernels/compute/mhc_split_sinkhorn_compute.cpp:57 — reconfig_data_format(a, CB_CONSTS) before matmul_tiles(a, CB_CONSTS) is reversed if the formats differ (also at :73).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek/mla/matmul_wo/device/matmul_wo_program_factory.cpp:54 — num_cores comes from the device DRAM-bank count, but the kernels index K_TILES_PER_CORE_A[NUM_CORES=12]. validate_on_program_cache_miss checks only rank, so a different bank count gives a silently wrong result or an out-of-bounds read.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa_decode/sdpa_decode.cpp:52 — the non-paged decode entry passes an unset program_config through (the paged entry at :113 applies a default), so the factory uses every available core per head.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv/conv_transpose2d/prepare_conv_transpose2d_weights.cpp:440 — bias prep hardcodes output_padding {0,0} while the op uses the real value, so prep and op can disagree on dimensions.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/blackhole/metal/llk_api/experimental/llk_unpack_A_sdpa_api.h:24 — defaulted transpose flags followed by operand = 0, which drives face geometry and formats. This is the exact shape removed from llk_unpack_A_init, and it is a trap for new callers (llk_math_topk_xl_copy_api.h:15 has the same operand = 0 default).


## `tt-cb-capacity`: CB and L1 region sizes disagree with actual writes

The host sizes a circular buffer, dataflow buffer, scratch region or stack from one quantity, but the kernel writes or keeps alive a different one. Five forms recur. (1) The wrong unit or the wrong tensor: total size across all cores (buffer->size()) instead of the per-bank shard; the other operand's shard spec; logical row bytes instead of the aligned page stride the kernel actually advances by; an element count where tiles were meant; a buffering multiplier applied to a CB that sits on a tensor's own buffer. (2) Kernel transfers larger than the page: aligned-down reads that add misalignment, packets that append extra aligned pages after the payload, fill loops bounded by a user shape parameter (kernel window) instead of the tile or chunk size, and hardcoded `+1024` slack standing in for a page-size expression. (3) FIFO geometry assumptions: one thread produces a runtime-sized count of tiles into a fixed 2-page CB before it consumes any, which deadlocks it; variable-size pushes whose running offset steps past the FIFO limit instead of landing on it; in-place L1 accumulation that needs the CB to hold exactly one block, broken when the CB is aliased onto a larger output shard. (4) Host fit predicates that are wrong: they are evaluated before the op's own L1 output is allocated, counted in the wrong units, leave out a CB, or are hand-copied from the factory and have drifted from it. (5) Fixed regions that are too small for the worst case and have no runtime guard, such as a per-thread TLS+stack region copied from a sibling core role, or a log buffer whose admission test does not reserve room for closing records it has already promised.

**Checks**

- For every CB/DFB bound to an existing buffer (set_globally_allocated_address, CBDescriptor.buffer, borrowed_from, create_cb with a Buffer*), recompute total_size = page_size * num_pages. Check it is <= that same tensor's per-core aligned shard (aligned_size_per_bank or shard_shape x aligned page). Flag sizes built from buffer->size(), from the other operand's shard spec, or with a buffering_factor, *2 or num_stages multiplier.
- For every row-major sharded buffer that a kernel walks in L1, compare the stride the kernel advances by (usually aligned_page_size) with the page or entry size the host declared. A raw width*element_size is a bug unless validate() forces the row to be alignment-sized. Test with a row width below the L1/NoC alignment.
- For every kernel write into a CB (noc_async_read landing, raw get_write_ptr loop, fabric packet), compute the maximum bytes written: payload + max misalignment (align-1) + any appended aligned pages, or loop bound * stride. Compare that with the host page size * reserved pages. Treat loop bounds taken from user shape parameters (kernel_h*kernel_w, channels, freq) as suspect. Sibling fill loops that feed one consumer must use the same clamp.
- For every host 'fits in L1' predicate, recompute it term by term against the factory's real CB allocations: same units (tiles, elements, bytes), same buffering multipliers, every CB included, same work split. Then find where it runs. At wrapper or routing time, before the op's own interleaved L1 output exists, it must subtract that pending output's per-core footprint. Inside a factory it must not.
- In a compute kernel where one thread both pushes to and later pops from an intermediate CB, find the maximum number of tiles pushed before the first pop. If it depends on a runtime count, not a constant <= the CB depth, large shapes deadlock.
- For a CB filled with variable push sizes (a full chunk plus a remainder, or per-core-group counts), check that the running write offset lands exactly on the FIFO limit at every wrap. A depth that is merely the LCM of the push sizes is not enough.
- Where a CB is accumulated in place (packer L1 accumulation, reload-and-add), check that its depth equals exactly one block's footprint. If it is aliased with the output shard, check that the aliasing guard covers every dimension that can make the shard hold several blocks: batch, M blocks and N blocks.
- When a factory and an L1-usage predictor, or several default surfaces (config struct, op fallback, binding default, 0 = auto helper), each size the same buffer or block, check that they all resolve through one source of truth. After an API migration, diff every CB size expression before and after.
- Validate caller-supplied scratch or intermediate tensors against the same per-core size the auto-computed path produces. An optional tensor used as-is is an overrun path.
- For fixed per-thread regions (TLS+stack) and fixed log or ring buffers, check that the size is chosen per role rather than copied from a sibling role, and that it covers the debug-instrumented worst case. Admission tests must reserve room for every closing record already promised plus the new one. Evaluate them at one slot left, with nesting.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `CircularBufferConfig(<buf>->size()` or `.total_size = X.buffer()->size()` next to set_globally_allocated_address or `.buffer =`: ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/device/*program_factory*.cpp
- `.buffer = <t>.buffer()` or borrowed_from CB/DFB whose total_size or num_pages contains buffering_factor, double_buffer, `* 2` or num_stages: pool/upsample, sliding_window, conv
- page_size/entry_size = `W * element_size()` or `stick_size_bytes` on a borrowed sharded buffer without tt::align or aligned_page_size: data_movement/transpose, untilize*, reshard
- `is_enough_space(` or `get_max_l1_space(` in wrapper-level (non-factory) .cpp without get_pending_l1_output_reservation: data_movement/* and experimental/quasar/* forks
- `noc_async_read(..., size + misalignment)` or `padded_stick_size + misalignment` in readers, with the landing CB page = tt::align(row_bytes, alignment) and no extra alignment slack: slice, padded_slice, slice_write
- L1 estimate `(2 * W_padded + 2 * H_padded + H_padded * W_padded) * element_size()` or similar element-unit sums next to factories that allocate ht*wt TILES
- share_out_interm_buffer or do_not_inplace_interm0_out_CB decided from format equality or `per_core_M != out_block_h` alone while intermed0 num_entries = out_shard_tiles and packer_l1_acc is on: matmul/device/factory/*
- Two sequential `for (j...; j < freq|HtWt|num_tiles; ...)` loops in one compute kernel, push_back to a 2-page CB in the first and wait_front in the second: ttnn compute kernels
- `total_pkt_size = packet_size_bytes + 1024` or CircularBufferConfig(packet_size_bytes) where kernels send payload + 2*aligned_page: ttnn/operations/ccl/*
