- `bufferHasRoom<...>()` with no additional slots before `stackSize +=`, and __local_stride/__tls_size defsyms reused across core roles: tt_metal/tools/profiler, llrt/hal/tt-2xx

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (tilize/untilize, transpose, reshard, concat, repeat, slice); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/* (copied factories that miss fixes made to the main copy); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory (sharing the out/interm CB under L1 accumulation); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/pool and sliding_window/halo and conv (config and scratch CBs, reader fill loops); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl (packet-sized CBs and intermediates); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction/generic (variable push sizes, host fit predicates); tt_metal/tools/profiler/kernel_profiler.hpp (marker buffer admission); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-2xx/quasar + llrt/hal/tt-2xx (per-role TLS/stack region sizes)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land in the main factory and miss the forked copy under experimental/quasar/* (tilize/untilize L1 reservation, matmul share guard, transpose estimate). Always grep for the same function name in the forks.
- A tunable default fixed on one surface regressed through the others: the config struct, the op fallback, the binding default and the prepare-weights helper each picked their own value. Mismatched blocking between producer and consumer gave near-zero PCC, and the workaround for that brought the L1 overflow back. Route every surface through one helper.
- A migration to a new API (program descriptors, dataflow buffers) quietly put back the old worst-case CB size, and the predictor/allocation self-check tripped. Diff every size expression across a refactor.
- Guards come back approximate: a path that had been rerouted away returned for a new layout (sharded row-major transpose) with a newly written L1 estimate in the wrong units. A reintroduced path needs its fit check recomputed against the real CBs.
- LCM-of-push-sizes depth was accepted as a fix, but the offset still stepped past the FIFO limit on corner shapes. The real invariant is that the offset lands exactly on the limit at every wrap, plus an L1-fit check for the larger size that follows from it.
- Fixes corrected the auto-computed path but left a caller-supplied optional tensor, or a non-target arch branch, on the old sizing (reduce_to_root intermediate; the profiler zone reservation fixed only under the Quasar branch). Region-size fixes enlarge a constant but add no runtime guard.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tools/profiler/kernel_profiler.hpp:725 — the non-Quasar profileScope admits a zone start with bufferHasRoom() at zero extra slots, while the Quasar branch at :723 reserves 2*MARKER-1; a nested zone's unconditional end marker can land one marker past the per-RISC buffer
- tt_metal/tools/profiler/kernel_profiler.hpp:831 — main_accumulate_start uses the same zero-headroom bufferHasRoom() before it reserves an end slot
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/reduce_to_root/device/reduce_to_root_op.cpp:106 — a caller-supplied optional_intermediate_tensor spec is returned as-is, and validate() never checks its per-core size against payload + 2 aligned pages
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/tilize/tilize.cpp:68 — is_enough_space routing check with no pending L1 output reservation; the main tilize.cpp:85 subtracts it
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/tilize_with_val_padding/tilize_with_val_padding.cpp:104 — same missing pending-output reservation in the Quasar fork
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/untilize/untilize.cpp:75 — same missing pending-output reservation
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/untilize_with_unpadding/untilize_with_unpadding.cpp:102 — same missing pending-output reservation
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/transpose.cpp:155 — sharded row-major WH fit estimate counts double buffers in elements while the factories allocate 2*Wt+2*Ht tiles (+Ht im2), so it under-counts and borderline shapes overflow L1
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transpose/transpose.cpp:151 — copy of the same under-counting estimate
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_batched_hs_dram_sharded_program_factory.cpp:277 — out/interm0 share storage on format equality alone, with no packer_l1_acc/one-block guard, unlike the main copy's share_out_interm_buffer condition; multi-batch shards can drop K blocks
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_mcast_2d_program_factory.cpp:406 — in-place interm/out guard checks only per_core_M != out_block_h; with out_block_w < per_core_N the shared shard holds several blocks under L1 accumulation (reachability unverified; the 1D factory at mcast_1d:170 has the same guard)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/transpose/device/transpose_hc_sharded_program_factory.cpp:360 — borrowed sharded input/output CBs use raw stick_size_bytes as the page with no alignment round-up; narrow W may mismatch the padded per-row allocation stride


## `numeric-edge`: Special values break SFPU, composite and host numerics

Kernels and composites get the normal range right and fail on the edges: NaN (either sign), +/-inf, -0.0, subnormals, INT_MIN/INT_MAX, zero padding, and probability or clamp endpoints. There are four recurring mechanisms. (1) SFPU float predicates check bits, not IEEE values. '== 0' means all bits are zero and '< 0' means the sign bit is set, so -0.0 counts as nonzero and negative, and the sign of a NaN steers range classification, clamps and ge/le written as the complement of lt/gt. (2) Arithmetic stands in for a select or compare: x-y then a zero test (inf-inf gives NaN), mask*a + notmask*b (NaN*0 and inf*0 give NaN, and the FPU and SFPU disagree on this), a - floor(a*recip(b))*b (0*inf when b is infinite), float-to-int-to-float rounding that saturates non-finite values, and -max(-x) or a sign-magnitude conversion that overflows at INT_MIN. SFPSWAP and float compares order values in sign-magnitude, so INT_MIN turns into -0 and pairs of negative values sort in reverse. (3) Hand-built exponent/mantissa code guards one end of the range and forgets the other: reciprocal of 0 comes out finite, an NR seed at inf goes through underflow and then 0*inf, subnormal decode is off by one in the exponent, and uint8 typecast of 0 shifts an implicit-one mantissa. (4) Host-side handling patches or hides the problem: static_cast of a float +/-inf or negative pad value to an unsigned integer (UB), where() overrides keyed on value equality (a==b gives 0, which is wrong at inf), overflow bounds copied from exp onto other functions, lo/hi pairs that are never ordered, and goldens or tests that clamp, nan_to_num, pick a friendly pad value or loosen PCC so the defect can't show up.

**Checks**

- For every float predicate against zero in SFPU code (==0, !=0, <0, >=0, >0, <=0, and helpers such as an is-zero wrapper), evaluate it at -0.0, +NaN and -NaN against the torch answer. On this hardware -0 is 'nonzero and negative', and a NaN takes its sign bit's branch. When a fix lands for one op, grep the shared helper and every sibling op, tier (tt-llk vs metal llk_api) and arch copy.
- For every comparison op family (zero-compare, scalar-compare, tensor-tensor, and mixed-dtype or scalar-first fallbacks), fill in the NaN, +/-0, inf-inf and subnormal rows of the truth table. Ordered compares with NaN must be false, != must be true, and +0 must equal -0. Flag ge/le written as the negation of lt/gt, and any compare built as subtract followed by a zero or sign test.
- Wherever signed integers are ordered or converted (SFPSWAP min/max, a float compare on int bits, a two's-complement to sign-magnitude load mode, manual sign|(-x), host -max(-x) or abs), test INT_MIN, INT_MAX, pairs of negatives and 0 vs INT_MIN ties. Also check what the load mode leaves in the register against what the comparator assumes, per arch and per direction (row, column, scalar).
- For any range-classified transcendental (overflow/normal/underflow arms chosen by a derived exponent or a clamped value), trace +NaN, -NaN, +inf and -inf through every arm on every arch. Flag an arm that writes a literal 0, inf or -1 without first excluding NaN.
- For hand-built exponent/mantissa code (reciprocal, rsqrt, NR seeds, float-to-int typecast, fp16/bf16 decoders), evaluate both ends of the exponent range: +/-0, the smallest and largest subnormal, the largest finite value, +/-inf and NaN. Check for inf*0, inf-inf and denormal flush-to-zero steps. Look for a guard (or a construction argument) at each end, and diff the guard against sibling variants (uint8 vs int32, WH vs BH vs Quasar).
- Treat a conditional select written as arithmetic (mask*a + (1-mask)*b, cmp(x)*grad, add(multiply(ltz,..),multiply(eqz,..))) as a bug when the unselected operand is NaN or inf. Check whether the answer changes between the FPU and SFPU engines.
- For formula ops (fmod, remainder, div with rounding, addcdiv, rdiv), build the full special-value table: 0, -0, +/-inf and NaN on each side, plus sign combinations. Compare the native kernel against the composite fallback. Flag overrides keyed on value equality (a==b gives 0, a==0 gives 0) and single-constant patches such as +inf that ignore operand signs.
- In host code, grep for static_cast from a float pad, fill or scalar value to an integer type. Trace which values reach it (reduction identities of +/-inf, negative pads) and which dtypes validation accepts. Any unsigned or int dtype without a per-dtype sentinel or bit_cast is UB.
- For block-float outputs (bfp8/bfp4), work out f(pad_value), usually f(0). If it is inf or NaN, it poisons the shared exponent of the real data in the same 16-wide block on shapes that are not tile-aligned.
- Audit the oracle along with the kernel. Flag goldens that clamp, apply nan_to_num, or call torch in its undefined region (for example inverted clamp bounds). Flag tests that choose a special pad_value, narrow the input range, lower PCC into the failure band, list a dtype that every vector invalidates, or are named for one op but call another. Check that random-threshold comparisons (rand < p) are exact at p=0 and p=1 given the generator's real interval after rounding.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -nE 'v_(else)?if\s*\(\s*\w+\s*(==|!=|>=|<=|<|>)\s*-?0(\.0*)?[fF]?\s*\)|_sfpu_is_fp16_zero_' in tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu and tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_*/common/inc/sfpu
- v_if(v < s){0} v_else{1} or 'v - s >= 0' (a complement of a strict compare) in ckernel_sfpu_unary_comp.h and apply_unary_float_comp in tt-llk ckernel_sfpu_comp.h
- eqz|gez|lez|nez|gtz|ltz( ttnn::sub_sfpu( ... ) and postprocess UnaryOpType::EQZ/NEZ/GTZ on float paths in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/binary/binary.cpp and binary_ng_utils.cpp
- TTI_SFPSWAP / SFPSWAP_MOD1_VEC_MIN_MAX near InstrModLoadStore::INT32_2S_COMP or INT32 loads, plus manual '0x80000000 | (x & 0x7FFFFFFF)' conversions, in reduce, max_min, clamp, relu, topk, sort and cummax SFPU kernels
- v_if(e < 1) { y = 0.0f; } or clamp(xlog2, 0, 255) with no is_nan/is_finite guard in ckernel_sfpu_exp*.h and other exp-based kernels, especially the Quasar ports
- magic - (as<vUInt>(val) >> 1) NR seeds guarded only by v_if(val != 0.0f) in ckernel_sfpu_sqrt_custom.h, rsqrt.h, recip.h and cbrt; setexp(x,126) or '126 - exexp' in reciprocal kernels
- a - floor|trunc(a * recip(b)) * b, and v_if(a == b) { result = 0 }, in ckernel_sfpu_binary_fmod.h / binary_remainder.h with no is-inf(b) case
- ttnn::multiply(<gtz|ltz|eqz|logical_and|ge|le result>, grad) or add(multiply(mask,a),multiply(notmask,b)) in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary_backward and binary_backward
- static_cast<(std::)?u?int(16|32)_t>\(\s*(fill_value|pad_value|value|scalar)\s*\) from float in ttnn data_movement pad, fill_pad and tilize_with_val_padding factories; get_pad_value returning numeric_limits<float>::infinity() without per-dtype cases
- constants near log(FLT_MAX) (88.5, 88.7, 3.4e38) used in non-exp kernels or backward ops; torch.clamp/nan_to_num inside ttnn golden functions; pad_value=1.0 next to bfloat8_b in tests; check_with_pcc(...,0.99) next to siblings at 0.999

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/hw/ckernels/{wormhole_b0,blackhole,quasar}/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu (comp, unary_comp, binary_comp, fmod, remainder, exp, sqrt/rsqrt/recip, i1, max_min, reduce, typecast, logical_not, mask); tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_*/common/inc/sfpu (shared predicate helpers, comp.h, relu.h int path); Quasar ports of SFPU kernels, which lag the WH/BH special-value fixes; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/binary and binary_ng (compare lowering, scalar and mixed-dtype fallbacks, composites); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/unary_backward and binary_backward (mask-multiply selects, domain constants, unordered bounds); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction/generic and data_movement pad/fill_pad/tilize_with_val_padding (pad identities, float-to-int casts); ttnn/ttnn/operations golden functions and tests/sweep_framework/sweeps/eltwise (oracles that hide edges); hand-written format converters (tt-train safetensors, dprint parser, tt-llk dest helpers)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land on one op, one path or one arch. The same -0/NaN predicate was fixed in sign, heaviside and sqrt one at a time while the shared zero helper, eqz/nez/ltz/gez and logical_not kept it. The tensor-tensor compare was fixed while the scalar-first and mixed-dtype fallbacks still subtract-then-test. WH/BH got the guard and Quasar did not. After any fix, sweep the helper, the sibling op families, every tier (tt-llk vs metal) and every arch.
- Some fixes only move the edge. A comparator flip fixed p=0 but broke p=1 until the generator's interval was also changed. A signbit fix repaired +0 and broke -0. A hard-coded +inf patch ignored the signs of the operands. Re-run the whole special-value table after the change, not just the reported row.
- Workarounds that live in tests get recorded as fixes: a friendly pad_value, a narrowed input range, PCC lowered into the failure band, a dtype invalidated wholesale, or a golden that clamps or calls nan_to_num to match the device. The kernel defect survives, and the test can no longer fail.
- Host-side where() patches written to cover a kernel defect turn wrong once the kernel is fixed (a==b gives 0 at inf, forced 0/0 results). Newly native kernels have brought back the same overrides, so native and fallback paths drift apart on 0/0 and inf.
- A fix can depend on arch-specific arithmetic, such as a canonical-NaN property or FPU NaN*0=0 against SFPU IEEE behaviour, or on a sentinel pad value that hides the edge. When dispatch moves to another engine or arch, the bug comes back. Record the hardware assumption and test the raw bit patterns (for example 0xFFC00000, 0x80000000).
- A UB fix for one dtype (INT32 sentinels carried as raw bits) left the neighbouring dtype (UINT32 +/-inf identity) and the copies in other factories on the old float-to-int cast.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_mask.h:26 — the mask op tests the mask with _sfpu_is_fp16_zero_ (v == 0.0F, still unfixed in tt-llk ckernel_sfpu_is_fp16_zero.h:18), so a -0.0 mask counts as nonzero. The line :50 inf variant and the BH copy are the same.
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_comp.h:110 — eqz/nez go through _sfpu_is_fp16_zero_ and ltz/gez (:130/:140) use 'v >= 0.0f', so -0.0 misclassifies (BH copy at :109). Latent if the metal tier is the only caller, so confirm reachability.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_logical_not.h:39 — the float layout uses 'v == 0' (a bit test), so logical_not(-0.0)=0 where torch gives True. The BH copy at :37 is the same.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/blackhole/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_unary_comp.h:106 — calculate_unary_ge is 'v_if(v < s) 0 else 1' and unary_le is the complement of 'v > s', so a NaN input returns 1 for ge/le. No NaN rejection in the scalar compare family.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_unary_comp.h:109 — unary_ge is 'v - s >= 0' by design as the complement of lt, so NaN (or inf-inf) with a clear sign bit gives ge=1.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise/binary/binary.cpp:554 — the scalar-lhs relational path still computes eq/ge/le as eqz/gez/lez(sub_sfpu(lhs, rhs)) (lines 548-554), so eq(inf, inf) comes out false because inf-inf is NaN.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_binary_fmod.h:99 — the override 'v_if(a == b) result = 0' can give fmod(inf, inf)=0 where NaN is expected. BH :95 is more likely hit because it uses a native compare. There is also no inf-divisor guard: a - trunc(a*recip(b))*b gives 0*inf.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_binary_remainder.h:299 — float remainder computes a - floor(a*recip(b))*b with no is-inf(b) case, so remainder(finite, +/-inf) can come out NaN. The BH copy has the same shape.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/wormhole_b0/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_i1.h:87 — I1_MAX_INPUT=88.5 is exp's overflow bound applied to i1, which stays finite up to about 91.9, so the kernel saturates to about 1.15e37 and returns a finite value where the answer is inf (BH :87 is the same). The golden in ttnn/ttnn/operations/unary.py:488 clamps the same way, which hides it.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction/generic/generic_reductions.cpp:117 — get_pad_value returns +/-inf for UINT32 max/min (only INT32 gets a raw-bit sentinel). That reaches fill_pad_program_factory.hpp:67 static_cast<std::uint32_t>(float), which is UB and a wrong identity.
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/quasar/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_exp.h:123 — 'v_if(e < 1) { y = 0.0f; }' underflow arm with no NaN exclusion, so exp(-NaN)=0 on Quasar. Nearby, ckernel_sfpu_sqrt_custom.h:17 has only a 'val != 0.0f' guard, so the NR seed at +inf gives NaN.
- tests/sweep_framework/sweeps/eltwise/binary/fmod/fmod_unary.py:111 — the 'fmod' unary sweep calls ttnn.remainder with the remainder golden (:99), so fmod-with-scalar is never tested even though it differs from remainder on negative operands.


## `tt-work-split`: Host work-split and core-role sets disagree

A program factory works out the same core set or per-core share in two places that disagree: a work-split helper versus index arithmetic in the runtime-arg loop, a kernel-placement range versus the work-assignment range, a reserved set versus a placed set (an exact shard grid on one side and a bounding box or merged set on the other), or the output-spec and shard computation versus the factory. The drift appears only in degenerate or unusual cases. Examples: zero full cores, a partial or padding-only core, a non-rectangular or offset (non-(0,0)) sub-device grid, core counts that are not a power of two (such as 110), batch > 1, and remapped or zigzag work. The arithmetic mistakes repeat. Ceil-divided shares get used as exact divisors. Divisor results are rounded after the search. Unsigned `total - start` or `share - overshoot` wraps. A boundary or partial element is subtracted twice. Wrap-to-next-row uses end_coord, or resets to x=0 instead of the rectangle origin. A block size is chosen from an L1 budget and never clamped to the real problem size. The result is a hang when CB push/pop or semaphore counts no longer match across reader, compute and writer. Other results are two roles on one core, NOC writes past the destination allocation, wrong output, or spurious host TT_FATALs.

**Checks**

- For every multi-kernel factory, list the core set each CreateKernel, CB, semaphore, multicast target and runtime-arg loop uses. Check that they all come from ONE source (one split_work_to_cores result or one shard grid). Check that cores for other roles (fabric senders, mux, reducers) are subtracted from the UNION of every placed set, including bounding boxes and merge() results, not from a narrower exact grid.
- When a runtime-arg loop reclassifies cores (full, cliff, partial, padding, idle, reducer) by index arithmetic instead of membership in the split's own CoreRangeSets, try each degenerate count by hand: zero full cores, zero cliff cores, one core, a single row or column, and active < available. Flag special-case exclusions such as `i != 0`, and flag modulus divisors that can collapse to 1.
- For any helper taking (start, count, grid), decide whether grid is an absolute extent or a size relative to start. Flag callers that pass a nonzero sub-device start with a relative bbox grid_size, and wrap logic that resets to 0 instead of the rectangle origin. Then check that the output-spec, shard-spec and validation code use the same anchor as the factory.
- Check the arithmetic invariant after every post-adjustment: num_cores * per_core >= total with overshoot < one share (or exactly == total when kernels assume exact division). That covers rounding to even, clamping to a grid limit, and capping the core count at the number of work blocks. Any value computed with ceil and later used as a divisor to derive group or core counts must divide exactly, or the remainder must be handled. Assert groups * per_group == total.
- Replace every unsigned `total - start`, `share - overshoot` or `size() - count - partial` used as a count with a guarded min(share, remaining). Check whether a 'cores with X' count already includes the partial or boundary element before it is subtracted again.
- Check that the extent the host splits over (padded shape, tile count) equals the extent each kernel loops over (runtime or compile-time total_num_rows). Every assigned block must produce exactly one push, consume and pop on reader, compute and writer, even with zero valid rows. When source and destination heights differ (padding or unpadding), keep 'rows to iterate' separate from 'source row stride'.
- For conversions between two tilings or paddings (retile, tilize/untilize with padding), clamp the per-core producer and consumer counts separately, each to its OWN buffer's physical page count. Check whether the op flattens N*C*H into one 2D matrix when padding is applied per outer slice. Test with N*C > 1 and H not a multiple of either tile height.
- When a block size comes from a resource budget (L1, CB, Dest), clamp it to Mt/Nt (floored to >= 1) and re-legalize every derived value: subblock divisibility, grid, shard shape. Flags that change a dimension's meaning (fuse_batch) must be read from the program config by both the output-spec code and the factory, never hardcoded differently.
- For kernels, list the get_arg_val indices: they must be distinct and increasing and match the host push order under every feature flag. The runtime-arg vectors for idle or placeholder cores must be at least as long as the prefix the kernel reads before its early exit. When two interleaved counters hand out core coordinates, cap each one so the ranges can never overlap, and assert the combined core list has no duplicates.
- When work is remapped (zigzag, pairing, rotation), per-core counters compared with host counts must use the same index space (loop ordinal vs remapped index), and the host must distribute whole pairs. Evaluate `% num_cores` only after the num_cores == 0 guard.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `sub_device_cores.subtract(X)` followed by kernels created on `X.bounding_box()` or `X.merge(...)`; grep `bounding_box()` near `subtract(` in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/ccl/**/*program*.cpp and experimental/transformer/all_reduce_*
- `has_cliff_row && i != 0 && ((i + 1) % (full_cores_per_row + 1))` or any cliff/full classification by loop index instead of `cliff_*_core_range.contains(core)`: tilize/untilize block factories, including experimental/quasar copies
- `num_cores_to_corerangeset(num_cores, <allowed_worker_cores>.bounding_box().grid_size(), ...)` or `num_cores_to_corerangeset(start_core, n, grid_size)` with a relative grid; `CoreCoord{0, y+1}` wrap: matmul_device_operation.cpp compute_output_specs, layernorm/rms sharded helpers
- `output.padded_shape()[-2]` vs `logical_shape()[-2]` passed as total_num_rows; `this_block_num_rows = total_num_rows - start_row_id`; `size_2d += total_num_rows`: tilize_with_val_padding and untilize_with_unpadding readers and writers
- `physical_volume() / tensor_width` used as a single flattened height across two tile heights; `output_rows = nblocks * ratio` without min() against the output's physical rows: tilize retile factories
- `num_cores - num_cores % 2` after a divisor search; `get_max_cores_divisible_by_tiles_per_core_tiles(..., true)`; `limit / num_cores_z`: tt_metal/common/work_split.cpp and its callers
- `std::ceil(a/b)` or `div_up(...)` whose result is later used as `/ per_core` to derive core, group or reducer counts; `units_last = share - (assigned - total)`: sdpa_decode, argmax, topk and other factories accepting sub_core_grids
- hardcoded `out_subblock_h = 4; out_subblock_w = 2`, `/*fuse_batch=*/true` literal in compute_output_specs, `.fuse_batch = false` forced in auto-config, per_core factor from a capacity ladder with no `std::min(.., Mt)`: matmul config (main + quasar)
- `get_arg_val<uint32_t>(0)` appearing more than once in a kernel; idle rt-arg vectors built as `std::vector<uint32_t>(N, 0)` with a literal N
- `total_pairs % num_cores` or `x % num_cores` evaluated before a `num_cores == 0 ?` ternary; `core_grid_ranges[i + 1]` indexed without a bounds check

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/{tilize,tilize_with_val_padding,untilize,untilize_with_unpadding} block and retile factories and their dataflow kernels; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/* (stale copies of fixed factories: tilize, matmul config, matmul output specs); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device (auto program-config selection, compute_output_specs shard grids, 1D/2D mcast factories on offset sub-devices); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl and experimental/transformer/all_reduce_* (sender/worker core reservation vs reduction core placement); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa and sdpa_decode (core allocation, reducer/output slot assignment, zigzag balancing); tt_metal/common/work_split.cpp (split_work_to_cores CoreRangeSet overload, num_cores_to_corerangeset*, divisor helpers); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/conv/conv2d (activation-reuse partial/padding-core counts, CB sizing under feature flags); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/reduction (argmax, topk with sub_core_grids)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes to main factories often skip the duplicated experimental/quasar copies. The tilize cliff-row guard, the matmul auto-config clamp, subblock and fuse_batch, and the output-spec fuse_batch literal all went unfixed there. After any fix, grep the whole tree for the identical expression.
- A fix to the program factory's core placement often leaves the other derivations of the same placement unfixed: output-spec shard grid, validation, override callbacks. The 2D matmul output spec was re-anchored to the sub-device start, but the 1D and reuse branches still anchor at (0,0).
- Fixing one side of a host/kernel contract re-broke later. An ifdef cleanup made the kernel always read a runtime arg that the host pushed only under one flag. Guard the contract itself (always push, or the same predicate on both sides), not just the case that was reported.
- Clamping a row count to make a hang go away (padded height as total_num_rows) can conflate two invariants, rows to iterate and source row stride. The hang is gone for plain tilize, but padding ops read nonexistent input rows or step batches wrongly.
- Workarounds that swap the primitive (split to slice) instead of repairing the split arithmetic leave the trap in the shared helper (request_even rounding) and can bring new semantic bugs at call sites, such as a real/imag swap.
- A clamp added next to a new min() exposed an adjacent unsigned underflow ((Mt-1) with sub-tile Mt) that needed a follow-up. Re-check every derived quantity at the new boundary values the clamp makes reachable.

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/layernorm/device/sharded_layernorm_factory_helpers.cpp:271 — passes start_core with a relative all_core_grid_size to num_cores_to_corerangeset, and the wrap test at :283 compares against absolute bbox.end_coord.x and resets to x=0; rms_allgather_program_factory.cpp:341 has the same shape
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/all_reduce_create_qkv_heads/device/all_reduce_create_qkv_heads_program_factory.cpp:319 — output_tensor_cores taken from shard_spec grid rather than the buffer's cores-with-data, so reduction kernels on empty shard cores can wait forever
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/untilize_with_unpadding/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_unary_stick_layout_wh_multicore_metal2.cpp:80 — unsigned total_num_rows - start_row_id wraps when a block starts more than a tile past the logical rows (split is over the padded input height)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa/device/ring_joint_sdpa_program_factory.cpp:2031 — `total_pairs % num_cores` evaluated before the num_cores==0 guards (and at :2035 for total_q_chunks)


## `stale-sibling`: Change lands in one copy, siblings keep the stale contract

A contract changes in one place: a signature, parameter name, module path, struct or bitfield layout, config default, return dtype, required input format, env var, or a hardware register that must now be programmed. The change or fix then reaches only some of the code that depends on it. The copies left behind still build or import, but they are wrong. Typical survivors are the other variant of the same op (unicast vs multicast, one-shot vs set_state/with_state vs inline, prefill vs decode), the same helper on another arch or in an experimental/Quasar/model-private fork, a second consumer of a shared host-computed define, a debug/sanitizer/logging macro that rebuilds state from hard-coded indices, or a separate process that has to derive the same value. These stale siblings survive because nothing runs them. They are JIT-compiled kernels the host build never compiles, headers pulled in only by an optional debug feature, tests that are disabled or hardware/topology-gated, arch-conditional branches, dynamic Python access (getattr, lazy imports inside a broad except), or two PRs merged close together where each was green alone. Some failures are silent: a count that only reaches a software counter and never the register, a granularity that divides one loop bound but not its twin, or a config override applied after the object that caches it was built. These show up as corruption, dropped tiles or wrong numerics, not as crashes.

**Checks**

- When a diff changes a contract (signature, param name, module path, header/namespace, field layout, default value, return dtype, env var), mechanically enumerate EVERY consumer repo-wide with grep or AST. Include JIT-compiled device kernels under **/kernels/**, tests/, models/, sweeps, scripts, CI yaml, and code merged after the branch point. Flag any consumer still using the old spelling or relying on the old default.
- Diff sibling variants of one operation side by side: unicast vs multicast, one-shot vs init_state/with_state vs inline vs wide-address, posted vs non-posted, prefill vs decode factory, and each arch plus experimental/Quasar/model-private forks. Flag any register write, guard, drain or validation that one variant has and another lacks, especially if a recent fix touched only one.
- A parameter that only feeds a software counter or bookkeeping and never reaches a hardware register write (e.g. ndests credited to an ack counter but never written) means the fix missed that path. So does an address parameter that defaults to 0 when hardware will write through it.
- For every host-computed compile-time define (granularity, chunk size, block count), list every kernel loop that consumes it and the trip count each loop pairs it with. The value must divide or bound ALL of them, not just the one it was derived from, especially for usually-equal pairs (QK vs V head dim, in vs out width). The kernel should static_assert divisibility or handle the remainder.
- Debug, sanitizer and logging macros that rebuild a transaction from hardware state must read the same cmd-buf/index expression the issuing code used, for every NoC mode and RISC. A literal per-RISC buffer constant in debug code, or a function taking cmd_buf that calls a sanitizer macro without forwarding it, is a finding.
- When a shared config default or flag changes (e.g. an optimization now on by default), list every model that reuses the shared module with its own replacement component, and every construction path (demo, vLLM generator, tests). Check that each one honors the flag or turns it off in its config class, BEFORE any constructor that latches the flag in __init__.
- When several processes, ranks or tiers each build the 'same' model or buffer, check that each runtime-rewritten field (vocab size, padding multiple, device count) comes from the same source with the same rounding formula. Look for a cross-process shape check.
- Resolve every absolute `from tests.` / `from models.` import and every function-local relative import to a file on disk, including imports inside arch-conditional branches and inside try/except blocks that swallow ImportError/Exception.
- For env vars and runtime options set by tests/harnesses (setenv, putenv, os.environ, monkeypatch, CI env blocks), verify the product code still reads each name. A name nothing reads means the claimed configuration is silently not in effect.
- When a packed host/device bitfield or shared mailbox struct changes, grep for literal masks/shifts and field paths in every encoder, decoder, python tool and debug-only header. Require shared named constants with static_asserts. Treat a disabled test's exclusively-compiled headers as unverified.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/**/noc_nonblocking_api*.h: `*_with_state` / inline functions taking `ndests`/`num_dests` where that name appears only in `noc_nonposted_writes_acked[...] +=`; atomic/multicast functions with `uint32_t atomic_ret_val = 0` and no return-address write under `if constexpr (noc_mode == DM_DYNAMIC_NOC)`
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/debug/*.h and dataflow_api.h: literal `NCRISC_RD_CMD_BUF|NCRISC_WR_CMD_BUF|BRISC_*_CMD_BUF` inside debug/logging macros; `DEBUG_SANITIZE_*_STATE(` inside a function whose signature has a `cmd_buf` parameter the macro does not receive
- tt-llk (all arches + experimental/): `grep -rnE 'STALLWAIT\([^)]*(SRCA_VLD|SRCB_VLD)' | grep -v 'MATH *|'` then check whether a Dest->Src move follows; diff each helper against its twin in the other arch directory
- ttnn/**/program_factory.cpp: `compute_defines.insert({"*_GRANULARITY"` derived from one dimension; kernels: `(cols >= X_GRANULARITY) ? (cols / X_GRANULARITY)` without `static_assert(cols % X_GRANULARITY == 0)`
- models/**: `<args>.<flag> = ` appearing after `create_tt_model(`/`Transformer(` in the same function; `self.<flag> = configuration.<flag>` in __init__; generator_vllm.py files that build ModelArgs without the override their sibling model's generator has
- Python: absolute `from (tests|models)\.[a-z0-9_.]+ import` whose dotted path has no file; imports under `if is_blackhole():`/arch branches; function-local `from .<mod> import` inside `try:` with `except Exception` that only logs
- Python helper pairs that name the same logical parameter differently (e.g. `kvpe_cache` vs `tt_kvpe_cache`); callers passing keywords missing from the callee's ast.FunctionDef; `obj._private` / `object.__getattribute__(self, f"_{name}")._x` on another module's class
- Factories with defaulted `dtype=`/`layout=` whose output a newer consumer asserts on; tests comparing `ttnn.to_torch(...)` of ttnn.uint32/uint16 tensors against signed goldens via torch.equal/allclose with no `.to(...)`
- tools/profiler + hostdev headers: hex masks / small shifts (`& 0x7FF...`, `<< 3) |`) applied to packed ID words next to a header that defines *_MASK/*_SHIFT; `GET_MAILBOX_ADDRESS_DEV(<field>)` paths after a struct move
- Multi-executable examples (tt-train 3-tier worker/aggregator/optimizer vs main): `vocab_size =` and `round_up_to_tile` per executable, diffing the value source and divisor (num_devices vs tp_size)

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx and tt-2xx/quasar NoC nonblocking APIs (v1/v2/v3, per-arch copies); tt_metal/hw/inc/api/dataflow + api/debug (watcher sanitizer, pause, noc_logging); tt_metal/tt-llk per-arch common/llk_lib helpers and experimental/ forks; hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api SFPU composites; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa* (prefill/decode/ring-joint, plus experimental/quasar copies); models/tt_transformers shared modules reused by models/demos/* (qwen*_vl, gemma, deepseek) and their demo/vLLM generator paths; tests/ and models/ Python imports after directory reorganizations (sweep_framework, device_perf_tests, demos); tt_metal/impl/kernels + jit_build (per-kernel-type path handling, hashing); tt-train/sources/examples/nano_gpt 3tier vs main worker

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- The fix covered the variant that failed, not its family. A register write added to one-shot paths skipped the with_state/inline pair, and a pipeline drain added in one arch file or PR never reached the other arch's twin. Before closing, enumerate every variant and arch copy.
- Follow-up commits kept finding more of the same miss (more stale importers after a test move, more uint-vs-int tests after a dtype contract change, the t3000 copy of a fixed blackhole test, a missed vLLM generator). Grep for the pattern repo-wide, not just the file that failed in CI.
- A fix can break the next invariant. Inserting a reconfig between a config override and its use tripped an LLK assert, and a consumer later switched from reading a flag per call to latching it in __init__, which silently disabled overrides applied after construction. Re-check ordering whenever a read moves from call time to construction time.
- Semantic merge races (a rename or param removal merged alongside a PR that adds callers of the old form) end in same-day reverts. Rebase on latest main and build all JIT kernels and default-off test dirs before merge. Make old APIs fail at compile time.
- Fixes that remove the immediate caller often leave the trap in place: a defaulted factory argument, a helper pair with divergent param names, a broad except that masks ImportError, a hash keyed on the unresolved input string, or no static_assert on divisibility. Harden the contract (required arg, static_assert, shared constant), not just the call site.
- Hardware-behavioral siblings can be arch-inverted. An identical-looking line on another arch is not automatically the same bug, so check the counterpart's behavior (or measure) before propagating a fix.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/cmath_common.h:156 — move_d2a_fixed_face (and move_d2b_fixed_face at :165) wait only on the source-bank valid bit, with no FPU-pipeline drain. The BH twins and the neighbouring WH helper have the drain. Reached via WH binary dest-reuse. A hardware run on shipped code showed no failure, so confirm the WH pipeline behavior before changing it.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa_decode/device/sdpa_decode_program_factory.cpp:410 — DHT_GRANULARITY comes from DHt alone (a power of two, min with dst_size), but the decode kernel uses it with vDHt as cols. vDHt not a multiple of it (e.g. 16/12) would silently drop tiles per row. The prefill factory already checks both divisors.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transformer/sdpa_decode/device/sdpa_decode_program_factory.cpp:422 — the Quasar copy has the same granularity derived from DHt alone.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa/device/kernels/compute/compute_common.hpp:448 — `cols / DHT_GRANULARITY` has no static_assert(cols % DHT_GRANULARITY == 0), so a host-side regression drops tiles instead of failing to compile (same gap in the Quasar compute_common copies).
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/dataflow/dataflow_api.h:2586 — noc_async_write_one_packet_with_trid_with_state issues on the caller's cmd_buf, but its sanitizer macro reads write_cmd_buf. Fabric callers pass a different buffer, so the watcher checks stale state. - models/demos/qwen3_vl/tt/generator_vllm.py:99 — builds a plain ModelArgs with no use_qk_fused=False override, although its rotary setup ignores the flag. The qwen25_vl generator (:83) needed that override and has it.

- tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp:673 — compute_hash hashes the caller-supplied source_ string, not the resolved path_. The same relative name resolving to different files (different CWD vs root) can collide on one cached binary.
- tests/sweep_framework/sweeps/normalization/batch_norm/batch_norm.py:20 — imports tests.ttnn.unit_tests...backward.utility_funcs, which now lives under tests/ttnn/nightly/..., so the module fails to load.
