## `data-race`: Unordered publish, reset and reuse across agents

Two agents touch the same word, register, buffer or container, and nothing orders them. The agents can be host threads, RISCs, TRISC threads, cores, devices or processes. On device the same few shapes keep coming back. (1) A credit, flag or semaphore is published before its data is committed: a NoC write followed by an atomic inc with the barrier after the inc, a RISC store followed by a CB push, or a multi-word message whose ready flag travels inline with its payload. (2) A source slot is overwritten or popped while an async engine (eth send, NoC write, packer L1-accumulate) is still reading it. (3) A local plain store resets or read-modify-writes a counter that remote agents increment atomically: a per-batch set(0), an init()-time zero, a `+=` after the go signal. The increment is lost and the waiter hangs. (4) A shared per-core register or config field is written by a thread other than the one that consumes it, or blindly cleared by common init code that every thread runs. (5) An instruction with no hardware ownership gate, such as a Dest-to-Src move, checks a bank or valid state that a still-in-flight instruction will flip. On the host the causes are: removing or bypassing a serializing layer without locking every entry point, locks that let references escape or that cover only some methods, check-then-act caches, teardown that mutates worker-owned state before join(), mutable static variables shared by worker threads, and parallel per-device init through a driver that is not thread-safe. These races almost always depend on timing. They vanish under watcher, DPRINT, the profiler or an added delay, and delay-based 'fixes' only hide them.

**Checks**

- Semaphore reset or RMW vs remote increments: for every counter or semaphore that another core or device increments with a NoC or fabric atomic, find every local plain store (set(0), `*p = 0`, `+=`). Flag any that can run after the local agent has sent the go signal, credit or data that lets the remote agent increment again, including resets inside batch or iteration loops, init()/prologue zeroing, and resets of persistent or global semaphores reused across launches. Safe idioms: a monotonic wait target with one reset at exit, an atomic subtract of the consumed amount, or rotating semaphores.
- Credit after data: for every handshake, the credit (semaphore inc, CB push, flag write) must be issued after the data is committed. A NoC write needs a write barrier BETWEEN the write and the atomic inc, not after the inc. RISC L1 stores need a same-address load-back before cb_push_back. A host message needs the flag written last in a separately ordered write (fence, strict mapping or barrier). Never rely on one struct write staying atomic once the struct can exceed the transport's write size.
- Source reuse under async engines: every L1 slot that is the SOURCE of an eth send, a NoC write or a packer L1-accumulate must be provably consumed (write flush, txq idle, CB push/wait round trip, packer stall) before it is overwritten, popped, re-packed or reused for a second logical message. A persistent datamover must drain all outstanding handshakes before it exits.
- Cross-thread config ownership on a Tensix core: for each config, debug or mode register field, find the unit that reads it and every thread that writes it. Flag writes from a thread other than the consumer that have no drain or semaphore ordering, especially on paths where the consumer never waits on the writer's data (SFPU-only ops, fill then pack). Flag whole-register blind writes or clears in init code that all threads run, and any set in init with no matching restore. When a setter moves to another thread, check that its clear moved with it.
- Ungated Src-bank writers: every Dest-to-Src move (and any Src writer without a hardware ownership gate) needs a STALLWAIT on bank-valid PLUS the math-pipeline-idle condition, because a still-in-flight FPU op flips the bank pointer late. Add an SFPU drain when Dest may have been SFPU-written. The unpacker's dummy valid-publish must gate on its own bank. Then diff the WH, BH and Quasar copies of each helper: a drain present on one arch and missing on another is the most common live sibling.
- Anonymous counters with several producers: when several producers increment one semaphore, a wait of the form 'count >= i+1' followed by reading producer i's data is wrong. Wait for the total first, or use one semaphore or flag per producer. An exact-match wait (== N) on a counter that can overshoot (free-running producer, two signal streams on one counter, a lapping multicast) deadlocks; use >= instead.
- Multi-TRISC shared values: a value that drives control flow in compute (a skip flag, a count, a pointer read from L1 or a CB) must be sampled by one thread and broadcast by mailbox, or must provably not change between the three threads' reads. Flag each thread dereferencing the same mutable L1 word on its own, Tensix semaphores accessed over MMIO by more than one TRISC, and 'fixed with a delay' comments. A value that a flag says is 'written after all threads drained' must be written by the last thread only, not emitted outside the per-thread ifdefs.
- Host lock coverage: for a class with a mutex or lock_api_function_, diff the methods that take the lock against ALL public or override methods that mutate the guarded members, including begin/end and record/replay pairs and the FD vs SD sibling implementations. Flag any accessor that returns a reference or iterator out of the lock scope, and any serialization layer (worker queue) that other entry points bypass (finish/synchronize bindings, ops registered with the plain wrapper).
- Caches, static variables and counters: a function-static or global map must do lookup, construct and insert as one atomic claim. No reference into it may escape while another thread can assign to it. A throw during construction must release the slot and wake waiters, or every later caller deadlocks. Mutable non-thread_local static variables reached from worker threads need a lock. An ID must be taken from the return value of the same fetch_add that advanced the counter. Per-hart firmware state on multi-hart images must be thread_local or indexed by processor id.
- Teardown and parallel bring-up: in destructors or shutdown paths of classes that own threads, only read-only inspection may happen before signal+join(). Clear, store(0) and pop must come after the join. For per-device async fan-out in init, reset or teardown, every driver or cluster call inside the lambda must be proven thread-safe across chips. A serializing 'revert' with no named root cause is a workaround, and sibling phases still at risk should be checked.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `noc_semaphore_set(<sem>, 0)` / `.set(0)` / `*sem = 0` inside a for/while body or init(), where <sem> is also the target of `noc_semaphore_inc` / `fabric_*_atomic_inc` from another core or device (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl, experimental/ccl, fusion codegen barrier.py)
- `noc_semaphore_wait_min(X, N); noc_semaphore_set(X, 0)` on a GlobalSemaphore at kernel exit, together with host `do_init_barrier = !persistent_output_tensor.has_value()` (all_gather / high_bw / reduce_scatter factories and readers)
- `async_write(...)` or `noc_async_write*` immediately followed by `noc_semaphore_inc`/`.up(` with `async_write_barrier()` only after the inc; `cb_pop_front` right after `noc_async_write*` with no barrier (ttnn dataflow kernels)
- `*worker_completion_sem_addr(...) +=` or other plain RMW on a worker-incremented counter after a go-signal multicast; a duplicated fake-ack block that passes `first_stream_used` in one copy and `stream` in the other (tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels/cq_dispatch*.cpp, Quasar #ifdef paths)
- `TTI_STALLWAIT(p_stall::STALL_MATH, p_stall::SRC[AB]_VLD)` without `p_stall::MATH` directly before `TTI_MOVD2A/MOVD2B`; `WAIT_SFPU` present in the BH copy of a file but not the WH copy (tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_{wormhole_b0,blackhole,quasar}/{common/inc/cmath_common.h,llk_lib/**})
- `cfg_reg_rmw_tensix<ALU_(ACC_CTRL|ROUNDING_MODE|FORMAT_SPEC)_*` or `reg_write(RISCV_DEBUG_REG_*` inside unpack/pack files (cunpack_common.h, llk_unpack_*.h, cpack_common.h) or firmware trisck/brisc init: cross-thread writes to fields another engine consumes
- `NOC_CMD_BUF_WRITE_REG(noc, cmd_buf, NOC_(PACKET_TAG|AT_LEN_BE|CTRL)` with no dominating `while (!noc_cmd_buf_ready(` on the same cmd_buf, e.g. setup writes before a loop whose body waits (tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx/*/noc_nonblocking_api.h); plus `inc_noc_counter_val` placed after the send
- `pack_tile<true>(..., slot)` in a K-block loop with L1_ACC enabled, one reserve_back before the loop and one push_back after it, and no packer STALLWAIT or CB round trip at the block boundary (ttnn matmul / moe fused_swiglu / post_combine_reduce compute)
- Host: `static (std::)?(vector|unordered_map|map|set)<` without const/thread_local/mutex; `lock_guard` in a function returning `T&` or an iterator; `~X()` bodies calling clear()/store(0) before `.join()`; `std::async`/`detail::async` lambdas per device that call write_core/reset_cores/clear_*_state (tt_metal/distributed, impl/device/firmware, llrt, jit_build)
- `t6_semaphore_post<p_stall::NONE>` or a semaphore post inside a producer helper that is followed by more in-place writes to the same CB; split MOP `run()` called twice with a wait only before the second (tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute/experimental/sdpa.h, sdpa compute_streaming.hpp)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl and experimental/ccl kernels (semaphore reset/credit ordering across devices, persistent global semaphores); tt_metal/tt-llk/* llk_lib and common/inc (Dest-to-Src moves, cross-TRISC config ownership, WH vs BH vs Quasar drift); tt_metal/hw/firmware (trisck/dm.cc shared registers, reset handshakes, per-hart globals on Quasar) and tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx noc_nonblocking_api.h; tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels (completion counters, go-signal vs counter reset, Quasar-only paths); tt_metal/distributed mesh command queues (FD/SD lock coverage, completion-reader teardown, trace record); tt_metal/impl/device/firmware initializers and llrt (parallel per-device bring-up, launch-message ordering, binary cache); models/demos/deepseek_v3_b1 and experimental deepseek_prefill fused ops (persistent-mode fabric channel sharing, aliased CB/L1 regions, L1-acc reuse); models/experimental/ops/descriptors/fusion/codegen/barrier.py (generated init resets and multi-TRISC signals)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land on one arch or one copy only. The Dest-to-Src MATH drain and the SFPU drain went to BH, but the WH copies are still missing them. The high_bw all_gather reset/init-barrier fix was never applied to the near-copy ttnn all_gather reader. After any fix, grep every arch, variant and metal2 copy of the file.
- Timing workarounds (a delay loop, alignment tricks, serializing a phase, swapping acquire_dst for tile_regs_*, avoiding an op, disabling tests) make the symptom disappear without naming the mechanism. The race comes back when timing or struct size changes, or it stays live in sibling phases. Treat 'stopped reproducing' with no identified mechanism as unfixed.
- Locks were added only partially: only copy/move got a mutex while readers took none, accessors returned references out of the lock scope, record_begin was locked but record_end was not, and checks were downgraded from always-on to debug-only asserts. The guard existed on paper, but the shared state was still reachable unlocked.
- Guards meant to enforce the invariant are dead. A non-aliasing ASSERT compares a byte address to a 16B-word address and can never fire. Loading-mode checks are TT_ASSERT, which compiles out in release. Do not count an assert as protection until its units and build mode have been checked.
- A fix removed one symptom and missed a second race underneath. Removing the MMIO semaphore exposed per-thread L1 sampling. Replacing append order with a sort fixed nondeterminism but kept the wrong key domain. A 'rank 0 only' fix deadlocked because the guarded work contained a collective. Re-derive the invariant after each fix, and do not stop at the first symptom.
- Error and teardown paths are left out: a claim-slot cache whose construction can throw leaves waiters blocked forever, and a fork after construction reuses 'unique' temp names. Check the exception, fork and shutdown paths of every synchronization fix.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels/cq_dispatch.cpp:1223 — Quasar fake acks do a plain RISC `+=` on the worker-incremented L1 completion counter after the multicast go has already been sent (~:1202), so a concurrent worker NoC atomic increment can be lost and the wait hangs
- tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels/cq_dispatch_subordinate.cpp:446 — the same non-atomic `+=` after go, and it indexes first_stream_used instead of the command's stream (dispatch_d passes `stream`), so on non-default sub-devices the fake acks land on the wrong counter
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/all_gather/device/kernels/unicast_reader.cpp:145 — destructive `noc_semaphore_set(data_valid_ptr, 0)` at exit on a persistent semaphore; combined with all_gather_unicast_factory.cpp:136 (and multicast_factory:163), which skips the init barrier when a persistent output is given, this is the pre-fix high_bw shape (lost next-invocation credits, remote write before local output init)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/llama_all_gather_matmul_async/device/kernels/worker_reader.cpp:105 — noc_semaphore_inc of the receiver's out_ready sem is issued right after the payload async_write, and the write barrier comes only after the inc, so the credit can overtake the data
- models/experimental/ops/descriptors/fusion/codegen/barrier.py:539 — `*compute_done = done` is emitted outside the TRISC_* ifdefs, so PACK signals the coordinator before MATH/UNPACK finish their drains, which contradicts the documented drain-before-signal invariant; :672 also zeroes compute_done from all three TRISCs in init
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/common/inc/cmath_common.h:155 — WH move_d2a_fixed_face waits on SRCA_VLD only, with no MATH drain (BH has MATH|SRCA_VLD); :164 move_d2b_fixed_face is the same; both are live on the dest-reuse eltwise path
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/llk_lib/experimental/llk_math_mul_reduce_scalar.h:126 — WH DEST_TO_SRCB path has srcb_bank_wait but no WAIT_SFPU before MOVD2B, and _llk_math_mul_reduce_column_ (:207) also lacks the WAIT_SFPU that the BH copy has at :129/:219
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx/blackhole/noc_nonblocking_api.h:1413 — ncrisc_noc_read_any_len_with_state writes NOC_AT_LEN_BE before any cmd_buf_ready wait, so it can change the length of an in-flight request on the same cmd buf; also the WH read at wormhole/noc_nonblocking_api.h:1209 and the write variants at BH :1552 / WH :1346
- tt_metal/llrt/llrt.cpp:58 — get_risc_binary claims the slot and then constructs outside the lock; if the ll_api::memory constructor or the callback throws, the slot stays null forever, so current waiters and every later caller for that path block on the condvar
- tt_metal/distributed/fd_mesh_command_queue.cpp:1432 — record_end() takes no lock_api_function_() (its caller end_mesh_trace doesn't either) while record_begin() does; it mutates the cross-CQ shared launch-message state and the sysmem bypass mode
- tt_metal/distributed/mesh_socket_utils.cpp:221 — a function-static unordered_map of exchange-tag counters mutated with ++ and no lock (also :232); a concurrent socket creation corrupts the map or desyncs the tags across ranks (thread_local would be wrong here)
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/ethernet/dataflow_api.h:583 — the non-aliasing ASSERT compares a byte address to a >>4 word address, so it is dead and cannot catch the ack-source aliasing race it guards; the same issue at ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/kernels/edm/erisc_async_datamover.hpp:263


## `tt-mesh-coords`: Logical mesh geometry mistaken for physical, local, global reality

Host and kernel code computes multi-device geometry (ring size, ring index, neighbours, wrap/boundary mode, first-hop direction, multicast ranges, device ids, shard slots) from the wrong source. That source can be a global fabric setting instead of the op's resolved topology. It can be the logical mesh view instead of the physical wiring, which matters when a logical axis folds or turns on a 2D fabric, when a 2-device "ring" collapses, or when an axis has no closing link. It can be a host-local coordinate list instead of the global mesh (multi-host), a gateway or MMIO chip instead of the device the value will be used on, or a hard-coded shape such as 8x4, a mesh size of exactly 32, or a rank-2 MeshCoordinate(0,0). A second recurring mechanism is looping over the full global mesh range and calling a per-device accessor (get_device, get_device_buffer, try_pin) with no ownership filter. On a single host every coordinate is local, so this only fatals on multi-host. A third mechanism is mixing id namespaces or placement models between a producer and a consumer: cluster-major vs row-major ids, ring distance modulo the total device count instead of the axis length, positional zipping of a local-only visitor with an all-coordinates list, or a linear start+rank*len reading data a round-robin writer placed. The usual test shapes (1x1, single host, one straight axis, one cluster, uniform harvesting) make both sides agree, so these defects surface as hangs, wrong-device writes or bad PCC only on Galaxy, multi-host, folded 2D or 1-D/N-D meshes.

**Checks**

- For each multi-device op, trace where topology, ring size and neighbour set are decided. The program factory must use the op's resolved attribute, not a global fabric query or a caller value passed straight through. That attribute must be demoted to what the real axis supports: size 1 or 2, a partial axis, a missing wrap link. Check every entry point (standalone op and fused path), not just one.
- Find every loop over MeshCoordinateRange(mesh.shape()), get_coords(), or a topology's coords that calls a per-device accessor (get_device, get_device_id, get_device_buffer, try_pin, sysmem_manager, MaybeRemote::value). Each needs an is_local filter or a proven local-only input. Compare sibling branches (pinned vs fallback, sharded vs replicated) and check they treat remote coordinates the same way: one path must not fatal while another silently skips.
- When an API that returns device coordinates can return a host-local subset, look for global geometry derived from it: ring size from size() or max+1, index from subtracting a local minimum, '[0][axis]==0' or 'spans 0..extent-1' checks, 'single device' as size()<=1, neighbour existence by list membership. Each must use the global mesh shape or view instead.
- Under 2D fabric, compare the set of connections the host opens (usually logical-axis neighbours from get_neighbors) with the physical first hops the kernel uses (get_next_hop_router_direction per destination). Also check that multicast start/range derived from logical positions still reaches every peer the waiter counts when the logical axis folds or turns. Init/handshake and data traffic must be routed by the same function.
- Every modulo-wrap neighbour or hop computation ((i+1)%N, (i+N-1)%N, (a-b+N)%N) must be guarded by a ring/torus check. Line endpoints must get 'no neighbour'. N must be the dispatch-axis length, not the total device count. On a 2-device axis, forward and backward coincide, so WRAP is wrong.
- Grep for fixed-rank coordinates and shapes in code that accepts any mesh rank: two-argument MeshCoordinate(0,0) defaults, num_rows()/num_cols(), coord[0]*cols+coord[1]. Each should derive its rank from shape.dims() or reject non-2D meshes at the API boundary. Tests must cover 1-D and 3-D meshes, not only 1x1 and RxC.
- Where device ids come from a data tensor (expert mapping, routing table) or a producer's placement, check that both sides use the same id space (row-major linearized, cluster-major, axis index, fabric chip id) and the same placement formula, including rotation or boundary-chip terms. Test references must come from the writer's mapping, not a copy of the consumer's formula.
- When one component configures or queries on behalf of another device (MMIO gateway vs remote chip, coordinator vs member, chip 0 fallback), check that every grid size, bank count, harvesting or NoC-coordinate query uses the device the value will run on. A local chip's logical-to-virtual core coordinate sent to a remote chip is wrong under heterogeneous harvesting.
- Size or shape predicates that switch on placement constraints must use 'multiple of' or '>=' where the intent is 'one or more units', not exact equality (==32). Every copy of the predicate (runtime, offline tool, tests) must match. Count-based choices must count the right set: user-exposed chips, not all enumerated chips including gateways.
- In kernels, 'no connection slot for this direction' guarded only by ASSERT (compiled out in release) followed by an early return or an out-of-bounds .get() turns a host/kernel mismatch into a silent hang. Flag it: it should be a hard error or a host-side validation.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `get_fabric_topology()` inside a program factory or device op, or `operation_attributes.topology` reaching get_neighbors/get_boundary_mode with no get_usable_topology on the way (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl, experimental/ccl/moe, deepseek_prefill)
- `for (... : MeshCoordinateRange(mesh_device->shape()))` or `device_storage().get_coords()` followed by `get_device(`, `get_device_buffer(`, `try_pin(` with no `is_local(` (tt_metal/distributed, tt_metal/impl/tensor, ttnn/core/services, ttnn/core/graph, experimental/fusion)
- `device_storage().get_coords().size()` used as a ring size, or `.size() <= 1` / `> 1` used as a single-device gate (ccl_common.cpp, experimental/indexer_score, sdpa)
- `MeshCoordinate(0, 0)` / `MeshCoordinate{0, 0}` / `value_or(MeshCoordinate{0,0})` / `->num_cols()` / `->num_rows()` / `coord[0]*num_cols()+coord[1]` in code that takes an arbitrary-rank MeshDevice (tt_metal/distributed, data_movement/moe_*)
- `(device_offset > 0) ? get_forward_connection() : get_backward_connection()` under is_fabric_2d; `fabric_multicast_noc_unicast_atomic_inc` with start/range computed from `linearized_mesh_coord / mesh_cols` or `% mesh_cols` (ccl kernels, deepseek_prefill writers, broadcast)
- `get_neighbors(` then `append_fabric_connection_rt_args` over those neighbours in a factory whose kernel indexes `fabric_connections[get_next_hop_router_direction(...)]` or `dir_to_slot[...]` (all_to_all_dispatch/combine, dispatch_metadata)
- `ASSERT(dir_to_slot[...] != DIR_TO_SLOT_EMPTY)` followed by `return;` or `.get(dir_to_slot[...])` in fabric writer kernels
- grep -nE '\((i|index|ring_index|device_index) *\+ *1\) *% *(num_devices|ring_size)' and '(x - linearized_mesh_coord + num_devices) % num_devices' in ccl/moe host and kernel code; check for a topology guard and the axis-length N
- hard-coded mesh literals: `cluster_axis == 0 ? 8 : 4`, `mesh_size() == 32`, `ring_size = <literal>`, and `number_of_devices()` / `all_chip_ids().size()` feeding a mesh shape vs `number_of_user_devices()` (ttnn ccl impls, tt_metal/fabric, tools/scaleout, test fixtures)
- `chunk_start_idx + device_index * Sq|S` in ops that also accept a block-cyclic / rotated KV layout; `/ num_devices` next to ShardTensor2dMesh or a 1D ShardTensorToMesh on a 2D mesh (models/tt_transformers, deepseek models)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/ccl_common.cpp and ccl/common/host/moe_utils.* (topology, ring and neighbour helpers); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/all_to_all_dispatch, all_to_all_combine, experimental/ccl (moe, all_to_all_async_generic, dispatch_metadata); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek_prefill dispatch/combine (FABRIC_2D factories and writers); tt_metal/distributed (mesh_command_queue_base, mesh_buffer, fd_mesh_command_queue trace, sockets, pinned_memory_cache); tt_metal/impl/tensor tensor_apis / distributed_tensor_apis and ttnn/core (tensor printing, graph processor, services, distributed composer); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/indexer_score and transformer/sdpa sparse ops (SP-ring causal geometry); tt_metal/fabric control_plane and tools/scaleout (mesh-size predicates, pinning); models/tt_transformers/tt model_config / attention / mlp / lm_head (1D vs 2D Galaxy sharding)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes usually repair the one branch that crashed, such as the replicated read path, the cluster_axis path or the pinned path. The sibling branches (sharded read/write, non-cluster_axis, non-pinned fallback) keep the same assumption. Sweep every branch of the same API, and check that the branches agree on how they treat remote or odd-rank coordinates.
- Changing an API from global to host-local coordinates, for example to fix a multi-host pinning crash, silently broke downstream consumers that derived ring geometry from that list, and those needed later hang fixes. When a function's return set narrows, grep every consumer for 'first is origin', 'size equals ring size' and similar assumptions in the same change.
- A 2D-fabric fix that moved to the right connection manager kept a straight-line multicast handshake, which later had to be replaced by per-peer routed unicast once folded axes appeared. Fix connection selection and barrier fan-out together, and test on a folded logical view, not just a straight physical axis.
- Workarounds at the call site (a Galaxy-only branch that bypasses a 1D-sized config helper) leave the helper wrong for the next caller. Fixing via a demotion 'at the op boundary' holds only for the fused entry point. Put the invariant in the shared helper, or validate it there.
- Tests can bless the bug: golden files regenerated in bulk after a regression, or a reference that copies the consumer's linear formula instead of the producer's placement. Back mapping tests with invariant checks, and derive references from the writer.
- Rewrites dropped an earlier local-partition intersection step. Driver or submodule bumps that carried the topology fix also brought unrelated breakage and were reverted. Diff the old implementation for filtering steps, and treat vendored bumps as unreviewed code.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/moe/selective_reduce_combine/device/selective_reduce_combine_device_operation.cpp:161 — caller topology passed straight into attributes with no get_usable_topology. The writer comment assumes it was already demoted, which holds only for the fused path, so a Ring on a 2-device or unwired axis gets WRAP.
- tt_metal/distributed/mesh_command_queue_base.cpp:130 — read_sharded_buffer uses num_cols()/num_rows() and a two-argument MeshCoordinate(device_y, device_x), so it fatals on a 1-D or N-D mesh. The sibling of the fixed replicated path.
- tt_metal/distributed/mesh_command_queue_base.cpp:46 — write_sharded_buffer has the same 2-D-only num_cols/num_rows and two-argument coordinate construction.
- tt_metal/distributed/mesh_buffer.cpp:163 — the sharded MeshBuffer shard count uses num_cols()*num_rows(), which is 2-D only.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/moe_expert_token_remap/device/moe_expert_token_remap_program_factory.cpp:150 — value_or(MeshCoordinate{0,0}) plus coord[0]*num_cols()+coord[1] is a rank-2-only flattening.
- tt_metal/distributed/h2d_socket.cpp:956 — an empty descriptor coordinate falls back to rank-2 MeshCoordinate(0, 0), which mismatches a 1-D or N-D mesh. The same shape is at d2h_socket.cpp:908.
- ttnn/core/services/d2h_socket_service.cpp:377 — with no topology, it loops the full MeshCoordinateRange(mesh shape) and calls get_device(coord) with no is_local filter, which fatals on multi-host.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/fusion/device/fusion_semaphore_bank.hpp:97 — iterates the global MeshCoordinateRange and calls get_device_buffer with no is_local check, so it throws if the bank is built on a multi-host mesh.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/ccl_common.cpp:256 — the non-cluster_axis get_topological_dimension returns the host-local get_coords().size(), and the linear index is the position in the local list. Wrong ring geometry on multi-host. get_active_physical_devices (~:453) also builds a host-local list.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/indexer_score/device/indexer_score_host_common.hpp:129 — device_index_for returns 0 when the local get_coords().size()<=1, and max_linearized_rank (indexer_score_device_operation.cpp:39) scans local coords only. Both assume a single host.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/indexer_score/device/kernels/indexer_score_causal_geometry.hpp:74 — the both-axes path still uses linear chunk_start_idx + device_index*Sq against a rotated block-cyclic writer, with no validation rejecting a mid-slab rotated start. sparse_sdpa_msa_device_operation.cpp:247 has the same linear formula.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek_prefill/combine/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_combine.cpp:212 — a missing route or connection slot takes an ASSERT then a silent return, which drops barrier increments for the remaining peers and hangs in release builds. writer_sender_dispatch.cpp has the same shape.


## `tt-program-cache-key`: Under-keyed program and kernel-binary cache keys

A cache (the ttnn program cache, the kernel JIT binary cache, the halo or metadata memo maps, "already compiled" flags) uses a key that does not cover every input that shapes the cached artifact. A hit then replays a program, binary, CB layout, work split or set of compile-time accessor args built for a different call. The result is silent wrong output, a hang, or a fatal "kernel not placed on core" error far from the cause. The dominant mechanism is a hand-written hash (custom compute_program_hash, to_string-based get_hash, config_hash, descriptor hashers) that lists fields by hand. It drops tensor shape (or keys padded shape and volume while kernels read the logical shape), dtype, tile, alignment, buffer type, shard distribution, per-operand broadcast role, optional or preallocated tensor specs, opt level, include paths or newly added struct members. Duplicate or forked copies of a hasher drift apart, and the fix lands on one copy only. Weak hashing is a second mechanism: `seed =` used instead of combining, xor-shift coordinate hashes, or a hash used as the sole identity with no exact-key comparison. A third mechanism is inputs that are not part of the call at all, such as the target device's harvesting, free L1 or allocator state. The inverse bug also occurs: per-call scalars (step, lr) or transient heuristics are left in the key, so the cache splits and grows with every call, and traced serving then recompiles at request time.

**Checks**

- For every custom compute_program_hash, list every value the factory bakes into the program on a miss (compile-time args including TensorAccessorArgs and bank or shard tables, defines, CB sizes and formats, core set and work split, kernel or factory choice). Diff that list against the hash inputs plus whatever override_runtime_arguments rewrites on a hit. Anything neither hashed nor refreshed is a stale-hit bug.
- Flag any hash that keys a tensor by dtype()/memory_config() (or memory_layout() alone) instead of the whole tensor_args or tensor_spec(). Also flag keys on padded_shape() or volume() when the factory reads logical_shape()[-1]/[-2], logical_volume(), tile(), alignment or buffer_type(). Tile padding makes distinct logical shapes alias to one padded shape.
- Diff attribute_names/attribute_values, and any hand-listed hash tuple, against every member of operation_attributes_t, and every descriptor or config hasher against its struct definition, including optional overrides added later (ceil_pad_hw, opt_level, compiler_include_paths, face_geometry, bfp8_pack_precise). A member missing from the tuple is invisible to the cache.
- Find EVERY hasher over the same struct or op: generic_op versus program_descriptors, WH/BH versus Quasar config_hash, mainline versus experimental/quasar forks. Confirm they hash the same field set. Fixes routinely land on one copy only, and a resync can undo them.
- Check whether a custom compute_program_hash exists only to exclude one field or to work around weak mixing. A custom hash opts the op out of exact canonical-key collision resolution (only the op-name prefix is compared), so its whole correctness rests on a 64-bit hash. Prefer hashing the whole struct with the named fields zeroed.
- In hand-written std::hash specialisations and combine chains, confirm each field is mixed into the seed (not assigned with `seed =`). Confirm per-field hashes are not symmetric xor/shift forms. Trace whether the hash reaches a cache that compares hashes only.
- Classify each hashed attribute as code-shaping or runtime-only. Per-call scalars (step, lr, iteration, seed, scale) and transient heuristics derived from free L1, allocator state or timing must stay out of the key. Every runtime-arg slot derived from them, including values such as pow(beta, step), must then be rewritten on every hit.
- For inputs outside the call arguments (target device harvesting and grid, arch, rtoptions, free L1, lowest occupied address, IRAM or other build toggles), check that each feeds the key if it changes codegen or factory choice. Check that per-object compiled flags and binary vectors are keyed per device or build key, not held as a single bool.
- For multi-operand or broadcasting ops, and for every overload feeding one device op, check that per-operand role and shape (which operand broadcasts, scalar versus tensor regime, even or uneven sharding) is in the key. Check that each overload populates the same key fields.
- For traced or pre-warmed paths, check that the warmup enumerates the exact key set, using the model's real block or page size. Check that request inputs (page-table width, fill width) are normalised to the captured shape. On descriptor cache-hit paths, prefer an assertion that the re-derived core set matches the cached program's core set.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rn 'compute_program_hash(' ttnn/cpp: bodies calling hash_operation<...>(... .dtype(), .memory_config() ...) with no tensor_args, tensor_spec(), logical_shape() or tile()
- compute_program_hash that uses padded_shape()/volume() while the factory derives SRC_BCAST/ROW_BCAST defines or row strides from logical_shape()[-1]/[-2] (eltwise, data_movement)
- memory_config().memory_layout() without buffer_type() or shard_spec in hash argument lists (eltwise, topk family, legacy ops)
- hash_objects_with_default_seed(kernel.kernel_source ...): list every copy (tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptors.cpp, generic_op, deepseek moe builder) and compare against KernelDescriptor/CBFormatDescriptor/CBDescriptor/SemaphoreDescriptor fields
- config_hash() const overrides and Kernel::compute_hash in tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp: compare against DataMovementConfig/ComputeConfig/EthernetConfig members and what set_build_options copies into JitBuildOptions
- std::hash<std::string>{}(to_string()) or get_hash() whose to_string field list is shorter than the struct (sliding_window config feeding pool, halo and conv caches)
- `seed = ` inside a hash-combine chain; `std::hash<..>(x) ^ (std::hash<..>(y) << 1)` in std::hash specialisations used by op hashes
- operation_attributes_t with step/lr/iteration/seed/scale and no attribute_names or custom hash (moreh optimizers); fields computed from is_enough_space()/free L1 stored in hashed attributes (tilize/untilize families and their quasar mirrors)
- TensorAccessorArgs<prev.next_compile_time_args_offset()>() for a second accessor that shares one common-runtime-arg vector with the first but gets no CRTA offset (binary_ng dataflow readers)
- `is_sharded()` followed by `shard_spec()->` with no has_value() check in worker-grid or router helpers (ternary and binary_ng device ops)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise (binary_ng, ternary, unary, unary_backward); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (slice, concat, reshape_view, reshard, interleaved_to_sharded, tilize/untilize); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar (forked ops that miss mainline hash fixes); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/generic and tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptors.cpp (descriptor hashers); tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp and tt_metal/impl/program (kernel compile hash, config_hash, build key); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl and transformer/sdpa (hand-listed attribute hashes); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh (per-call scalars in the key); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/sliding_window and pool (to_string-based config hash)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- The fix landed on one copy of a duplicated hasher, and the copy that actually keys the cache (generic_op versus program_descriptors, or a Quasar fork versus mainline) stayed broken. Grep for every hasher of the struct and every forked op tree, and re-check forks after each resync, which can re-introduce removed fields.
- Replacing a whole-struct hash with a hand-enumerated list, to exclude one per-call field or to work around weak mixing, repeatedly dropped shape, dtype or optional tensors. It also opts the op out of exact-key collision resolution. The durable fix is to hash everything and zero or exclude only the named fields.
- Shape-keying fixes went partial in stages: volume, then logical H/W, then alignment/tile, then outer-dim factorisation. Each fix closed only the aliasing pair in its test. Enumerate every property the factory reads, not only the property the reproducer varied.
- Refactors of override_runtime_arguments (for example a migration to descriptors) silently dropped refreshes that the key had relied on, which reopened a stale-hit hole. When a cache-hit path is rewritten, diff what it used to refresh against the key.
- Excluding per-call scalars from the key fixed cache growth, but it left derived runtime values (step-dependent exponents, second kernel groups) unpatched, or the replacement hash lost the shapes. Pair every exclusion with a check that each dependent runtime-arg slot is rewritten on a hit.
- Moving a heuristic into hashed attributes fixed staleness but left dead or unread heuristic fields splitting the cache in sibling ops. Every hashed field should be read by selection, the factory, validation or output-spec code.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/generic/device/generic_op_device_operation.cpp:53 — the program-cache hash_kernel omits kernel.opt_level and kernel.compiler_include_paths, both of which are used to build the kernel
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/generic/device/generic_op_device_operation.cpp:71 — hash_cb_format_descriptor omits face_geometry, which feeds the JIT CB tile/face build options
- tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp:531 — the WH/BH ComputeKernel::config_hash omits bfp8_pack_precise (the Quasar variant at ~1618 includes it), yet the flag is copied into build_options and changes the generated pack format
- tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp:560 — neither Kernel::compute_hash nor config_hash includes the per-kernel opt_level, which is applied as -O flags, so kernels that differ only in opt level share a binary
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/sliding_window/sliding_window.cpp:1283 — to_string() (the source of get_hash) omits the ceil_pad_hw override, which is returned by get_ceil_pad_hw and fed to pool kernels, so DRAM-sliced pool slices can collide in the program and halo caches
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/llama_reduce_scatter_create_heads/device/llama_reduce_scatter_create_heads_device_op.cpp:126 — the custom hash keys input dtype, memory config and device id but not logical shape, subdevice_id or qkv_memory_config, all of which the factory uses
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/slice/device/slice_device_operation.cpp:306 — the custom hash omits end_tensor and preallocated_output specs, which mainline slice later added because they are baked into accessor compile args
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/slice/device/slice_device_operation.cpp:136 — the preallocated output is validated only by padded_shape against the logical spec, with no layout, dtype or tile check (the pre-fix mainline form)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/binary_ng/device/binary_ng_utils.cpp:769 — the native-L1-sharding scalar and subtile-broadcast branches return true without an L1 buffer-type check or an output shard-spec match (the pre-fix mainline router)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh/moreh_sgd/device/moreh_sgd_device_operation.hpp:15 — the attributes contain lr and there is no custom hash or attribute_names, so every distinct lr is a cache miss plus a new program, although lr is only a runtime arg
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/tilize/device/tilize_device_operation_types.hpp:16 — enough_space_width comes from live free L1 and is stored in hashed attributes but never read by selection, which splits the cache (the same holds in the quasar tilize_with_val_padding types.hpp:17)
- ttnn/api/ttnn/mesh_device_operation_adapter.hpp:1177 — any op with a custom compute_program_hash gets only the op-name canonical key, so a 64-bit collision or an under-keyed custom hash becomes a wrong hit with no exact-key fallback


## `tt-runtime-args-override`: Program-cache hits replay stale args and CB addresses

A cached program is reused whenever the program hash matches. Anything the hash leaves out (buffer addresses, globally allocated or sharded CB backing buffers, semaphore addresses, per-call scalars such as slice/token/start indices, shape-derived strides and work splits) must be re-applied on the cache-hit path. The hit path is a hand-written override_runtime_arguments callback or the descriptor/binding framework's fast patcher. It drifts away from the create path in a few recurring ways. (1) Omission: the override is empty, defined but never returned, walks an empty core list, re-points no CBs, skips the mask/bias/scalar CBs, or refreshes only addresses while stale strides, start ids or offsets stay in place. (2) Misplacement: literal arg indices, or positional input_buffers.at(N)/output_tensors[N] indices, go stale after the kernel's arg layout or the op's tensor order changes; one index is written across kernel variants or core roles with different layouts; an output CB gets re-pointed at an input buffer. (3) Wrong guards: compound sharded flags such as `!(a||b||out)`, or an override guard stricter than the create-time condition, skip addresses that still need refreshing. (4) Framework patcher gaps: bindings are resolved by first-occurrence Buffer* identity, so aliased or duplicated tensors collapse onto one slot; an empty resolution is treated as "nothing to patch" on a path that never rebuilds; raw uint32 addresses or compile-time-arg addresses are mixed into a binding-based factory; a tensor held in operation attributes is invisible to the binder. (5) Structural mismatch: create() delegates to another factory while the cache records the first factory, override recomputes the work split with a different formula than the compile-time block count, a set-once API is called on the reuse path, or program-local config tensors are freed after create().

**Checks**

- Diff create against the cache-hit path slot by slot. For every kernel, core role and runtime-arg index that create writes (addresses, start ids, counts, strides, offsets, idle-core zeros), the hit path must write the same field at the same index, under the same condition, over the same core set. If a value is not rewritten, prove it depends only on hashed inputs. A hit path that calls the same builder as create is the robust form.
- For each attribute that compute_program_hash (or attribute_values) omits (slice_index, token_idx, update_idx, batch_offset, semaphores, compute_kernel_config), trace every value derived from it. Runtime args must be rewritten on hit. Anything structural (CB size/format, compile-time args, defines, program shape such as decode vs prefill) must be moved into the hash instead.
- For every CB created with set_globally_allocated_address, a CBDescriptor .buffer/.tensor, or borrowed_from, confirm the hit path re-points that exact CB handle at the SAME tensor role (output CB to output) under the same sharding condition. Watch for CBHandle defaulting to 0, CBs created with no handle kept, and optional mask/bias/gamma CBs.
- Expand every guard around an address write per operand. An address may be skipped only when THAT buffer is served by another mechanism (such as a sharded CB). Flag `!(a_sharded || b_sharded || out_sharded)` and any override guard that is stricter than the create-time condition for adding the arg.
- Check literal and positional indices on the override: runtime_args[N], input_buffers.at(N), output_tensors[N], tensor_return_value[N]. N must match the kernel's get_arg_val order and the op's CURRENT tensor order, in every kernel variant, layout and core role (worker/hop/idle/sender). Re-check any op that gained an optional or preallocated output, dropped an intermediate, or reordered args. Operator[] bounds are debug-only asserts, so a bad index corrupts memory silently in release.
- In descriptor/binding factories, every kernel-visible device address must enter as a Buffer*/tensor binding drawn from tensor_args or outputs. It must never be a raw uint32, a compile-time arg, a value from operation attributes, or a semaphore address that nothing re-patches. Optional tensors are bound as null when absent. Program-created config tensors must be owned by the cached program (op-owned tensors or workload buffers).
- In any hit path that maps cached bindings back to current tensors by pointer identity, ask what happens when one buffer fills several slots (op(X,X), a dummy tensor reused for several optionals, an in-place output). An empty or ambiguous resolution must force a rebuild or a hard error, never a patch-nothing fast path. Every gate that admits the fast path must imply that address patching succeeded.
- Confirm each factory's create() builds only its own program. All kernel-set or layout-changing branches must live in select_program_factory or the hash, so that the override the cache pairs with a program matches how it was built. Work-split math in the override must come from the same function or saved shared_variables as the compile-time block counts.
- On reuse paths, check each API's precondition. Set-once or create APIs (SetCommonRuntimeArgs, CB constructors with alignment/bounds checks) must not be reached a second time, and checks done at construction (offset alignment, offset+size within bank) must be repeated when a new offset or size is applied.
- Only a test that runs the op twice with the program cache on and freshly allocated, simultaneously live tensors in different slots will catch these (include aliased and mixed sharded/interleaved cases). Treat a model-level fix that works only by resizing a buffer or disabling the cache as masking an override bug.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `runtime_args\[[0-9]+\] =` or `GetRuntimeArgs(program, id)[x][y][N] =` inside override_runtime_arguments* / cache-hit lambdas in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/device/*program_factory*.cpp; compare with create's push_back/emplace order and the kernel's get_arg_val
- `if (!(x_sharded || y_sharded` / `if (not src1_sharded)` wrapping `->address()` writes in override bodies (matmul factories, CCL fused matmul copies)
- Per file: count set_globally_allocated_address vs UpdateDynamicCircularBufferAddress; `CBHandle .* = 0;` created conditionally; UpdateDynamicCircularBufferAddress(program, cb_out, *src...) role swaps
- `input_buffers.at(N)` / `output_tensors.at(N)` / `tensor_return_value[N]` in overrides of ops that accept optional preallocated outputs or fused multi-output ops (experimental/ccl, moreh, data_movement)
- `override_runtime_arg[a-z_]* = [` defined but factory returns `{.program = std::move(program)}`; empty override bodies; shared_variables designated initializers missing the core list the override iterates
- `return ResolvedBindings{}`, `std::find(tensor_buffers.begin()...)` and `if (!sv.resolved_bindings.empty())` with no else-rebuild in ttnn/api/ttnn/mesh_device_operation_adapter.hpp and tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptor_patching.cpp
- `buffer()->address()` or `semaphore.at(i).address()` flowing into compile-time args, *CtArgs structs, or uint32 runtime-arg vectors (lines marked smuggled-rta-ok) in create_descriptor / create_workload_descriptor bodies, especially in helpers shared by fused ops
- compute_program_hash / attribute_values that omit a per-call attribute (slice_index, token_idx, cur_pos, update_idx, semaphore, compute_kernel_config) or reduce shapes to volume, paired with an override that only patches addresses
- `return OtherFactory::create(` inside a *ProgramFactory::create; override bodies that recompute `(n + num_cores - 1) / num_cores` or their own split_work_to_cores
- SetCommonRuntimeArgs / Create* calls reachable from override_runtime_arguments / apply_* reuse paths; set_total_size(N * X_tile_size) where X differs from the CB's page size

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/api/ttnn/mesh_device_operation_adapter.hpp and tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptor*.cpp (descriptor cache-hit fast path, binding resolution); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory (legacy 1D/2D mcast and gather_in0 overrides) plus their experimental/ccl fused copies and experimental/quasar forks; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl and transformer/sdpa ring_joint (global-semaphore and fused all-gather args, WorkloadDescriptor factories); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (slice, sharded_partial, pad, repeat, permute, reshape, concat, tilize/untilize factory selection); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh (positional overrides and raw-address descriptor factories); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/eltwise binary_ng/ternary (coarsened hashes, sharded CB rebinding); sharded ops using globally allocated CBs (softmax, nlp_kv_cache_load_slice, padded_slice)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes are routinely op-local while the defect is in a shared shim. Fixing one op's aliasing or its empty-bindings case left the WorkloadDescriptor branch still skipping patching silently, and other ops (ring-joint SDPA) later hit the same gap.
- Copies of a factory drift. The gather_in0 guard and core-list fixes reached the main 1D factory, but the fused all-gather+matmul copy still stores all cores and keeps the old sharded guard. The Quasar forks mirror legacy overrides verbatim. Sweep every copy after a fix.
- Migrating to descriptor/binding frameworks re-broke invariants the hand-written overrides had held (binary_ng output CB base, moreh raw addresses, rotary token offsets, sdpa chunk_start_idx held in attributes). Each migration needs a two-call cache test and a parity check of the fast path against a rebuild.
- Adding a fixed-index write to a shared override regressed other kernel variants with a different layout (partial vs non-partial, tile vs row-major). It was re-fixed the next day with a variant guard. Named index constants shared with the builder prevent this.
- Narrowing a cache key while patching only addresses opened hash collisions. After the stride fix, Alignment and Tile still had to be added to the key. Treat each key change as a new audit of every derived value.
- Model-level workarounds (resizing a global CB, moving inputs to L1, deallocating early) moved the L1 layout so stale addresses happened to hold usable data. The real override bug remained until someone ran with the program cache disabled.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/api/ttnn/mesh_device_operation_adapter.hpp:660 — WorkloadDescriptor cache-hit branch applies bindings only `if (!sv.resolved_bindings.empty())`, with no rebuild and no fatal. An op whose first miss had duplicate input buffers keeps first-call addresses forever
- tt_metal/impl/program/program_descriptor_patching.cpp:114 — rt-arg bindings are resolved by first-occurrence std::find on Buffer* (CBs at :199). Aliasing at miss time maps several slots onto one tensor, and the hash does not cover buffer identity
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/llama_all_gather_matmul_async/device/llama_1d_mm_fusion.cpp:698 — fused gather_in0 copy stores all_cores_vec (:655, idle/hop cores included) and writes writer_runtime_args[1] on all of them under the old `if (not src1_sharded)` guard. The main 1D factory fixed both
- tt_metal/api/tt-metalium/runtime_args_data.hpp:26 — RuntimeArgsData::operator[] bounds check is TT_ASSERT only, so an override writing a bad index corrupts memory silently in release builds
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa/device/ring_joint_sdpa_program_factory.cpp:3358 — fused all-gather gets the hash-excluded global semaphores baked into worker args. The override (:3417) runs apply_descriptor plus scalar re-apply but never re-patches semaphore addresses, unlike the standalone all-gather override
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/ring_attention_all_gather_async/device/ring_attention_all_gather_async_multi_core_with_workers_program_factory.cpp:370 — halo semaphores.at(2).address() is baked into halo reader/writer args with a 'persistent' waiver, and nothing re-applies it on a hit, even though semaphores are excluded from attribute_values
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa/device/ring_joint_sdpa_program_factory.cpp:3421 — WorkloadDescriptor factory relies on resolve_bindings, and validation does not reject aliased joint/K/V inputs (for example a shared dummy tensor), so bindings come back empty and patching is skipped
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_mcast_2d_program_factory.cpp:3424 — legacy override (used by CCL fused matmuls) updates cb_src2 for any sharded in0. cb_src2 is 0 unless in0 is block-sharded, while height-sharded in0 (c_0, :2805) and L1-sharded in1 (c_1, :2822) are globally allocated with no handle kept
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_mcast_2d_program_factory.cpp:3053 — Quasar fork of the same override. c_0/c_1 are globally allocated at :2432/:2449 but never rebound
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/kv_cache/device/update_cache_device_operation.cpp:162 — hash keys only op_type and the tensors, so compute_kernel_config is left out. fp32_dest_acc_en picks the interm CB format and tile size at create time (update_cache_multi_core_program_factory.cpp:153), and that choice is structural and never re-applied
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/generic/device/generic_op_device_operation.cpp:92 — CB hash includes `cb.buffer != nullptr` but not whether cb.tensor is set. A tensor-backed CB and a locally allocated CB can share a cache entry, and the rebind then skips the CB
- tt_metal/impl/host_api/tt_metal.cpp:1813 — UpdateDynamicCircularBufferAddress applies a new address_offset without the constructor's L1-alignment and offset+total_size-within-bank checks (circular_buffer_config.cpp:76-91), so a hit with a larger offset can overrun the backing buffer silently


## `platform-divergence`: Per-arch, per-core, per-build assumptions silently diverging

Code is correct on the one platform it was written or tested on, and a hidden assumption breaks on another arch, core type, board, toolchain, host topology or target mode. The recurring forms are these. (1) A two-way `arch == X ? a : b` choice, or an `is_blackhole`-only branch, where the else side quietly covers every other arch, including ones added later, so a newer arch gets a stale arch's clock, alignment, burst size, grid or load mode. (2) A hardcoded literal where a per-arch or per-device value belongs: 32B alignment masks, grid sizes, NoC or DRAM or PCIe coordinates, chip 0 as "the MMIO chip". (3) One constant or layout shared across core types or across host and device that is valid in only one of them: a barrier address reserved only in the active-eth map, an `#if COMPILE_FOR_*` that changes a struct layout the host sees differently, a link address that differs from the runtime address after IRAM relocation, a 64-bit store into a buffer that is only 4-byte aligned, which traps on RV64 but is split safely on RV32. (4) Several paths that must agree but are maintained separately: production firmware vs a standalone test-harness boot, runtime vs precompile config, mock vs emulator vs silicon, main-tree ops vs their per-arch experimental copies, a filtered accessor vs the raw member. (5) Behaviour that depends on the environment: thread count, core grid, device id or gather order feeding RNG seeds or tie-breaks, or lenient compilers accepting UB such as punned reads, VLAs and uninitialized locals.

**Checks**

- For every arch conditional (`arch() == WORMHOLE_B0 ? ...`, `is_blackhole`, `#ifdef ARCH_BLACKHOLE`) that selects a size, pitch, alignment, clock, bandwidth, burst limit, load mode or grid: list every current arch (WH, BH, Quasar) plus a hypothetical new one, and check what the else or default branch gives each. Prefer a HAL query or an exhaustive per-arch table that throws on an unknown arch.
- When an op has a per-arch or experimental copy (experimental/quasar/*, *_v2, ported model modules), diff its arch branches and fixes against the main-tree version. Fixes routinely land in one copy only.
- Flag numeric alignment literals (31/32/63/64, 0x1f/0x3f, `round_up(x, 32)`, `alignment = 32`) in memory-map bases, allocator math, L1/CB size predictors and DRAM fragment reads. They must derive from the arch's max(L1, DRAM) NoC alignment. Also check each sub-row NoC read (offset +32B, face +512B) against the strictest supported arch's DRAM alignment.
- When one address constant (barrier, scratch, mailbox, return value) is taken from one core type's map but written to a whole class of cores, check that it lies in a reserved hole in EVERY map it touches, and look for a static_assert or HAL overlap check that pins this.
- In headers compiled by both host and device, find `#if` on device-only macros (COMPILE_FOR_*, arch, core type) that change an array bound, a member or a type. Require sizeof/offsetof static_asserts per core type. For buffers with only N-byte field alignment, flag stores or loads through pointer types wider than N on RV64 targets.
- For every hardware endpoint or grid baked in as a literal or per-arch constant (PCIe/DRAM/ARC NoC coords, `CoreCoord(7,7)`, chip id 0), ask whether it varies by board, SKU, harvesting, firmware or topology. Check that the value comes from a runtime descriptor, that any value emitted as a JIT define is part of the build key, and that board-type switches cover every shipping SKU.
- For every non-silicon mode predicate (mock, emulated, simulator, `!= Silicon`, `is_mock_or_emulated()`), check that each call site treats exactly the right modes. The HAL layout, build key and compile flags must match silicon. Skipping launch must not also skip compile. Missing hardware handles must be null-guarded with a working fallback.
- For hardware features that reset to an unsafe default and are disabled by firmware (CSR or chicken bits, caches, FIFO modes), enumerate every boot path: production firmware per core, test harnesses, simulators. Confirm each one applies the same setting, and that code bracketing the feature keys off runtime state, not a build macro.
- Where output must be reproducible, check that seeds, stream offsets, chunk boundaries and tie-break order depend only on logical element position. Thread count, core index, grid-size work split, physical device id and all-gather order must not affect them. Index min/max/eq must run in a dtype whose reduce path is exact over the full index range.
- Treat build flags that silence diagnostics (-Wno-maybe-uninitialized, -Wno-strict-aliasing, -Wno-vla) and arch-gated test shrinkage or skips (`if arch==BH` smaller blocks, early `return` under fast dispatch) as markers of unverified divergence. Hunt the construct the flag or skip hides.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -rnE '\(arch\)? ?== ?tt::ARCH::WORMHOLE_B0\) ?\?' ttnn/cpp ttnn/core: two-way arch ternaries in perf models and rooflines (data_movement/common/common.cpp, operation.cpp). The else branch is 'every other arch'.
- grep -rn 'is_blackhole' ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar and compare with the main-tree factory, which says `is_blackhole || is_quasar`. Also grep for `TT_THROW("Unsupported architecture` in quasar copies that the main tree already handles.
- grep -rnE '\*reinterpret_cast<(const )?(u?int(16|32|64)_t|float)\s*\*>\(&' in host program factories. Compare the pointee width with the variable's declared width (a uint16_t read as uint32_t is an OOB read that goes into compile-time args and hashes).
- grep -rnE '& ~(31|63)|\+ (31|63)\)|\| \((32|64) - 1\)|alignment = 32|round_up\([^,]+, ?32\)' in tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/*/eth_l1_address_map.h, llrt/hal/*, and ttnn size-estimation code.
- `get_dev_addr(HalProgrammableCoreType::ACTIVE_ETH, ...)` whose result is applied to all eth cores (tt_metal/llrt/tt_cluster.cpp barrier params); cross-check against the IDLE_ETH UNRESERVED range.
- `#if defined(COMPILE_FOR_(ERISC|IDLE_ERISC|BRISC|NCRISC|TRISC))` that changes a constexpr count or an array bound in tt_metal/hw/inc/hostdev and hostdevcommon.
- `CoreRange(CoreCoord(0, 0), CoreCoord(<lit>, <lit>))`, `grid_size.x = <lit>`, `get_noc_addr(<lit>, <lit>,`, `chip_id = 0`, `get_driver(0)` in ttnn program factories, kernels and cluster code with no compare against compute_with_storage_grid_size.
- `seed + core_index` / `seed + thread_id` / `seed + device->id()` inside per-core or per-thread setup in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/{rand,uniform,bernoulli,experimental/dropout} and tt-train random.
- Raw `local_mesh_binding_.mesh_ids[0]` next to a filtered accessor in tt_metal/fabric/control_plane.cpp; in general, a raw member read in a class that also exposes a user-visibility-filtered getter.
- `is_mock_or_emulated()` / `!= tt::TargetDevice::Silicon` / `get_tlb_manager()` without a null check in tt_metal/{llrt,distributed,impl}; `pcie_cores.empty() ? soc_d.grid_size` and per-arch coord tables in tt_metal/jit_build.

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/* (per-arch op copies lagging the main tree); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (sharded, pad, tilize/untilize, common perf/roofline); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-1xx and tt-2xx memory maps, NoC APIs, hostdev shared headers; tt_metal/llrt (HAL per-core tables, tt_cluster barrier/driver setup, rtoptions target modes); tt_metal/jit_build (device defines, build key, precompile vs runtime config); tt_metal/fabric/control_plane.cpp (mesh binding and topology assumptions); ttnn normalization/groupnorm and conv2d (DRAM alignment, L1 predictors); models/common sampling modules and their ported copies (tie-break, sub-core grids)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land in one copy only. The main-tree factory, the partial factory, the experimental per-arch copy, the sharded vs interleaved writer and the v1 vs v2 model module each need the same change. The siblings stayed broken and kept the old `is_blackhole`-only branch or the old hardcoded constant.
- Workarounds that 'make A match B' (a uniform chunk size, disabling a hardware mode, filtering one accessor, deleting the unused op) without explaining the hang leave the real limit latent. Mark them as workarounds and audit the other readers of the raw state.
- A fix changed the explicit arch branch but left a sibling model's `WH ? x : y` ternary, so the stale constant survives and even mixes with the fixed value downstream (cycles computed at one clock, divided by another).
- Moving a hardcoded value into a multi-index table introduced index-order bugs, and the regression test exercised a nearby combination (idle eth, slow dispatch) rather than the targeted one, so nothing would catch a regression.
- Self-check guards were disabled on the diverging arch instead of fixing the model. Even after re-enabling, a later refactor silently dropped half the check (the full-check helper lost its callers).
- Hardware-errata fixes that depend on opt-in annotation or on a build macro (pointer tags, bracketing only via one helper under #ifdef) leak: new code bypasses them. Compiler-leniency fixes were not closed tree-wide because the diagnostic stayed suppressed in the build flags. Some fixes were reverted once silicon changed, so re-verify errata workarounds per silicon revision.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/interleaved_to_sharded/device/interleaved_to_sharded_program_factory.cpp:443 — alignment branches at lines 92/148/443/453 gated on is_blackhole only; the main factory uses `is_blackhole || is_quasar`, so this Quasar copy takes the WH (aligned) path
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/matmul/device/utilities/matmul_utilities.cpp:352 — noc_max_page_size handles only WH/BH and throws on Quasar; the main-tree utility was fixed for Quasar
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/common/common.cpp:320 — device_frequency_hz = WH ? 1e9 : 1.2e9, a stale clock for BH/Quasar; the ternaries at 283/309-315 send every non-WH arch to BH latency/bandwidth
- ttnn/core/operation.cpp:48 — `int size_bytes` overflows at 2 GiB or more, and a decimal GB/s value is divided by 1024^3; line 19 falls back to WH arch and 1.0 GHz when input 0 is a host tensor
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/pad/device/pad_rm_sharded_width_only_program_factory.cpp:111 — uint16_t local read as uint32_t via reinterpret_cast (2-byte OOB stack read into a compile-time arg, which makes the hash nondeterministic); same at experimental/quasar/pad/.../pad_rm_sharded_width_only_program_factory.cpp:84
- tt_metal/llrt/tt_cluster.cpp:499 — the eth L1 barrier address from the ACTIVE_ETH map is applied to all eth cores; on WH it lands inside the idle-eth unreserved range (see wormhole/eth_l1_address_map.h:122) and nothing asserts it is reserved there
- tt_metal/fabric/control_plane.cpp:3008 — raw local_mesh_binding_.mesh_ids[0] used as my_mesh_id, bypassing the user-mesh filter (also 3713, 2930, 2776, and is_local_mesh at 2581); this picks a gateway mesh if one is listed first
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_unary_sharded_gn_rm_gb_v2.cpp:51 — the #else (non-BH) branch does a DRAM half-row read at offset +half_row_bytes; the aligned form is keyed on ARCH_BLACKHOLE only, so any other arch with 64B DRAM alignment breaks
- models/common/modules/sampling/sampling_1d.py:810 — builds the compact tail mask with no sub_core_grids guard (TTSampling disables it under sub-core grids); the slice+concat in _mask_invalid_vocab_tail_logits can misassemble width-sharded logits
- tt_metal/jit_build/jit_device_config.cpp:48 — no PCIe cores silently falls back to grid_size as the PCIe coordinate; line 95 is a separate hardcoded per-arch precompile table that must mirror the soc-descriptor query, and pcie_core is not in the build key (build_env_manager.cpp)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/deepseek_prefill/masked_bincount/device/masked_bincount_program_factory.cpp:37 — fixed 8x8 core grid with no check against compute_with_storage_grid_size; fails on smaller or harvested grids
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/dropout/device/dropout_program_factory.cpp:40 — seed offset by physical device id, so the same logical mesh position gives different masks depending on which devices back it (related: rand/uniform/bernoulli seed + core_index depends on the grid work split)


## `tt-init-uninit`: Init/uninit pairs leaking or skipping persistent config

Every compute op owns some persistent hardware state: unpacker geometry (tile descriptor Z/Y dims, Tile_x_dim, strides, x-end), format registers (SrcA/SrcB/ALU/packer), sticky mode bits (within-face transpose, tilize mode, zero-substitution flag, packer read counters, the SFPU control register), SFPU programmable constants and MOP loop counts. None of it is reset between ops, and much of it survives across programs until a device reset. Bugs come in four shapes. (1) An init does not program state its execute path reads. It relies on hw_configure, which runs once, or on a previous op. It may call a generic init instead of the op-specific one, forward defaulted or hardcoded dims or CB ids, or use a predicate that differs from the MOP builder's. (2) An uninit is not the exact inverse of its init. It may restore too little (an empty stub, a plain uninit after a _with_dt init, a missing packer-mode restore), write registers the init never touched (an accidental repair that hides another op's leak), or restore a full-tile constant or a stale snapshot instead of the operand's own geometry. (3) The kernel sequences ops badly. An init is hoisted out of a loop and a foreign init runs inside the loop body. The loop back-edge carries the previous iteration's transpose or format. A format-only reconfig is used where face geometry also changed. Startup is programmed for the wrong CB pair or source order. A retarget to an intermediate CB is never restored before the output pack. (4) Arch and branch divergence. A same-named init does different work per arch (for example, BH reciprocal init in approx mode skips the Newton constant). A reconfig branch is empty (non-int8). An RMW mask drops a field that was computed. A skip-if-unchanged key covers only the dst format. Set and clear are done from different TRISCs. The symptoms are order-dependent: the op passes in a sweep and fails when run alone after reset, or fails only after a specific earlier op, with tiny or partial tiles, with mixed dtypes, or on the second outer-loop iteration.

**Checks**

- For each LLK init/uninit pair, per arch and per template branch, diff the set of registers and fields each one writes. The uninit must write exactly what the init wrote. Flag an uninit that writes a register the init never touches, because it masks another op's leak. Flag an uninit that restores a hardcoded 16x16 / 4-face / y=1 constant, or an init-time scratch-GPR snapshot, instead of a value computed from the current operand's face_r_dim, num_faces and format. Flag an empty uninit whose comment says 'nothing to restore' next to an init that writes cfg outside the MOP.
- For every sticky global mode (Haloize/transpose, tileize mode, zero-substitution flag, SFPU control register or SWAP direction, packer reads-per-xy-plane, dest-read-control bits, Dest remap), list the inits that set it to a non-default value. Then show that every other init that can follow in the same kernel without a full hw_configure, including *_init_short, experimental and custom variants, programs it explicitly, even when the value it needs is 0. Where the canonical init calls a reset helper (_configure_default_zero_flag_state_, _init_sfpu_config_reg), any sibling init missing that call is a leak.
- For each state the execute path or MOP reads, confirm the op's own init writes it, not only hw_configure/startup (runs once) or a previous op. Check that the init uses the same predicate as the MOP builder when picking which unit or register to program (e.g. unpack_to_dest AND is_32bit_input), and that the paired unpack init on the other thread is the op-specific one (reduce, not generic AB).
- Trace init parameters through every wrapper layer (compute API -> llk_api -> LLK, and test helpers). Flag any MOP-shaping or geometry argument (ct/rt/kt, transpose, face_r_dim, num_faces, CB id) that is dropped, marked [[maybe_unused]], defaulted, or hardcoded, when the matching execute call takes it at runtime. Flag default values on CB-id parameters of init/uninit APIs.
- SFPU ops: list every programmable constant (vConstFloatPrgm*/vConstIntPrgm*, LREG11-14, LUT or replay slots) read by the body and by every inlined helper (reciprocal Newton, exp, log). Check that the op's own init writes each one under the SAME template conditions the body uses. A classic mismatch is an init keyed on APPROX while the body always runs Newton iterations. Diff the helper init per arch, and flag hardcoded <true> where APPROXIMATION_MODE should be forwarded.
- Compute kernels: walk every loop body to its back-edge. For each LLK call, find the last init or reconfig on the same unit along every path, including the previous iteration's tail. A hoisted init with a foreign init (copy, transpose, bcast, tilize) inside the loop is a bug. After a tilize/untilize, or any switch to a CB with a different face_r_dim, num_faces or tile shape, require a geometry-aware reconfig (reconfig_full_operand / tile-dim reconfig), not a format-only or pack-only reconfig. Watch for matmul reading reversed operands (SrcA <- in1).
- Compute kernels: pair every pack_reconfig_data_format / reconfig_data_format / *_with_dt init that retargets to an intermediate CB with a restore to the next consumer's CB before the next pack or unpack. Check that each reconfig names the CB actually written. Also check that the host predicate selecting the kernel constrains every dtype the kernel assumes equal, including the output and preallocated tensors.
- Check that compute_kernel_hw_startup's (srcA, srcB, out) CBs and SrcOrder match the first real op (tilize vs matmul, Reverse order for matmul), and that any init placed right after startup targets the CB programmed on the register it unpacks into.
- Reconfig/skip logic: every compile-time branch (int8 vs non-int8, skip flags, arch ifdef) must write each register the full configure writes for that format, at the right address and mask. Every field computed into a register union must be in the RMW mask. A skip-if-unchanged key must cover every input the skipped work consumes (format AND tile shape, face dims, page size).
- Global bits set by init and cleared by uninit: record which TRISC writes each, and whether it is written by RISC MMIO or a Tensix instruction. Set and clear on different threads, or a RISC store with no ordering against dependent work, is a timing-dependent leak. A caller that has to pass a mismatched template value to uninit to get correct output is a symptom of this.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- tt_llk_*/llk_lib: `grep -n 'FACE_DIM_16x16\|Tile_x_dim_cntx0\|TileDescriptor_ADDR32 + 1'` inside *_uninit_ bodies; diff against the matching *_init_ writes
- tt_llk_*/llk_lib: RDCFG into SR_UNPACK_*_STATE_* in an init paired with WRCFG from the same GPRs in the uninit (stale snapshot; shared scratch GPRs across untilize/fast-tilize/reduce_custom)
- tt_llk_*/llk_lib/experimental: _llk_math_*_init_* bodies emitting TT_OP_(MVMUL|ELWADD|ELWMUL|ELWSUB|GAPOOL) with no _configure_default_zero_flag_state_ call
- tt_llk_*/llk_lib: `grep -L Haloize_mode` over files defining _llk_unpack_*_init_; cross-check against compute-API *_init_short entry points that bypass hw_configure
- hw/ckernels/*/llk_sfpu: bodies calling sfpu_reciprocal_iter<N> / _sfpu_reciprocal_ / log/exp bodies whose *_init calls sfpu_reciprocal_init<APPROX...> or nothing (BH approx skips vConstFloatPrgm0=2.0)
- hw/ckernels/*/llk_sfpu: reads of vConst(Float|Int)Prgm[0-2] with no matching assignment in the paired init; TTI_SFPCONFIG(<nonzero>, 0xF, ...) without restore on exit
- ttnn kernels/compute: `tilize_init_short_with_dt(` ... `tilize_uninit(` (non-_with_dt), or tilize/untilize helpers followed by matmul/copy on another CB with only `reconfig_data_format(` (no reconfig_full_operand)
- ttnn kernels/compute: `pack_reconfig_data_format(<intermediate cb>)` with no later reconfig back to the output CB before `pack_tile(.., out)`
- ttnn kernels/compute: `pack_untilize_dest_init<` after matmul_block / before tile_regs_commit in the same Dest section (BH toggles Dest remap after data was written)
- hw/ckernels/*/llk_api + hw/inc/api/compute: init/uninit wrappers with `face_r_dim = FACE_R_DIM`, `ocb = 16`-style defaults, `[[maybe_unused]]` geometry params, or llk_*_init called with fewer dims than the execute call

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_{wormhole_b0,blackhole}/llk_lib/llk_unpack_{tilize,untilize,A,AB}.h (teardown geometry, Haloize); tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_*/llk_lib/experimental/ (custom mm/eltwise/reduce inits skipping canonical resets); tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu/ (programmable-constant init vs body, per-arch helper inits); tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_*/common/inc/cpack_common.h and llk_pack_common_api.h (packer reconfig masks and skip keys); tt_metal/hw/inc/api/compute/{tilize,untilize,reduce,matmul}.h (wrapper param forwarding, fused inits); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa*/device/kernels/compute (tilize->matmul geometry transitions); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/matmul/*attn_matmul* and matmul gathered bmm kernels; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/{eltwise/ternary,reduction/topk,data_movement/sort}/device/kernels/compute (loop-carried and pack-retarget leaks)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- A restore added to an uninit for a register its init never writes fixes one sequence by accident and hides another op's leak. Later attempts to parametrize or remove it got reverted after model regressions. Fix the op that leaks, and make the uninit the exact inverse of its init.
- Fixing the invariant in one init leaves identical siblings broken: experimental/custom variants, the other arch's copy, the Quasar port, or the sibling kernel in the same op family. After any init/uninit fix, grep every init that issues the same instruction class, or every kernel with the same op transition.
- Restoring a broader init (full mm_init, mid-kernel hw_startup) makes the symptom go away without naming the register it repairs. It was later found to mask the real stale geometry (wrong root cause), or it stayed in as an admitted workaround. Demand the specific register.
- Changing teardown semantics (e.g. uninit now restores the operand's own geometry instead of a full-tile default) exposes every downstream caller that silently relied on the old reset. Audit all consumers when uninit semantics change.
- Format-only fixes were partial: the first fix inserted a format-only reconfig where face geometry also differed, and needed a follow-up tile-dim-enabled reconfig. Separately, a fix that added a CB parameter with a default value bound the wrong CB until the default was removed.
- Invariants guarded only by LLK_ASSERT are unguarded in production builds. Fixes that were verified only in the direction the bug was reported missed the reverse transition and the loop back-edge.
