## `ordering`: Signal published before payload is visible

Most bugs in this class have one shape. A producer writes a payload (config, L1 stats, command bytes, semaphore init, tile address) and then publishes a signal (GO, doorbell, mailbox, semaphore up, event record, write pointer). Program order is taken as proof that the payload is visible first, but the transport gives no such guarantee. Posted NoC writes to different destinations, write-combined or posted PCIe/TLB writes, RISC stores versus NoC reads, RISC stores to different memory-mapped units, and Tensix-queued config writes versus a RISC release store can all complete out of order. A write barrier does not drain atomics, and a stall that gates later coprocessor instructions does not hold a RISC store. A second family is protocol-order bugs, where the signal count or event does not match the event it claims to guard. Examples: a wait threshold already satisfied by an earlier pre-signal, a "slice ready" signal raised at the top of the loop before that slice's data wait, an event recorded before the caller enqueues the transfer it is meant to fence, a local credit taken before the peer's matching credit, and ids allocated on one thread while commands are emitted on another. A third family is lifecycle order. Examples: a reset or remap of shared device state is ordered only against the resetting queue, fences reach only the devices a workload covers while the host marks the region free for everyone, teardown frees state before stopping executors or turns no-op frees into real device work, an observer callback runs before the state it queries is committed, a peer writes into a consumer's L1 before the consumer has finished its own init, and firmware handoff happens without quiescing the resident firmware. A fourth family is compiler and front-end order: inline asm without a memory clobber, and a mode CSR write that is not serialized before instructions already fetched.

**Checks**

- For every publish point (GO/launch, doorbell, write pointer, mailbox_write, semaphore up/inc, event record, ready flag), list every write the receiver, or any core a started receiver may signal, depends on. Name the primitive that guarantees those writes COMPLETE before the publish. Program order, a flush-only call, or a coprocessor stall mask is not enough. Accept only an ack-based barrier on the same NoC, a store fence (host WC/PCIe), a same-address blocking load/read-back (RISC store), or a RISC-blocking drain (Tensix work before a RISC store).
- Data-movement RISC store to L1 through a volatile pointer, then a remote signal the peer answers with a NoC read, or a local NoC write/multicast whose source is that L1: require a same-address blocking load between them. NoC barriers do not order RISC stores.
- Every NoC atomic (noc_semaphore_inc, Semaphore::up) must be covered by an atomic barrier, not a write barrier, before (a) the same RISC reads that address, (b) a local decision depends on it landing, and (c) EVERY kernel exit, including early returns and else-branches near the end of kernel_main.
- Semaphore-count protocols: write the full increment timeline for each semaphore, including local pre-signals and bootstrap signals. Check that every wait threshold equals the exact cumulative count at the event it guards. A threshold at or below an earlier wait on the same semaphore is a no-op. Check that a signal is raised only after that item's data wait or barrier. Check that saturating decrements can never hit a zero semaphore early.
- State shared across several queues or devices, where commands go only to a subset: check that each fence, stall or drain the host bookkeeping assumes (a region freed, a sync satisfied, a launch-msg reset) reaches the excluded or other members too. Trace include-X, exclude-X-at-wrap, include-X again. Flag 'only queue 0 does it' and comments like 'cores should already be idle' that no code enforces.
- Cross-queue events and deferred work: confirm the event is recorded AFTER the protected op on the same queue, even when a caller, generator or callback enqueues that op. Confirm that monotonic ids are allocated on the same thread or lock as the emission of their command.
- Host-to-device: for payload writes followed by a pointer, flag or reset release, check for an sfence or membar covering every memory type written (DRAM, L1, ETH, remote tunnelled chip). Check that multi-field control values (pointer plus toggle, size plus flag) go out in one write. Check that a struct with a trailing go/valid byte is not assumed to arrive atomically once its size grows. Check that control words use the strict path, not the relaxed/WC path.
- Producer/consumer kernels launched together (router/mux, worker/EDM, dispatcher/workers): check that the producer's first remote write into consumer L1 waits for an explicit 'consumer initialized' signal. Check that snapshot reads are barriered before sending 'open', on the NoC they used. Check that the host clears readiness counters before launch and that the expected count matches the number of signallers.
- Teardown, init and callbacks: stop and join executors before freeing members. Do not move cache clears or releases ahead of device close. Run observers and hooks only after the state is committed. Before resetting a core that runs resident firmware, wait for it to be quiescent and disable its interrupts. Compare each core-type branch against its siblings for a missing step.
- Inline asm that touches memory (vector ld/st, AMOs, fences used as cache invalidates) needs 'm' operands or a "memory" clobber. Any CSR write that changes fetch mode (gathering or fusion) needs the arch's serialization before the dependent instructions. Every writer of the shared resource, including raw record intrinsics and compiler-emitted ones, must run in the safe mode.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- ttnn/**/kernels/dataflow: `volatile .*\*.*\[0\] = ` or `p_.*\[0\] =` followed within ~10 lines by `.up(noc` / `noc_semaphore_inc` / `async_write_multicast(CoreLocalMem<...>(same_ptr)` with no `load_blocking` in between (groupnorm/layernorm/reduction mcast kernels)
- ttnn/**/kernels/dataflow and tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels: `Semaphore<>(...).up(` or `noc_semaphore_inc(` in the last block of kernel_main, or before `return`, with no `async_atomic_barrier`/`noc_async_atomic_barrier` after it. Check sharded or else-branches especially.
- tt_metal/tt-llk/*/llk_lib and hw/inc/api/compute: `cfg_reg_rmw_tensix<` / `TTI_*` then `TTI_STALLWAIT` then `mailbox_write(` with no `tensix_sync()` in between; `semaphore_post(` adjacent to `mailbox_write(`
- ttnn ccl/sdpa fused ops: `wait_min(.*_expected \+ 1)`, `signal_op_sem.up(` / `synchronize_workers_and_signal_op(` at the top of a loop body before `noc_semaphore_wait_min`; `rt_args_idx += N` skip counts after a pushed fused rt-arg block; helper params that default to `= true` and switch on a protocol the consumer must cooperate with
- tt_metal/distributed + tt_metal/impl/program/dispatch.cpp: loops over devices or subgrids not in the workload (`chip_ids_in_workload`, `unused_sub_grids`) that emit only a go signal or counter bump, next to `stall_first`/`stall_before_program`; `add_dispatch_go_signal_mcast` without a preceding BARRIER wait; `reset_launch_msg_state` keyed on `cq_id == 0`
- tt_metal host (llrt, impl/dispatch, impl/debug, firmware initializers): `write_core`/`write_dram_vec`/`memcpy_to_device` followed by `write_reg`/`write32`/deassert/launch or by a `read_core` of the same address, with no `sfence`/membar/read-back loop; `write_core(&zero, sizeof(uint32_t), ...)` on launch or go control words
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api (socket_api.h, remote CB, prefetcher_pipe.h): `bytes_acked +=` / `pages_acked +=` in a wrap or page-size branch without first waiting on the peer's sent counter; `align(write_ptr, page_size)` with no matching credit of the pad on both sides
- tt_metal/hw/ckernels/blackhole and tt-llk blackhole: `TTI_REPLAY\([^)]*, *1\)` or `lltt::record` outside `load_replay_buf`; `csrrs zero, 0x7c0`; `#if defined(ENABLE_GATHERING)` guards; `asm("fence")` / `asm volatile("(vle|vse|amo|fence)...` with no "memory" clobber
- models/**: `record_event(` immediately followed by `return <device tensor>` whose caller then does `.cpu()`/`copy_device_to_host_tensor` on another cq; `sha256(...to_json())` or `hash(memory_config)` used as a persisted cache key
- close()/close_impl()/destructors: `disable_and_clear_*`/`clear()` placed before `devices.reset()`, or `executor.reset()`/join after `allocator_.reset()`/`buffers.clear()`; `track_*`/`notify*` calls placed before `status_ = ALLOCATED` or the address is assigned

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/impl/dispatch and tt_metal/impl/program/dispatch.cpp (go-signal and config sequencing, completion events, host CQ writer); tt_metal/distributed (fd_mesh_command_queue, mesh_workload_utils, sub-device manager, MeshDevice teardown); tt_metal/tt-llk/*/llk_lib and hw/inc/api/compute (cross-TRISC mailbox/semaphore handoffs, config-then-release); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa, sdpa_decode and experimental/ccl (fused-op counting semaphores, atomic-at-exit); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm dataflow kernels (RISC store then remote NoC read or multicast); tt_metal/impl/device/firmware and tt_metal/llrt (host reset/launch ordering, relaxed vs strict writes, resident-firmware handoff); tt_metal/fabric (router/mux init handshake, worker-to-EDM connection open); Blackhole ckernels and tt-llk BH common (gathering-mode CSR serialization vs replay-buffer records)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Reordering two source lines is not a fix. One handoff fix swapped the semaphore post ahead of the mailbox write and needed a follow-up that forced completion with a read-back, because stores to different memory-mapped units retire in any order. Always ask which primitive guarantees COMPLETION.
- A correct store-drain fix (same-address blocking load) was reverted within hours because a simulator flagged it as an unaligned access, and it was never relanded, so the race is live in the shipped tree. When a fix is reverted for a tooling reason, check whether an equivalent form (an aligned 32-bit drain) was ever relanded.
- Fixes applied per kernel or per arch leave siblings. A matmul_block workaround was removed from one kernel but not from the conv copy. A ring wrap-credit fix landed in the remote CB but not in the socket copy. An arch-gated event-ordering fix covered an arch-independent contract. Diff every copy of the primitive after a fix.
- Hardware-mode hazards were closed by turning the mode off globally, not by root cause. The guard is a build macro (the opt-in gathering path), so the invariant holds only in the default build, and raw record intrinsics that bypass the bracket helper remain. The standalone test harness also ran for a long time in a mode the product never ships.
- Reverts sometimes blamed the wrong change. A cache-key break was first attributed to a refactor, but the real cause was a new unconditional field in a serialized config that a model hashes. The model-side hashing was never fixed, so the next new field breaks it again. A teardown hang was avoided by reordering, and the hanging dealloc path was never root-caused.
- Protocol changes that widen a runtime-arg block or add a wait mode were fixed in the live consumers only. Dead or alternate constructors still skip the old width, and a permissive default (split enabled) stays in the helper, so the next new caller silently gets the unsafe protocol.

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_wormhole_b0/llk_lib/llk_fp32_dest_acc.h:74 — math issues Tensix-side cfg RMWs and a STALLWAIT, then RISC mailbox_write MATH_DONE at :76-77 with no tensix_sync. The stall does not hold the RISC store, so unpack/pack can be released before the config lands. Latent, static only.
- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/llk_lib/llk_fp32_dest_acc.h:72 — BH twin: cfg RMW, then STALLWAIT, then mailbox_write release at :74-75 with no RISC-blocking drain.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/kernels/dataflow/welford_reader_mcast_receiver_unary_gn.cpp:183 — volatile RISC stores of the partial mean/var, then reduce_receiver_sem.up at :188 (and a batched up at :206 under GN_DISTRIBUTED_AG), and the sender NoC-reads them. No same-address blocking load. The earlier drain fix was reverted and never relanded.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/kernels/dataflow/welford_reader_mcast_receiver_unary_sharded_gn_v2.cpp:107 — the same store-then-up at :111 with no drain. This is the site the reverted fix covered.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/kernels/dataflow/welford_reader_mcast_sender_unary_gn.cpp:354 — RISC stores of global_result, then async_write_multicast sourced from that same L1 (~:368) with no drain. Same shape, local NoC source read. The sharded_gn_v2 sender (~:217) is analogous.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa_decode/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_decode_all.cpp:457 — sharded-output GQA non-output core: Semaphore::up to the output core, then only async_write_barrier/pop_front before kernel exit. No atomic barrier drains the atomic, and the only atomic barrier in the file is at :368.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/transformer/sdpa_decode/device/kernels/dataflow/writer_decode_all.cpp:505 — the Quasar copy has the same undrained output_sem.up before kernel exit (its atomic barrier is at :415 only).
- tt_metal/impl/device/firmware/risc_firmware_initializer.cpp:1342 — DRAM-core init zeroes the launch read pointer and go index with relaxed write_core, then writes the reset PC with strict write_reg. The fix removed this exact mix from the other core types, and the ordering here is only implied.
- models/demos/deepseek_v3_b1/weights/cache/fingerprint.py:94 — content-hashes MemoryConfig.to_json() into artifact ids (also at :119). Any new unconditionally serialized field silently invalidates every prebuilt cache entry and drops into the rebuild path.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa/device/kernels/dataflow/fused_op_indexer.hpp:36 — the rt-arg constructor skips 2 slots after the 4 ring fields, but the fused block pushed is now 9 wide. Latent: the current includer, ring_joint_sdpa.cpp:165, uses the explicit-args constructor.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/api/socket_api.h:111 — the sender aligns write_ptr up on a non-wrap page-size change without crediting the pad, and the receiver skips the same pad without acking it (the remote-CB version credits both). Occupancy can exceed bytes_sent-bytes_acked and allow an overrun. Needs a two-page-size scenario.

Siblings: ckernel_sfpu_exp.h:212, ckernel_sfpu_rand.h:116, tt-llk blackhole llk_pack_fast_tilize.h:92,100. Latent in the default build.


## `identity-collision`: Distinct things share one key, id or slot

Two things that must stay distinct end up sharing one identity: a hardware id or tag, an L1 address or region, a semaphore, a command buffer, a map key, a cache key, or a parameter name. After that, one side's state overwrites the other's, or a wait is satisfied by the other side's traffic. The identity usually comes from a key that is too narrow for the container's scope. Examples: a logical core coordinate with no core type, a trace id with no manager, a chip id with no context, a leaf name with no parent path, a pointer or address kept after the object is freed, or a bare 64-bit hash with no exact-key compare. Other times the allocator does not know every producer of the id space: implicit id 0, ids that user kernels stamp themselves, ids that are truncated to a narrower width, or ids hoisted out of the per-consumer loop so every consumer gets the same connection. Hand-written memory maps and descriptors collide when region chains add the wrong size, when two copies of the same layout drift apart, or when coordinates alias in hardware. One word used for two purposes (credit plus teardown ack, or init plus exit handshake) collides across phases. The failure is almost always silent: corrupted data, a wrong program returned from the cache, or an intermittent hang.

**Checks**

- For every map, set, registry or cache, list each identity dimension that can vary at runtime inside the container's lifetime or scope: core type, device, context, sub-device manager, mesh coordinate, run or process, parent namespace, or object generation. Check that the key includes each one, or that the container is instantiated per dimension. A function that takes an identity argument and then leaves it out of the key is a strong signal.
- For every allocator of a hardware or runtime id space (NoC transaction ids, semaphores, tile counters, fabric worker channels, config slots, L1 addresses), list every other producer of ids in that space. That includes the implicit default id (often 0), ids stamped directly by users or kernels, loops bounded by a max-id constant, and other subsystems. Check that the sub-ranges are disjoint and that one constant defines each boundary. Check that every entry point that accepts an id validates it, legacy free functions as well as the newest API, and that the check still runs in release builds when a collision would fail silently.
- When one slot or id is chosen for a set of targets (cores, ranges, banks), check that it is free on EVERY target, meaning the union of used ids across all of them. Combining per-subset choices with max or min, or computing next = count * stride, is not enough. Also check that paths taking an explicit id validate it against the ids already in use.
- Find every call that hands out a per-consumer resource (a fabric connection or worker channel, a semaphore, a buffer slot). Check that it runs once per consumer inside the loop that creates the consumers, and is not hoisted above it or passed as a constant index (for example link_idx = 0) to every core.
- For every cache keyed on a hash (a map from uint64 or size_t to a program, workload or binary, or an on-disk directory named after a hash), check four things: there is an exact comparison of the key material; variable-length members are length-delimited; the key includes the producer or op identity; and no leaf falls back to a weak std::hash. For ops with a custom compute_program_hash, which opts out of the exact-key check, compare every field the create path bakes into the program against the key plus the fields the cache-hit override re-applies. Non-Buffer resources such as global semaphores are not auto-patched on a cache hit.
- For keys made from a pointer or device address, compare the object's lifetime with the container's. If the container outlives the object (a capture, cache, tracker or report) and entries are not erased on free, a reused address inherits the stale entry. Use a monotonic unique id. Also flag dedup or grouping keyed by data pointer alone: empty or null placeholders and same-base spans of different lengths all collapse into one group.
- Wherever a memory layout or descriptor is defined in more than one place (linker MEMORY regions, address-map constants, HAL tables, SoC yaml, sim and silicon copies), compute every [base, base+size) from each source. Check for pairwise overlap, check that the sources agree, and check that 'end of memory' means the end of usable memory. In chained maps (A = B + SIZE), check that the SIZE added belongs to region B. Check that coordinates meant to be distinct stay distinct after hardware aliasing.
- For every L1 semaphore or scratch word, list every agent that writes it and each phase that uses it. If one word serves two meanings or phases (flow-control credit and teardown ack, init and exit handshake, status-read scratch and ack), look for a peer's next-phase increment racing a local set(0), an exact-value wait on a count that can overshoot, and a leftover count carried into the next connection.
- Look for maps keyed by a logical core coordinate that are read inside loops over programmable core types or kernel groups. Logical coordinates overlap across core types, so each lookup needs a core-type or capability gate. Similarly, look for maps built by walking a hierarchy and keyed by a leaf name that is unique only within its parent.
- Where cached command-buffer state (set_state followed by with_state) or a named parameter or binding is involved, check whether two symbolic names can resolve to the same physical object under any mode or arch (for example dynamic NOC mode, where the data and register command buffers are the same buffer, or two tensor parameters bound to one tensor). Check that the resolution does not depend on container iteration order.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- grep -n 'set_trid\|_with_trid\|NocOptions::TXN_ID\|NOC_MAX_TRANSACTION_ID\|USER_TXN_ID_MAX' in tt_metal/hw/inc, ttnn kernels and tests: compare each trid loop bound or range with the per-arch reserved runtime pool, and check each entry point for a sanitize call
- static .*compute_program_hash in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/*device_operation*.hpp and *_op.hpp: each hit opts out of exact canonical-key comparison, so diff it against the attribute_values fields and the fields read in the program factory
- unordered_map<(uint64_t|size_t), .*(Program|Workload|Binary|Kernel)> and hasher.update(<string>) inside a loop with no length update, in tt_metal/impl, tt_stl and tt_metal/jit_build
- reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(ptr), ->address() or view_bytes().data() used as a key in member maps that outlive allocations (ttnn/core/graph, tt_metal/distributed, allocator trackers)
- per_core_*.find(core) or unordered_map<CoreCoord, ...> lookups inside for (kg : get_kernel_groups(programmable_core_type_index)) with no get_supports_* gate, in tt_metal/impl/program
- auto conn = get_*connection(...) or a constexpr link_idx placed before a for-each-core or worker loop that feeds append_fabric_connection_rt_args, in ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl, experimental/ccl and point_to_point factories

- #define MEM_*_SCRATCH (MEM_*_SCRATCH + MEM_*_SIZE) chains in dev_mem_map.h, and ORIGIN/LENGTH literals in hw/toolchain/*.ld that duplicate *_BASE constants in address maps, with no static_assert tying them together
- _set_state<...write_cmd_buf> followed by _set_state<...write_reg_cmd_buf>, noc_inline_dw_write or a semaphore set before a _with_state, in kernels that can build with dynamic NOC mode (models/demos/*/unified_kernels)
- Recursive walks over .subgraphs or children that write into unordered_map<std::string, ...> keyed by a leaf name, and 'placed everything' guards that compare counts instead of sets (tools/scaleout, tt_metal/fabric control plane)

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/impl/program (dispatch.cpp, program.cpp: CB/DFB/semaphore slot allocation, kernel-group config, cross-node DFB and prefetcher pipes, device hash); tt_metal/hw/inc dataflow API and Quasar dataflow-buffer runtime (NoC transaction-id and tile-counter id spaces); tt_metal/fabric (EDM/mux worker adapters, control-plane port pairing, physical system discovery MPI tags); ttnn device-operation program cache (custom compute_program_hash ops in experimental/ccl and transformer/sdpa, mesh_device_operation_adapter, tt_stl reflection hashing); tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/*/dev_mem_map.h, eth L1 address maps, hw/toolchain linker scripts, soc_descriptors yaml; tt_metal/distributed and ttnn tensor host buffers (pointer-keyed grouping, HostBuffer type identity, trace tracker per manager); ttnn graph capture and reports (graph_processor, graph_report sqlite ids); Multi-host and scale-out tooling (tools/scaleout cabling_generator, control_plane intermesh allocation)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes often reserve the one colliding value they saw (id 0) and leave the wider overlap alone. User-stamped ids still overlapped the runtime pool, and later the max-id constant, the legacy APIs and their docs still described the old full range. When you split an id space, update every constant, loop bound, doc and entry point that describes it, and put the guard on all APIs, not only the newest class or debug builds.
- Many fixes move the known callers off a hazard and leave the hazardous primitive in place: grouping by data pointer, a flat name-keyed map, a count-only placed-everything check, or the pytest ID hook falling back to str(val). The next caller hits it again. Fix the key or primitive itself, or add an assertion that rejects the colliding input.
- When one dispatch path is fixed, check its siblings. A fast-dispatch fix left slow dispatch packing configs by iteration order, and a merge fix left the fallback in only one caller of the helper. List every writer and reader of the keyed table (fast and slow dispatch, trace, cache-hit override, emulator) and fix them together.
- Collision protection regresses quietly. A later commit brought back a custom program hash, which opted the op out of the exact-key comparison again, and narrowing a key dropped semaphores that no cache-hit path re-applied. When a key or hash changes, re-audit what is keyed and what is re-applied.
- A partial fix combined per-subset answers (max of first-free ids per range) instead of computing one global free set, and needed a second fix. When you replace a pointer comparison with a dereference, keep the null-safety the old check had: a moved-from or default buffer now dereferences null where it used to raise TT_FATAL.
- Per-test patches (replacing callables in one test file) recurred the same day in a sibling file. Fix the shared hook or helper once, and scan the whole tree for the same shape.

On Quasar only [0,7] belongs to users; ids 8 and up belong to the runtime's dataflow-buffer pool.
- tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/tt-2xx/quasar/noc/noc_parameters.h:34 — NOC_MAX_TRANSACTION_ID=0xF disagrees with USER_TXN_ID_MAX=7 in dataflow_buffer_config.h:15. Kernels that loop or static_assert on the max constant reach into the runtime-owned id pool.

## `lifetime`: Device addresses and handles that outlive their owners

Most lifetime bugs here are one mistake: something saves a device address, raw pointer, reference or view, and nothing ties it to the owner of the storage. On the device side, three things bake addresses in and keep using them after the host has freed or reallocated the buffer: a cached program (a program-factory local whose address went into runtime or compile args), a captured trace (buffers allocated after a capture, or lazy per-variant captures while other traces are live), and an async or overlapped op (host drops the input or intermediate before the kernels finish). On the host side, the same thing happens through deferred execution: [&] or `this` captures handed to threads or queues, detach-then-throw timeouts, non-blocking reads that carry only a raw `.data()`, and joinable threads deleted after a "fatal" log that does not abort. Teardown order is its own group. Destructors reach singletons that are already gone or that re-create themselves when called (a parameterless `instance()`), or a parent whose close() reset the allocator. Other forms: `weak_from_this` inside a constructor, and binding keep_alive indices that tie the wrong argument. The last group is value-semantics bugs: a span or view into the object's own buffer that implicit copy/move does not re-point, a reference member bound to a temporary, a view built from a braced temporary, use-after-move, a double free or force-dealloc of storage shared with the caller, and a vector of objects whose interior pointers were saved before the vector reallocated.

**Checks**

- Program factories: for every device buffer or tensor created inside create() (CreateBuffer, MeshBuffer::create, create_device_tensor, to_device, config/index/constant tensors) whose ->address() goes into runtime/compile args, prove the owning handle lives with the cache entry (shared_variables, workload_descriptor.buffers, op_owned_tensors, or captured by value in the override lambda). Then check that override_runtime_arguments rewrites every address that can change, not just src/dst. Test: run the op twice with the program cache on and an allocation in between.
- Traces: list every device allocation reachable after the first begin_trace_capture on a device. That includes persistent inputs, sampling/param tensors, first-time compiles, op-internal lookup tables, per-call slices, and lazy `if x is None: allocate` paths. Flag any allocation that stays alive across replay of an earlier trace, and any capture-on-first-use keyed by seq_len/batch/sampling variant on the request path. Require prepare-all-before-first-capture. Run with the trace-allocation tracker on and a mixed variant sweep.
- Deferred closures: for every lambda passed to a thread, pool, queue, callback registry or detached std::thread, list its [&], `this` and raw-pointer captures, then prove a join or wait happens on EVERY exit path, including exceptions and timeout paths that call detach() and then throw. Non-blocking DMA/read APIs must carry an owning pin (shared_ptr, refcount) alongside any `.data()`/`.get()` pointer they queue.
- Thread shutdown: on a bounded join that times out, the std::thread must not be deleted while it is still joinable or while an async join task still references it. Treat a log_fatal/log_error that returns as if it did not happen.
- Teardown order: in every destructor, close() and release path, list the global or singleton accessors it calls and the parent handles it goes through. Flag parameterless lazily-creating `instance()` calls that can run after destroy_instance, cached raw context pointers whose owner an atexit handler deletes, and paths that check only weak_ptr.lock() and not the parent's is_initialized(). New mode-specific branches (hybrid allocator, mock or remote device) are where the older guard usually goes missing.
- Force-reset APIs (deallocate_all, deallocate_buffers, release_buffers, clear): check that every long-lived holder of handles into that pool is invalidated or cleared, and that each handle's own allocated status is updated. Holders include the program cache, traces, live tensors and ping-pong or persistent-buffer caches. Otherwise a later destructor frees an address that has been handed out again. Buffer caches must re-validate (is_allocated) what they return and must be keyed on every size-affecting property (shape, dtype, layout, topology).
- Self-referential and view-holding types: any class with an owning array or vector plus a span, pointer, string_view or no-ownership adaptor into it must define copy and move so they re-point the view, or delete them. Check every copy site too: postfix ++, operator+, operator[], return by value, swap. Separately, flag members of type reference, string_view or span that are initialized from a ctor parameter a caller can pass as a temporary (char*->std::string conversion, concatenation, braced list, optional.value() in braces).
- Temporaries and moves: flag `const auto&`/`auto&&` bound to std::get<N>/.first/[]/member access on a by-value call result. Flag generic rebuilders `T{f(fields)...}` over aggregates that have reference members, since tensor_args_t-style structs are only safe while used synchronously. Flag any read of x after f(std::move(x)), and std::forward/move of the same pack or capture that can run more than once (mutable lambdas invoked per device, per shard or on retry).
- Ops that free their input and then allocate the output (move, reallocate, in-place ops, concat): count the frees on every path. Handle same-address and overlapping-address outcomes, and compare the buffer type as well as the address. Never deallocate (especially force=true) tensors the op did not create or copies that share caller storage, and never read metadata of a tensor after freeing it.
- Python binding lifetime ties: for each keep_alive<nurse, patient>, count the indices by hand (0 is the return value, 1 is self or the new object). Confirm that the patient is the device or owner, and compare sibling overloads that differ by one inserted parameter.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- *program_factory*.cpp: `CreateBuffer(|Buffer::create(|MeshBuffer::create(|create_device_tensor(|to_device(` in create() whose `->address()` reaches SetRuntimeArgs/compile args while the handle is absent from shared_variables / op_owned_tensors / workload_descriptor.buffers
- models/**: `if self.trace_id_*[key] is None:` or `defaultdict(lambda: None)` trace maps followed by allocation plus begin_trace_capture on the request path; `capture_trace(` without skip_precompile=True after another trace exists; log text 'captured on first use' or 'set up on first decode'

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes often landed on one sibling and missed the twin. The watcher thread-shutdown fix left the identical dprint destructor alone, a conv3d reroute left the multi-core pad factory with the same freed constant, and one op's tensor_args fix left dozens of reference-member structs. After a fix, grep the exact shape across all sibling factories, servers and overloads.
- Workarounds in callers (clearing the program cache in a test fixture, running a conv unsharded, avoiding one launch path) left the unsafe API itself in place. The force-dealloc API still does not invalidate live Buffer handles, and the root cause of the wide sharded conv was never found.
- Trace-corruption fixes kept coming back in pieces: first up-front capture, then prepare/record separation, then tracker fixes, then sampling-variant warmup. Reordering captures cannot protect capture N from traces 1..N-1, and lazy 'capture on first use' fallbacks keep reappearing. Treat any remaining lazy path as unfixed.
- Rewrites quietly undid fixes. An owning string copy turned back into a string_view in the ELF loader rewrite, and a static-destruction regression test was folded into a fixture that closes explicitly, so it no longer tests the scenario. Check that the regression test still exercises the original lifetime, and re-audit rewritten files.
- Fixes that added a keepalive or cache introduced new defects: a per-layer keepalive leaked DRAM, and an unkeyed 'allocate once' intermediate is wrong-shaped for later calls. A PR description listed a fix (validate cached ping-pong buffers) that never made it into the merged commit. Diff the merged code against the claimed fix.
- Removing one ownership edge (a singleton holding shared_ptr to a device) moved the static-destruction UAF onto another singleton (an atexit-deleted context reached through a cached raw pointer). When fixing destruction order, list every global the destructor can still reach.

## `integer-width`: Narrow, wrapping, and mismatched-width integer arithmetic

Five mechanisms keep coming back.

(1) Whole-buffer or whole-tensor byte sizes, offsets and element counts pass through a 32-bit or int hop somewhere between a 64-bit source and its consumer: a struct field, a parameter, a helper signature (align, div_up, round_up) or a loop index. The same happens when a u32*u32 product is evaluated before it is widened. Tensors above 2^31 or 2^32 bytes or elements then silently wrap. The results are zeros, truncated transfers, a wrong heuristic choice, or dead bounds checks, because the guard tests the wrapped product.

(2) Free-running counters are combined at the wrong width. A peer counter that hardware stores in 16 bits gets differenced in 32 bits. A ring or slot index is derived as `counter % N` when N need not divide 2^width, so producer and consumer cursors diverge at the wrap. A wrap-aware signed compare gets a stale or constant target.

(3) Unsigned subtraction underflows when a cursor sits in a gap or padding region, or when a mode-dependent header offset (watcher words) is subtracted from a count that can be zero. The result is a huge length or offset.

(4) Integer semantics break in device SFPU kernels:
- subtract-and-test-sign compares overflow when the operands are 2^31 or more apart;
- sign handling is copied from float code (setsgn/abs) onto two's-complement int32;
- float quotients go through int16 conversions that saturate;
- the kernel assumes one Dest encoding (sign-magnitude or two's complement) while the production caller writes the other;
- INT_MIN has no sign-magnitude form.

(5) Hardware address fields (strides, carried offsets) and hand-rolled arithmetic (magic-number or ported division) are narrower than, or exact only over part of, the domain their signatures claim.

**Checks**

- Trace every byte size, offset, page index and element count from its 64-bit source (buffer()->size(), tensor volume, shape product) to the final consumer, whether that is a command field, a kernel arg, an allocator or a heuristic. Flag any narrower hop: a struct field, a parameter, a return type, a loop index (`auto i = 0`), or a generic helper signature. Also flag any multiply whose operands are both 32-bit before promotion. Widening only the destination variable is not enough.
- Any bounds check (TT_FATAL/ASSERT `x <= cap`) whose x is a 32-bit product can be dead: the wrapped value is small and passes. Check that the product is computed in 64 bits before the guard.
- For each producer/consumer counter pair, find the width each side is really stored and updated at (a hardware register may write only 16 bits). The difference must be truncated or compared at that width, and every path that sets capacity must bound it below 2^width. That includes device-side CB/socket reconfiguration, not only the host validator. Diff the same calculation across arches and across the dataflow and compute copies.
- For every index computed as `free_running_counter % N` or `& (N-1)`, check whether the counter can wrap within its lifetime, including across launches if it persists. If it can, N must be guaranteed to divide 2^width, or the cursor must be stored. Watch for a capacity that used to be a power of two and became configurable, and for one side storing its cursor while the other derives it.
- For wrap-aware compares (signed difference of unsigned counters), the target must be derived from the live counter within half the range. A literal 0, a reset-time snapshot or an unpatched placeholder hangs after 2^31 increments. 'No wait' or 'barrier only' needs an explicit flag.
- For every unsigned `a - b` (cursor minus range start, count minus header or watcher offset), prove a >= b on every path, including padding/gap regions and the 'never set' or empty state. Compare with sibling methods: if one guards with empty()/nullopt and another does not, the unguarded one is the bug.
- For every integer ordered compare, min/max or clamp on vInt/vUInt (v_if(a < b), subtract-then-sign), require an opposite-sign split or MSB fold. Test INT_MIN/INT_MAX and opposite-sign pairs. Check that the Dest Int32 encoding the kernel assumes matches what the production caller writes, not just the test harness default. Check that sibling APIs (min/max, WH/BH/Quasar) decode the scalar the same way.
- For every float-to-int conversion used as floor/trunc in a kernel (float_to_int16, convert<vInt16>), bound its input from the caller-legal operand ranges (tiny divisors, big numerators). For each correction guard after an approximation, check that its condition can actually be true; a truncated value compared with its own source is dead.
- Where hardware computes base + counter*stride, compute the maximum counter*stride for the widest shape and the widest datum (FP32 doubles it). Compare it against the stride-field width and the carried-offset window, and require an assert or a rebase. Defaulted parameters meaning 'legacy path' leave wrapper entry points unfixed.
- In cache-hit override paths, stored handles (CB, kernel, semaphore) must keep the API handle type, not uint32. Diff every size/alignment/offset formula in the override against create. For hand-rolled div/mod or magic multipliers, brute-force or prove them over the declared uint32 domain, or assert the valid range.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `uint32_t \w+ = .*num_pages \* .*page_size` and `uint32_t .*size_bytes = .*buffer\(\)->size\(\)`: host dispatch (tt_metal/impl/buffers), sockets (tt_metal/distributed), ttnn op factories/CCL heuristics
- `uint32_t H = shape[...]` followed by `H \*= shape[i]`, `% \(m \* n\)` with uint32 m,n, `std::uint32_t\{1\}, std::multiplies`: tt_metal/impl/data_format/tilize_utils.cpp and host layout helpers
- `count - watcher_count_word_offset_` / `size\(\) - <offset>` without an empty() guard: tt_metal/impl/kernels
- `(sent|count|counter|wr|rd)\w* *% *\w*(ring|fifo|num_buffers|slots|depth)`: device dataflow/fabric headers (tt_metal/hw/inc/api/dataflow, tt_metal/fabric/hw/inc)
- `fifo_num_pages = fifo_size / ` with no <= 0xFFFF bound; `uint32_t tiles_received` next to a 16-bit peer read: tt_metal/hw/inc/api, llk_io_pack/unpack
- wrap_ge(/stream_wrap_ge( called with a constant or placeholder count: dispatch kernels and program/dispatch.cpp
- `v_if\s*\(\s*\w+ [<>] \w+` on non-constant vInt with no sign split; `setsgn` on vInt; `static_cast<int>\(Converter::as_float|int\(f\)` on integer paths; `float_to_int16\(|convert<sfpi::vInt16>` on quotients: ckernel_sfpu_*.h (tt-llk + metal llk_api, all arches)

- `constexpr .*MAX_UNITS.* = 16` next to `unit_dims\[idx\+\+\]`; `uint16_t \w+ = .*<<` inside templates storing T; `fast_udiv_\d+` magic multipliers with no domain assert (tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/mod_div_lib.h)

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/impl/buffers (dispatch.cpp, buffer_page_mapping.cpp): whole-buffer offsets, page-mapping iterators; tt_metal/impl/data_format/tilize_utils.cpp and host tilize/pad utilities; tt_metal/hw/inc/api (dataflow_api, socket_api, remote_circular_buffer, cross_node_dfb) and llk_io_pack/unpack: CB/ring counter wrap; tt_metal/fabric/hw/inc/edm_fabric adapters and dispatch kernels (cq_dispatch/cq_prefetch): persisted counters, wrap-aware waits; SFPU integer and quotient kernels: tt_metal/tt-llk/*/common/inc/sfpu and tt_metal/hw/ckernels/*/metal/llk_api/llk_sfpu (relu, binary_comp, remainder, fmod, shift); tt-llk experimental pack paths (fast_untilize) and their test sources; ttnn op factories: CCL worker heuristics, cache-hit override_runtime_arguments, sort/index kernels; tt_metal/distributed h2d/d2h sockets; tt_metal/impl/kernels runtime-arg counts

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes that widen the destination variable but leave one operand narrow do not remove the overflow. A loop counter `auto i = 0` or a uint32 dimension still forces a 32-bit multiply, so the fix only moves the threshold from 2^31 to 2^32. Check every multiplicand.
- Later warning-cleanup or narrowing refactors silently undo width fixes (for example, folding outer dims into a uint32 H to fit a uint32 pair type). Pair any narrowing cast with an overflow TT_FATAL, and re-audit files where a width fix previously landed.
- Fixes land in one copy of a duplicated calculation and miss the siblings. Examples: fmod but not remainder, reduce_scatter but not all_gather heuristics, PrefetcherPipe but not CrossNodeDFB, relu_min but not relu_max, WH/BH but not Quasar, the main API but not the `_at_address` wrapper. After any fix, grep for every twin of the same formula.
- Symptom-only workarounds at one caller leave the root underflow in the shared primitive, as when a ttnn helper was patched while Kernel::set_common_runtime_args_count still wraps. Fix the primitive and match its sibling's empty() guard.
- Added correction guards can be unreachable by construction. For example, `trunc(x) > x` never fires. And a fix verified only with the test harness's default encoding (sign-magnitude) was never checked with the two's-complement data production writes. Verify the guard can fire, and test with production-encoded, extreme and negative stimuli.
- Large-size regression tests get reverted when they exceed runner memory. Size them just above the 2^32 threshold, or test the arithmetic directly, rather than dropping coverage. Changing a threshold or capacity can expose a latent core-count or offset mismatch in callers.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- tt_metal/impl/kernels/kernel.cpp:888 — set_common_runtime_args_count does `count - watcher_count_word_offset_` with no empty guard; zero CRTAs with the watcher on gives size()==UINT32_MAX over a null data pointer
- tt_metal/impl/data_format/tilize_utils.cpp:491 — `uint32_t H` accumulates the product of all outer dims (`H *= shape[i]`) with no overflow check before it is packed into PhysicalSize
- tt_metal/impl/data_format/tilize_utils.cpp:502 — `input.size() % (m * n)` with uint32 m,n: the product can wrap, even to 0 (divide by zero)
- tt_metal/distributed/h2d_socket.cpp:861 — `uint32_t num_bytes = num_pages * page_size_` computed before the `num_bytes <= fifo_curr_size_` guard, so a wrapped product passes the guard; same at :880 and d2h_socket.cpp:759/:797
- tt_metal/impl/buffers/dispatch.cpp:845 — uint32 dst_addr adds (device_page_offset - device_start_page)*aligned_page_size, and data_length at :847 is a uint32 num_pages*page_size; latent under the full-buffer mapping; same data_length at :1290 feeds the pinned-region bounds check

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/all_gather_async/device/all_gather_async_default_program_factory.cpp:261 — `uint32_t output_data_size_bytes = buffer()->size()` truncates the 64-bit size (heuristic takes uint32 at :159); same at strided_all_gather_async_program.cpp:284

- tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_blackhole/common/inc/sfpu/ckernel_sfpu_relu.h:60 — _relu_max_impl_ uses a plain vInt `result > threshold` compare with no sign split, and the vInt threshold at :95 is decoded as float bits truncated to int (differs from relu_min); WH twin at the same lines

## `param-combination`: Edge-value and flag combos reaching unguarded paths

Code is written and tested for the common value of each parameter, but some legal combination hits a path that was never exercised. Typical cases: a count of zero (zero-volume tensors, zero-slot channels, zero pages), a dtype other than bf16, a batch above 1, a flag turned on outside CI, or a platform that supports fewer queues or devices. Four mechanisms recur. (1) Degenerate sizes. A size of 0 still yields a placeholder address, a first-element read or a zero-length transfer, and a later write or read at that address corrupts memory or traps. (2) A limit is checked against the wrong quantity. Guards or caps use a nearby but different number: the raw request count instead of the padded or slot-indexed allocation, a fixed kernel constant instead of the model or device memory budget, or the channel-slot size (header included) instead of the maximum payload. They pass for bf16 or small shapes and overflow for other dtype and size pairs. (3) Configuration fan-out. Init code builds state for every possible value of a knob, or a debug flag quietly turns on another subsystem or a sync. That breaks platforms or modes (trace capture, single-CQ clusters) that forbid the extra combination. (4) Workarounds that steer one config away from a broken path leave the path live for every other caller whose defaults still reach it. Guards that return status codes but are tested as bools can make the safety net itself unreachable.

**Checks**

- For every size, count or volume parameter (tensor volume, NUM_BUFFERS/slots template args, page count, packets, CQ count), ask what happens at 0. Look for an op that still launches and writes a tile to a placeholder address-0 buffer, an unconditional [0]/front()/base() read, a `count-1` wrap, or a zero-length NoC or DMA command. Require an `if constexpr (N > 0)`, a skip-launch or early return, or a static_assert.
- Wherever code checks a limit and then allocates or sends, check that the guard uses the SAME quantity that is actually used: the padded, bucketed or max(slot)+1 extent, not the raw count; bytes including header or alignment; per-packet bytes after packing several pages. Check it against the real budget (model chunk size, device memory, fabric max payload), not an unrelated fixed constant.
- In fabric senders, confirm the per-packet cap comes from the max-payload query, not from the channel-buffer size, which includes the packet header. Then walk the dtype x page-size matrix: bf16 bit_floor versus other dtypes used raw, rows longer than the cap, multiple pages per packet. No emitted payload may exceed the max payload.
- Where a dtype-conditional expression chooses a size (e.g. `dtype == BFLOAT16 ? bit_floor(x) : x`), treat the non-bf16 branch as an untested path and verify it separately.
- When init or setup code loops over every possible value of a knob (1..max CQs, links, devices) instead of the configured value, check that every value in the range is supported on every platform. Prefer building only the selected configuration.
- For every debug, report or logging flag, trace its side effects: does it enable another subsystem, issue a device sync or readback, or change a tensor's type? Check each effect under every mode that forbids it (trace capture, async, non-default CQ). Check that a requested output that can't be produced raises instead of silently doing nothing.
- When a fix routes around a path (a flag, fast-path opt-in, or disabled tracing) instead of repairing it, list every other caller or default config that still reaches the bypassed path. Defaults keyed on device count, cluster type or batch size are the usual culprits. Test those callers at batch>1 on multi-chip meshes against a host reference.
- Flag validators that return an enum or status code but are used in a boolean context (`!f()`, `if (f())`, `&&`). If the success code is nonzero, the guard never fires.
- Check that every context manager or push/pop pair that changes global state restores it in `finally`, so an exception inside one combination doesn't leak state into the next.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- `get_tt_fabric_channel_buffer_size_bytes()` assigned to a packet_size / max_packet_size / payload cap variable (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/**/ccl, point_to_point, moe). Mux buffer-size uses are legitimate; payload-cap uses are suspect.
- `dtype == DataType::BFLOAT16 ? std::bit_floor(x) : x` next to a fabric size query (CCL and point-to-point program factories)
- `std::array<.*, NUM_BUFFERS>` with `[0]`, `.base()` or `addresses[0]` reached without `if constexpr (NUM_BUFFERS)`, and `wrap_increment<NUM_BUFFERS>` (tt_metal/fabric/hw/inc/edm_fabric)
- device ops whose program factory has no `skip_launch` or zero-volume early return, especially broadcast, copy and clone variants (ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement, eltwise); `size_ == 0` paths that mark a buffer ALLOCATED (tt_metal/impl/buffers)
- `for (.*= 1; .* <= max_.*; ` building per-configuration managers in device or dispatch init (tt_metal/impl/device, dispatch)
- `padded_batch = ` or `next((b for b in SUPPORTED_PREFILL_BATCH_SIZES if b >= ...` compared only against a fixed `_MAX_*_TOKENS` constant (models/**/generator.py, prefill planners)
- `allow_force_argmax.*(False|num_devices == 1)` and other config defaults keyed on device count (models/tt_transformers/tt/model_config.py, models/demos/*/tt/generator.py)
- `!\s*debug_valid_\w+\(` or `if (validate_...(` on functions returning uint16_t or an enum (tt_metal/hw/inc/internal/debug)
- `@contextmanager` with a bare `yield` outside try/finally; `synchronize_device(` in decorator or hook code not gated on trace-capture state (ttnn/ttnn)
- `ttnn.Tensor(tensor=<torch tensor>` with no `as_subclass(torch.Tensor)` (ttnn/ttnn/operations/core.py golden helpers)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl and experimental/ccl (fabric payload sizing per dtype and page size); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/point_to_point; tt_metal/fabric/hw/inc/edm_fabric (compile-time zero-slot channel templates); ttnn device ops on zero-volume or broadcast inputs (data_movement, eltwise) plus tt_metal/impl/buffers; tt_metal/impl/device and dispatch init (per-CQ or per-platform configuration); models/tt_transformers and models/common (batched prefill planning, sampling config defaults per mesh); ttnn/ttnn python decorators, tracer and report flags

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- The first fix picked a size constant that was 'nearly right': the channel-buffer size instead of the max payload. It only worked for bf16 because of bit_floor, and needed a second fix. Sibling factories that copied the same constant were never updated. When fixing a cap, grep every caller of the wrong constant.
- A batch-size fix counted requests, but rows were placed by caller slot id, so the allocation had to cover max(slot)+1. It also kept a fixed-constant token guard that still allowed OOM. Two follow-up fixes were needed. Fix the guard and the allocation on the same quantity.
- Zero-volume protection was added per-op (opt-in skip_launch) and not at the buffer or launch layer. Zero-size buffers still report ALLOCATED at address 0, so every op without the opt-in is still exposed.
- The zero-slot guard was put in the init callers and not in the accessor (SlotAddresses::base()), so every new caller of the accessor must remember the guard. Guard or specialize at the lowest layer.
- Routing around a broken path (forcing argmax for greedy decode) was copied into other models and never root-caused. Its stated rationale was disproven by the fix's own experiments, and default configs for other models still reach the broken path.
- A fix that looped over all configurations was reverted the next day because it broke a platform with narrower support. A later refactor closed it structurally by configuring only the selected value. Before landing, verify fixes on the platform with the narrowest support matrix.

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/point_to_point/device/host/point_to_point_device_op.cpp:22 — the packet cap is the channel-buffer size (header included) and is used raw for non-bf16, so packed pages or page segments can exceed the max payload
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl/all_to_all_combine/device/all_to_all_combine_program_factory.cpp:198 — the writer's max packet size comes from the channel-buffer size for non-bf16; the dispatch sibling uses the max-payload query
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/moe/selective_reduce_combine/device/selective_reduce_combine_program_factory.cpp:57 — same channel-buffer-as-payload cap for non-bf16 tokens (again at line 389)
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/all_to_all_async/device/all_to_all_async_program_factory.cpp:261 — pages_per_packet = channel_buffer_size/page_size, so packet bytes can exceed the max payload by up to a header
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl/llama_reduce_scatter/device/llama_reduce_scatter_program_factory.cpp:311 — wrong constant for the packet cap (also line 441 and llama_reduce_scatter_create_heads:416); safe only if the tile-multiple rounding happens to land under the max payload
- tt_metal/fabric/hw/inc/edm_fabric/fabric_erisc_datamover_channels.hpp:65 — SlotAddresses::base() reads addresses[0] of std::array<size_t, NUM_BUFFERS> with no N==0 guard; channel_base_address() (line 270) is called for every remote receiver channel from fabric_erisc_router.cpp:2061
- tt_metal/impl/buffers/buffer.cpp:555 — a zero-size buffer is marked ALLOCATED with no address (0); any op without a zero-volume skip that writes a tile targets the bottom of L1

- models/demos/llama31_8b_qb2/tt/generator.py:138 — explicitly disables force-argmax on a multi-chip ring (same for models/demos/gemma4_31b_qb2/tt/generator.py:77), exposing greedy decode to the unexplained multi-gather corruption

- ttnn/ttnn/operations/core.py:269 — the golden bfp helper builds ttnn.Tensor from a possibly-subclassed torch tensor without as_subclass (also line 716), which can hit the module-string importer mismatch


## `tt-cb-balance`: CB push/pop counts diverge across paths

A circular/dataflow buffer is a single shared FIFO, and it only works if every page reserved/pushed by the one producer is waited/popped exactly once by the one consumer, on every core, for every compile-time variant and runtime shape. In this repo the balance breaks in a few recurring ways. (1) Producer and consumer take their counts from different expressions: a count set once for all cores next to a per-core clipped count; a batch multiplier (N, C, NC) layered on a shard extent that already includes the batch; input vs output padded volume; global batch count vs per-shard batch count; full capacity or row width Wt vs the tail-clamped per-pass length; tilize call count vs block tile count, which agree only when a blocking parameter is 1. (2) The push and the pop sit under different control structure: one side guarded by an if constexpr/#ifdef on an optional input (mask, running stats, gamma/beta, bias) or a mode flag (sharded/extract/aliased) while the other side is unconditional; a push hoisted out of a batch/block loop while the pop stays inside, or the reverse; an extra pop on a branch the producer never serves. (3) Protocol shortcuts: pop without a prior wait on the same thread (which throws off the 16-bit tile counters over long loops); constant or scratch buffers waited or reserved but never popped or pushed; two kernels waiting on one buffer that only one of them pops; helpers whose default wait/pop policy assumes a push_back that never comes because the buffer is filled by remote NoC writes; ping-pong intermediates or aliased block helpers (the output buffer is also an input) that leave a buffer non-empty at the end of an outer iteration. (4) Capacity: wait_front(N) where N exceeds the buffer depth under a reduced-L1 sizing branch, or in-place buffering of more tiles than the hard-coded depth. Most of these stay hidden until per-core work exceeds the buffer depth, or until an outer trip count (heads per core, batches, output blocks) goes above 1.

**Checks**

- Per buffer, per core: derive the producer's total reserve/push and the consumer's total wait/pop as expressions of the runtime and compile-time args the factory passes (loop trip counts multiplied by the per-iteration amount). Check they are equal for every shape the factory selector can route to that factory, including edge/last cores with remainder shards, input and output padded shapes that differ, batch in N vs C, and multi-tile-row tensors.
- Enumerate every compile-time variant (each optional input present or absent, fused vs non-fused, sharded vs interleaved, extract vs alias, mask type). For each one, list every wait/pop/reserve/push per buffer. Flag a push that is unconditional while the pop sits under a flag (or the reverse), an #else branch that still touches a buffer the host never creates, and a constexpr buffer alias that resolves to a buffer that is still full or was never pushed.
- Loop-level placement: a push hoisted out of batch/h/w/block loops ('data already resident' / shard-aliased) while the consumer pops inside them, or a bias/scalar pushed per batch while the consumer waits once. Simulate TWO iterations of every outer loop whose common-config trip count is 1 (heads per core, batches per core, output blocks, Q chunks). Any non-constant buffer still occupied at the end of iteration one is a hang.
- Multi-pass streaming kernels: every length passed inside the pass loop must be the tail-clamped per-pass value, not the capacity or Wt, in every #if branch. Ping-pong swaps must stay inside their own pair. The pop total must never exceed the cumulative wait total on clamped edge blocks.
- Every pop_front must be preceded, on the same thread, by a wait_front that covers those tiles. This includes self-produced partials, L1-accumulation rewinds, padding drains and tail pops. Check the 16-bit counter wrap over long loops.
- Grep each buffer id across the op's reader, compute and writer kernels. It must have exactly one popping consumer, and any other kernel that waits on it without popping is a bug unless the pages are duplicated or the kernels coordinate. A buffer pushed in a loop with no pop anywhere (private scratch) hangs once iterations exceed its depth.
- When code moves to a kernel-library helper with a wait/pop policy, check each input buffer's real producer. A buffer filled out of band (remote NoC writes, a globally allocated shard, gated by a semaphore or signal buffer) needs a NoWait policy plus an explicit pop. Also diff the helper's implicit reconfig/init against the code it replaced.
- Capacity: every wait_front(N)/reserve_back(N) must be <= the allocated depth on every host sizing branch, including reduced-L1 fallbacks (e.g. cb_length shrunk below Wt). The depth must also cover the tiles buffered in place before the first pop. Resident-vs-streaming decisions must use the SAME expression on the reader side and the compute side.
- Host counts shared by both sides must come from per-core/shard quantities, and every integer division in them must be exact (asserted) or unable to truncate to 0. Evaluate with a shard holding fewer than one, or a non-integer number of, batches.
- Treat a host factory that pins a block size to 1, or a TT_FATAL that forbids a feature combination with a hang TODO, as a marker of a latent device-side imbalance. Once the guard is lifted, test with block > 1 and per-core work greater than the buffer depth.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- ttnn/**/kernels/compute: `if constexpr (.*has_value|do_gamma|do_beta)` or `#ifdef FUSE_*` wrapping wait_front/pop_front, while the reader/writer push the same buffer id without the same guard
- ttnn/**/kernels/dataflow (matmul readers especially): reserve_back/push_back placed once before the main loop under `#ifdef IN*_SHARDED` / `if constexpr (extract_shard_sub_blocks)`, while compute pops per block inside batch x bh x bw x block loops
- program factories: `SetRuntimeArgs(program, id, all_cores, {num_units_per_shard})` or compile-time counts next to a per-core loop that clips shard dims on end_core; runtime args like `{"B", NC}` next to Ht derived from shard_spec.shape[0]
- compute kernels with `for (cur_pass ...)` / cur_*_length_t variables: grep `cb_length_t\|dfb_length_t\|Wt` passed to reduce/exp/pad/fused-mask calls inside the pass loop; `std::swap(cb_X, cb_prev_Y)` with mismatched names
- `pop_front(` with no preceding `wait_front(` on the same buffer in the same block: PACKER_L1_ACC rewinds, padding-tail drains (`pop_front(CAP - live)`), end-of-kernel pops of gamma/beta whose wait sits under a different #ifdef
- wait_front on scaler/eps/bias/mask/scaler_global buffers with no matching pop after the loop; reserve_back without push_back on scratch buffers used via get_write_ptr; push_back followed by get_write_ptr on the same buffer
- same CBIndex::c_N in wait_front in both a dataflow kernel and the compute kernel of one op (cur_pos, index, page-table, metadata), with pop_front in only one
- compute_kernel_lib::reduce / ckl::* calls with the default WaitAndPopPerTile policy on a buffer id that never appears in any push_back in the op's kernels directory (all-gather stats, globally allocated shards)
- host sizing: `cb_length = ((available_L1 / tile_size) * 0.95) / 7`-style shrink fallbacks, next to compute `wait_front(Wt)` on the same buffers
- `x_per_batch = <per-core tiles> / batch` where batch comes from physical_volume() / (H*W); `tilize<...>(in0_num_subblocks)` vs consumer wait_front(block_num_tiles)

**Where it clusters:** ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization (layernorm, layernorm_distributed, rmsnorm_distributed, groupnorm Welford, batch_norm running stats) — optional gamma/beta/stat buffers and resident-vs-streaming parameters; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul (1D mcast in0/in1 readers, bmm_large_block_zm compute, DRAM-sharded readers) — shard aliasing, hoisted pushes, L1-acc partials, multiple factory copies; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa_decode and tt-train SDPA — ping-pong running max/sum, multi-consumer metadata buffers, per-head loops; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement (sharded, bcast, untilize, untilize_with_unpadding, reshape) — per-core clipped counts vs uniform counts, global vs shard batch; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/softmax large-tensor kernel — multi-pass tail lengths; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/moreh and reduction/moe — self-loop readers that use buffers as scratch; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/** — stale forks of main kernels and factories that miss fixes; ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/ccl fused ops — buffers filled by remote NoC writes, consumed through helpers

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- Fixes land in one kernel or factory copy and miss its twins: legacy vs new-host-API factories, main vs Quasar forks, RMSNorm vs LayerNorm, Welford vs plain, sharded vs interleaved. After a fix, grep every file that shares the reader or the criterion and apply the same change.
- Fixes that correct one length or count call site inside a multi-pass loop leave the sibling call sites in other #if branches (fused mask, pad, numeric-stable) at full capacity. Softmax took three rounds. Fix every length argument in the loop together.
- Removing a host TT_FATAL or a pinned block size of 1 exposes latent device-side imbalances that the guard was hiding. Such a removal needs its own test at the boundary shapes.
- Rebalancing a ping-pong for the one-head-per-core model config regressed multi-head cores and was reverted. The re-land was one missing end-of-iteration pop. Always test outer trip counts > 1.
- Fixing a hang by making the push per-block can expose a second defect in the same mode (a forward-only aliased shard pointer reading the next row-block's data), and that follow-up fix went only into one factory path.
- Balance fixes that restate the producer's count formula inline in the consumer drift later. Move the formula into a shared constexpr helper with static_asserts. Where the reader and the compute decide residency with different predicates, they stay equal only by accident of host sizing (e.g. window shrunk 7x).

**Candidate sites** (unverified leads)

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/rmsnorm_distributed/device/kernels/compute/rmsnorm_post_allgather_metal2.cpp:149 — wait_front(Wt) on gamma/beta buffers sized cb_length, popped only after the row loop. The factory shrinks cb_length below Wt for wide inputs (layernorm_post_all_gather_program_factory.cpp:233-234), and the shared reader then re-streams per row, so the wait can exceed capacity.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/rmsnorm_distributed/device/kernels/compute/rmsnorm_post_allgather_metal2.cpp:193 — dfb_beta.pop_front(Wt) runs under FUSE_BETA alone, but the only beta wait_front (line 170) is nested inside FUSE_GAMMA. With beta and no gamma, the kernel pops without waiting and the beta stage is silently skipped.

- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/layernorm_distributed/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_unary_interleaved_ln_rm_gb_post_allgather.cpp:151 — the reader re-streams gamma/beta when `dfb_iterations != 1` (Wt/window), but the compute kernels (layernorm_post_allgather*.cpp:60-62) choose pop-per-block when `Wt != dfb_length`. For window < Wt < 2*window, gamma is pushed once and popped per row. Today only the host's 1/7 shrink prevents this.

It lacks the `(in0_num_blocks_y > 1 && in1_num_blocks_x > 1)` term that the new path has at :4556, so an aliased in0 shard advances onto the wrong row-block.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/quasar/matmul/device/factory/matmul_multicore_reuse_mcast_1d_program_factory.cpp:1243 — the Quasar legacy copy has the same K-only aliasing criterion (compare the sibling copies at :4082/:6547).
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul/device/kernels/dataflow/reader_bmm_tile_layout_in1_sender_writer_padding.cpp:227 — IN1_SHARDED does one reserve/push of in1_block_num_tiles*num_blocks_inner_dim outside the batch/bh/bw loops (per-block push is compiled out at :517), while compute pops in1 per block for each output block. It starves unless host validation forbids more than one output block.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm/device/kernels/compute/welford_groupnorm.cpp:208-214 — under UNTILIZE_OUT, gamma-only or beta-only untilizes from dfb_in (tilized input that is waited but never popped). This is the alias chain the sharded variant removed. It is latent only because groupnorm_device_operation.cpp:74-81 rejects ROW_MAJOR on the interleaved Welford path.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/data_movement/untilize_with_unpadding/device/factories/untilize_with_unpadding_multi_core_sharded_program_factory.cpp:90 — ntiles_per_batch = tiles / batch uses floor division. A HEIGHT_SHARDED shard holding a non-integer number of H*W slices makes the compute and writer counts diverge, and there is no divisibility validation. The Quasar twin at :77 has the same formula.

It hangs for out_subblock_h > 1 if it is ever wired up again (currently unreferenced).

Other layernorm kernels already fixed this unbalanced exit. It is benign per launch but flagged by buffer-state checkers.
- ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/layernorm/device/kernels/compute/layernorm_sharded_post_allgather.cpp:150 — dfb_scaler_global is waited on in the all-gather-worker sqrt path and never popped, while the same kernel pops eps. Unbalanced at exit.


## `loop-bounds`: Wrong extents, trip counts and boundary iterations

A loop bound is taken from the wrong extent. Typical cases: a whole shard or core span used inside a per-block loop, a maximum group span (block_w) used on a narrower last item, a width used where a count is meant, a south-edge formula copied into the west-edge loop, or a chunk count halved when the chunk does not divide the half. Loop counts derived arithmetically from shape parameters (x-1, x/2, x>>1, ratio N=chunk/subblock) reach 0 at the smallest legal shape, or a default ITERATIONS is 0. Hardware loop builders never validate these counts, so the result is a silent hang or wrong output. Boundary iterations get mishandled: the next entry is prefetched past the end of a table on the last step, an exact-fit ring split issues a 0-byte second chunk, a range check uses `<` against an exclusive end, a peeled prologue or epilogue assumes the loop runs at least once, and a lag-by-one pipeline is never flushed at every end-of-stream point. State is scoped to the wrong loop: an inner loop advances an index the outer loop never resets, an accumulator for a shared register is declared per item, a per-user copy sits after the loop so only the last iteration survives, or clipping/all-reduce runs per micro-batch instead of once per step. Chunked or unrolled copies write past `count`, and a destination sized roundup(max, chunk) overflows when it is filled at an offset or in two ring-wrap segments.

**Checks**

- For every hardware loop, MOP or replay count, and every software trip count computed from shape parameters (x-1, x/2, x>>1, chunk/subblock_h, default ITERATIONS), evaluate it at the smallest legal shape: 1-row face, 1 face, 1 tile, block of 1, odd counts. Flag values of 0 or values beyond the field width unless a runtime check (not a debug-only assert) or a static_assert excludes that shape. The template/MOP builders accept any count without validation.
- When a kernel iterates a sub-extent (block in shard, group in core span, head, window), check that every read AND write loop is bounded by that sub-extent or the real per-core extent, not the parent or maximum span. Look for asymmetric guards: one pass or direction guarded, another not (for example the final pass guarded but the stats pass not). After a new tiling parameter is introduced, list every loop, stride and buffer size that used the old extent, in every kernel copy (metal1/metal2, 1D/2D, reader/compute/writer).
- For each loop that advances a cursor and prefetches the next table entry or runtime arg at the end of a step, check the final iteration. The read must be guarded by cursor < length, or moved to the top of the next iteration. Compare the largest index the kernel reads with the vararg/runtime-arg count the host declares. Where a 2-D cursor is advanced by an offset, check that the carry is normalised (x >= num_x).
- At every ring or buffer split into first chunk plus remainder, test the exact-fit case (start+len == end). No chunk may be zero length, so the split needs a strict `>` or an explicit ptr==end case. For every [addr, addr+len) range check, confirm that an exclusive end is tested with `<=`/reject-on-`>`, and fix duplicated copies of the macro together.
- In nested loops, list every variable the inner loop mutates in place (ring index, offset, stage counter) and confirm the outer loop re-initialises it. Conversely, when a loop over N objects programs a resource shared by all of them, the accumulator must live outside the loop and be written once. Per-item outputs must be written inside the loop and indexed by the loop variable, not placed after the loop.
- For pipelined or peeled loops (prologue, drain, `for (i = 1; i < N; ...)`), evaluate trip counts 0 and 1. Every handshake, pop and drain the peeled code relies on must still happen. The host gate choosing that kernel must exclude those counts, or the kernel needs an explicit if-constexpr path for them. For lag-by-one producers, list every point where the stream is treated as drained (finish, event sync, trace end, teardown) and confirm each one flushes.
- When a bound divides a count (/2 for halves, /N for shards) and the chunk size is a free parameter, check that a chunk straddling the boundary is kept and partially masked, not dropped. Check that all cooperating kernels derive the bound from one shared helper, and that several masks hitting the same chunk compose (max/intersect) rather than first-match in an else-if chain.
- For chunked or unrolled copies that may touch up to chunk-1 elements past count, check that the destination covers dst_offset + roundup(count, chunk) for every segment of a multi-segment (wrap) copy. Flag guards that check only count, and size limits tied to buffers by a comment instead of a static_assert.
- When a helper written for one output per acquire is reused on a multi-output (DEST chunk) path, confirm that each per-output side operation (reload, wait/pop, reserve/push, init) runs N times or once with count N, targeting dst_base+i.
- For partition and slicer helpers, enumerate the degenerate regimes (fewer units than workers in one or both dimensions). Check that exactly num_workers slices come out, none is unintentionally zero, and the sum equals the total units. Check for copy-paste of min/max and count/width variable names in each branch.

**Seeds** (code shapes to hunt for)

- tt_metal/tt-llk/*/llk_lib: `ckernel_template tmp(` / `load_replay_buf(` / `ckernel_unpack_template` whose loop arguments match `(face_r_dim|num_faces|block_ct_dim|ct_dim|rt_dim|unit_dim)\s*(-\s*1|/\s*2|>>\s*1)`
- SFPU calculators (llk_api ckernel_sfpu_*): `grep -rnE 'int ITERATIONS\s*=\s*[0-7]\b'`, then compare the `dst_reg +=`/`dst_reg++` stride against the default
- dataflow kernels: `cur_core_idx++;` / `x++ ... y++` immediately followed by an unguarded `get_vararg(`/`get_arg_val(` inside `if (num_tiles_read_cur_core == num_tiles_per_core)`; also `x += n % num_x; y += n / num_x;` with no `if (x >= num_x)`
- dispatch/ring code: `if (addr + size >= end)` followed by `chunk = end - addr; write(chunk); addr = base; write(size - chunk)`; watcher/sanitizer checks of the form `addr + len < BASE + SIZE`
- ttnn dataflow kernels (groupnorm, layernorm, per-group ops): `for (nt = 0; nt < block_w; nt++)` with a `page_id` of `... + nt + index_g_offset` and no `(index_g_offset + nt) < per_core_N` guard
- compute kernels (sdpa, streaming pipelines): `for (uint32_t i = 1; i < N; ...)` or peeled `N - 1` phases where N is a host-chosen ratio; `num_chunks / 2` or `/= 2` used as a loop bound; else-if mask resolvers keyed on `k_chunk == *_mask_chunk_id`
- matmul/conv sharded readers: a loop bounded by `shard_height_in_tiles` / a per-core extent inside a per-block iteration, next to sibling kernels that compute `shard_height / num_blocks`
- cq_prefetch/cq_common: unrolled copies with `n += CHUNK` into arrays sized `((N + CHUNK - 1) / CHUNK) * CHUNK` and filled at an advancing `*_pos` after a wrap
- ccl host slicers (ccl_common.cpp): `x % num_workers` inside a branch guarded by `x < num_workers`; inner loops bounded by a `*_cols_per_worker` width where a `*_worker_count` is meant; `num_workers = <shape dim>` reassigned before a padding loop
- Python models/trainers: `.copy_(` into a caller buffer placed after `for i, x in enumerate(split)`; `clip_grad_norm`/`sync_gradients` inside a micro-batch loop and not under `should_step()`

**Where it clusters:** tt_metal/tt-llk/tt_llk_{wormhole_b0,blackhole}/llk_lib (MOP/replay loop counts from tile geometry); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/transformer/sdpa (chunk bounds, straddle and padding masks, streaming prologue/drain); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/experimental/transformer/nlp_*_heads_decode kernels (core-table cursor prefetch); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/normalization/groupnorm kernels (last-group span); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/ccl (host slicers, ring index handling, EDM layout sizes); tt_metal/impl/dispatch/kernels (ring wrap splits, chunked copies, lag-by-one profiler records); ttnn/cpp/ttnn/operations/matmul sharded readers + host validation; models/ generators and tt-train loops (per-user/per-step loop placement)

**How fixes for this class went incomplete**

- When the same pattern appears in several kernel copies (flat vs subcoregrid reader, sender vs receiver, metal1 vs metal2, tile vs row-major slicer, WH vs BH, the _runtime variant), fixes often covered only the copy that was reported. Grep for the sibling by file-name stem and fix all copies in one change.
- Workarounds that gate a configuration out on the host (for example excluding the trip-count-1 case) leave the kernel wrong, and the condition was often narrower than the real hazard. The lasting fix was an explicit kernel path keyed on the real overlap condition.
- Fixes applied to one path of a dual implementation (streaming vs non-streaming) left the other with the old composition logic: first-match masks instead of intersecting them.
- Drain/flush fixes were wired into the most common end-of-stream entry point (finish) but not the others (event sync), so the trailing unit is still lost on those paths.
- A register-programming fix corrected the accumulator scope but kept a read-modify-write that read a different instance than it wrote, and it also broke the build with a duplicate declaration. Masked in isolated tests because reset values matched. Re-check the whole RMW, and compile, after a scope change.
- Guards added as debug-only asserts (TT_ASSERT, watcher ASSERT) leave release builds unprotected. The template builders still validate no loop counts, so every newly derived formula stays unguarded.
